- [Route Deep Architectural Bugs to Codex, Not Claude Code](model-selection/codex-reads-more-context-before-editing-for-architectural-bugs.md): Route bugs that stem from the codebase's fundamental architecture to Codex rather than Claude Code, because Codex-family models reportedly spend longer (10-15 minutes) reading the codebase up front before editing, producing more accurate fixes for that class of problem, claude-code + codex.
- [Route Operationally-Mature Backend Work (Migrations, Concurrency, Offline Recovery) to Codex by Default](model-selection/codex-builds-more-operationally-mature-backend-by-default.md): In a head-to-head build, Codex's architecture included immutable revisions, offline recovery, migration safety, and concurrency handling that Claude Code's equivalent build did not attempt without being asked, claude-code + codex.
- [Route Security and Compliance Reviews to Codex, Not Claude Code](model-selection/route-security-reviews-to-codex-over-claude.md): When you need a security audit done exactly as scoped, open Codex rather than Claude Code: Codex stays inside the boundaries you set, where Claude Code tends to explore or get creative, claude-code + codex.
- [Route Tasks That Drive a Real Web UI to Codex, Claude Loses Them on the Mechanics](model-selection/route-browser-driving-work-to-codex.md): For work whose success depends on operating someone else's web interface (uploading files into a CMS, building something inside a web design tool), hand it to Codex, claude-code + codex.
- [Route Work Between Claude Code and Codex by Task Type, Not by Picking a Winner](model-selection/route-work-between-claude-code-and-codex-by-task-type.md): Send design/UI, brainstorming, planning, non-technical output, and hook/subagent-heavy automation to Claude Code, claude-code + codex.
- [Run Third-Party Agent Harnesses on ChatGPT Auth: OpenAI Sanctions It, Anthropic Doesn't](model-selection/route-third-party-harnesses-to-chatgpt-auth-not-your-claude-login.md): OpenAI publicly allows signing into third-party runners like OpenClaw and Hermes with a ChatGPT subscription, while Anthropic's Agent SDK docs state third-party developers may not offer Claude.ai login or rate limits without prior approval, claude-code + codex.
- [Size a Local Model at Half Its Parameter Count in GB: and Don't Quantize Below 4-Bit if It Has to Call Tools](model-selection/size-local-models-by-half-params-and-keep-a-quantization-floor-for-tool-use.md): RAM needed ≈ params ÷ 2, kept under half your machine's memory; and heavy quantization destroys tool-calling before it destroys chat quality, so test the floor on tool-call reliability, claude-code + codex.
- [Stop comparing models on dollars per million tokens: tokenizers differ by ~35%, so measure price per successful outcome](model-selection/stop-comparing-models-on-dollars-per-million-tokens-tokenizers.md): Identical text costs a different number of tokens on each vendor's tokenizer, so a lower published price per token can still produce a higher bill; the only comparison that holds is cost per completed task on your own workload, claude-code + codex.
- [Switch to a Different Provider When a Model Loops on the Same Bug](model-selection/switch-provider-not-retry-when-stuck-in-circles.md): When your primary model gets stuck going in circles or fails outright on a hard bug, switch to a different provider's tool entirely (e.g. from Opus in Claude Code to a GPT model in Codex CLI) rather than continuing to retry the same model, claude-code + codex.
- [Turn Reasoning Off on Local Coding Models: It's Faster and Scored the Same or Better](model-selection/turn-reasoning-off-on-local-coding-models.md): Small local models loop inside the chain of thought and charge you twice for it, generation time on your hardware plus KV-cache RAM, for no measured quality gain on coding tasks, claude-code + codex.
- [Send Exhaustive 'Check Every Combination' Goals to Codex, Not Claude, and Budget Roughly 3 to 5x the Time and Tokens](model-selection/route-exhaustive-goal-runs-to-codex-and-expect-3-to-5x-the-time.md): Mark says Claude 5.5 is less aggressive and comprehensive on a /goal, while Codex on the same goal takes three to five times longer with far more tokens.
- [When Claude Refuses to Copy a Credential or Click Log In for You, Hand That Step to Codex](model-selection/hand-login-and-credential-steps-to-codex-when-claude-refuses.md): Mark says Claude's computer use lags Codex's and Claude refuses to copy credentials or use an API key even with permission, while Codex usually does it.

### MCP

- [Test Your Own MCP Server by Having One Agent Drive the Other Agent's App With Computer Use](mcp/test-your-mcp-by-driving-the-other-agents-app-with-computer-use.md): Point Codex's computer use at Claude Desktop or Claude Code, have it install your MCP server, run a real multi-turn session against it, and report back, codex + claude-code.

### Prompting

- [Add a Dictation Custom Mode That Turns Brain Dumps Into Instructions](prompting/dictation-custom-mode-for-brain-dumps.md): Voice tools support per-mode instructions applied to every dictation, claude-code + codex.
- [Ask for Live Formulas in Generated Spreadsheets and Click Into Cells to Check Them](prompting/ask-for-live-spreadsheet-formulas-and-check-the-cells-not-the-look.md): A generated spreadsheet can look right while holding typed values instead of formulas, whether it came from a prompt or from a skill built by screen-recording the task. Ask for live formulas, click into cells to check them, and change one input to confirm the outputs recalculate before you trust it, claude-code + codex.
- [Ban Specific AI-Slop Patterns (Inter, Roboto, Generic Gradients, Teal) When Building a Design System](prompting/taste-skill-ai-tells-forbidden-pattern-list.md): The taste skill's 'AI tells' section is a concrete forbidden-pattern list, no Inter, no Roboto, no generic gradients, no teal, that you paste into whatever is generating your design system to strip out the telltale AI aesthetic before it ever appears, claude-code + claude-design.
- [Borrow software-engineering vocabulary as "leading words" to steer agent architecture](prompting/name-a-known-pattern-like-tracer-bullet-to-steer-agent-architecture.md): Naming a known software-engineering pattern in your prompt (e.g, claude-code + codex.
- [Give Claude Code the Goal, Give Codex the Steps: the Same Prompt Does Not Transfer](prompting/shape-the-prompt-to-the-harness-goal-for-claude-code-steps-for-codex.md): An identical open-ended /goal prompt made Claude Code exercise scope restraint and made Codex sprawl, so hand Codex explicit phases and a cut list and reserve outcome-only prompts for Claude Code, claude-code + codex.
- [Give the Agent the Target Model's Own Docs Before It Writes Prompts for That Model](prompting/feed-the-target-models-own-prompting-guide-before-writing-prompts-for-it.md): When Claude Code or Codex is building something that prompts a *different* model (a video, image, or third-party LLM API), point it at that provider's API docs and model card first - every provider publishes its own prompting rules, and the agent's priors are not those rules, claude-code + codex.
- [In a Codex /goal Run, a Question From Codex Does Not Stop the Run, While Claude Code Rarely Asks](prompting/codex-goal-runs-keep-going-when-codex-asks-a-question-you-do-not-answer.md): The creator reports that Codex raises clarifying questions in the middle of a /goal run but continues if you do not answer. In their six Opus runs on one /goal prompt, Claude Code asked a question only once, codex + claude-code.
- [Insert a Planning and Mapping Phase Between Research and Build in Long Autonomous Prompts](prompting/insert-a-planning-phase-between-research-and-build-in-autonomous-prompts.md): Whatever the harness, do not let a long unattended prompt go straight from research to build: write an explicit planning and mapping phase between them, and check the run's scope early rather than at the end, claude-code + codex.
- [Name Your Real API in the Claude Design Wizard to Get a 1:1 Handoff](prompting/name-the-real-api-in-the-claude-design-wizard.md): Claude Design front-loads a wizard before generating, claude-design + claude-code.
- [Prompt for Interaction States Explicitly: Generated UI Ships Flat](prompting/prompt-interaction-states-they-are-omitted-by-default.md): Hover, focus, and transition states are the cheapest polish win and the one thing generated sites reliably omit: buttons swap colour instantly, inputs have no focus ring, cards don't lift, claude-design + claude-code.
- [Specify a ~300ms Transition Duration for Hover and Focus State Changes](prompting/use-300ms-transition-for-hover-focus-states.md): Ask for roughly 300 milliseconds of transition time on hover/focus effects (color inversion, scale, underline reveal) instead of leaving them instant, since instant state changes read as unpolished, claude-code + claude-design.
- [Steer animation work by naming the technique and keep a cheat sheet you can paste to the agent](prompting/name-the-animation-technique-instead-of-asking-for-an-animation.md): A bare 'make animations' prompt gives weak results. Naming keyframes, springs, easing, SVG path, layout transitions, shaders or particles lets you direct and debug the work, claude-code + codex.
- [Write the Whole Procedure Out When the Harness Runs a Local Model](prompting/over-explain-the-procedure-when-the-harness-runs-a-local-model.md): Terseness is a frontier-model affordance; a local model given the same prompt does not error, it just returns the formulaic default, so supply the decomposition and any precomputable numbers yourself, claude-code + codex.
- [When Claude says a hacky integration is impossible, have it watch a working run of the target tool first](prompting/when-claude-says-a-connection-is-impossible-have-it-watch-a-real-run-before-deciding.md): Mark's pattern: open the other app, run the feature while Claude listens to the tools being called, and only then let it decide if the connection is possible; he also says to make it distinguish a missing tool in this chat from a capability that can be implemented.
- [Give a /goal a Rubric, a Finish Line, and a 'Stop and Tell Me Why After 2 Hours' Clause](prompting/give-goal-runs-a-finish-line-and-a-stop-after-clause.md): Mark's example: 'get the checkout tests passing', a finish line, and 'After 2 hours, if you're stuck, stop and tell me why', or stop and tell the other model.

### Automation

- [Don't Pipe Routine Agent Status Posts Into Team Slack: Everyone Goes Notification-Blind in Days](automation/dont-pipe-agent-status-posts-into-team-slack.md): The Amp team's shipped/error posts stopped being read within days; keep agent reporting pull-based and reserve push for events demanding human action, claude-code + codex.
- [End Every Scheduled Run With a Handoff That Names the Exact Next Job](automation/end-every-scheduled-run-with-a-handoff-naming-the-next-job.md): Each wake-up of a recurring agent is stateless, so make the last step of every run append what changed, the actual outcomes, remaining risk, unresolved issues and the exact next job, and make the first step read that entry, claude-code + codex.
- [Give the agent a video-enabled remote KVM to e2e-test what refuses to run in a VM](automation/give-the-agent-a-video-enabled-remote-kvm-to-e2e-test-what.md): When the feature under test needs real hardware or a weakened OS security posture, a video-enabled remote KVM gives the agent eyes and input on a physical machine instead of a VM, claude-code + codex.
- [Let an Agent Attach Images and Video to a PR by POSTing to `uploads.github.com`](automation/upload-pr-images-and-video-via-the-github-uploads-api.md): GitHub's drag-and-drop attachment CDN is reachable from curl with `gh auth token`, so a headless agent can post visual proof without a browser or committing assets to the repo, claude-code + codex.
- [Log Each Side-Effecting Action the Moment It Happens, Not at the End of the Run](automation/record-side-effecting-actions-as-they-happen.md): If a scheduled run can place an order, send an email or move money, write the record at the moment of the call, so an interrupted run can be recovered without repeating the action, claude-code + codex.
- [Power Internal Tools With the Codex/Claude SDK on Your Cached CLI Login Instead of a Metered API Key](automation/build-internal-tools-on-the-agent-sdk-with-your-cached-cli-login.md): Construct the Codex SDK (or Claude Agent SDK) client with no arguments so it picks up the login your CLI already cached, and every button in your self-built internal tool runs a headless worker on the subscription you already pay for - no API key, no second credit card, codex + claude-code.

### Remote Access

- [Share a Localhost Internal Tool Over a Mesh VPN Rather Than Deploying It](remote-access/share-a-local-internal-tool-over-a-mesh-vpn-instead-of-deploying-it.md): To let a couple of teammates use an internal tool you built to run locally, put them on a mesh VPN (Tailscale) and hand them the localhost URL, instead of deploying it, adding auth, and paying for hosting, codex + claude-code.
- [Run Claude Code or Codex on an always-on cloud computer by signing in once and cloning your repo](remote-access/install-and-authenticate-claude-code-on-an-always-on-cloud-computer.md): Nate runs Claude Code and Codex inside his Grok bot agents' cloud computer: ask the bot to install the CLI, sign in yourself, then clone your repo.

### Gotchas

- [A Claude Design Export Pushed Straight to Vercel 404s: the HTML Isn't at the Repo Root](gotchas/claude-design-export-404s-on-vercel-because-html-is-not-at-repo-root.md): Vercel serves a static repo from its root, but a Claude Design export nests the site under its own project folder, so the first deploy returns 404 Not Found, claude-design + claude-code.
- [Budget for ~5MB Page Weight When Embedding a Spline 3D Export](gotchas/spline-exports-are-heavy-budget-for-page-weight.md): Spline-exported 3D scenes typically add around 5MB to a page, which will slow load time; only add them if you're prepared to trade that off, claude-code + claude-design.
- [Claude Design Builds Desktop-Only: Ask for Mobile Explicitly Before Shipping](gotchas/claude-design-builds-desktop-only-check-mobile-before-shipping.md): Neither Claude Design nor Claude Code optimizes a generated site for mobile unless you ask, claude-design + claude-code.
- [Claude Design Won't Export MP4: Hand the Animation to Claude Code to Render](gotchas/claude-design-has-no-mp4-export-render-via-claude-code.md): Animation projects have no MP4 export. Screen-record for a quick clip, or use the hand-off-to-Claude-Code command and have Claude Code render the file, claude-design + claude-code.
- [Give Each Tool Its Own Worktree When Running the Same Prompt on Claude Code and Codex](gotchas/give-each-side-of-a-model-comparison-its-own-worktree.md): If you send one prompt to Claude Code and Codex in the same project folder, they can end up editing each other's files and the comparison is spoiled. Put each run in its own git worktree, claude-code + codex.
- [Hand Off to Claude Code From the Project Root: From an Open Page You Only Export That Page](gotchas/hand-off-to-claude-code-from-the-project-root-not-a-page.md): Claude Design's share → hand off to Claude Code scopes the export to wherever you are. Trigger it while a single page is open and you get that one page, silently losing the other four, claude-design + claude-code.
- [Keep API keys out of agent sessions with a local llm-keys-ui web form](gotchas/type-api-keys-into-a-local-form-not-the-agent-chat.md): Instead of pasting a secret into an agent chat (Codex Remote or otherwise) to get it onto a remote machine, have the agent launch a throwaway local web form (llm-keys-ui) and enter the key there, then let it read the key back with `llm keys get <name>`, claude-code + codex.
- [One Bad Field in `plugin.json` Kills the Whole Plugin - Not Even the Valid Skills Load](gotchas/one-bad-field-in-plugin-json-kills-the-whole-plugin.md): The manifest schema is closed at ten fields and any violation past an unknown top-level key is fatal: the client discovers none of the plugin's components, claude-code + codex.
- [Run computer-use agents inside a VM so they stop stealing your keyboard focus](gotchas/run-computer-use-agents-inside-a-vm-so-they-stop-stealing-your.md): A computer-use agent driving Chrome and native OS pickers fights you for foreground focus on the host machine, which a VM cleanly solves, claude-code + codex.

### Hooks

- [Replace an AGENTS.md Reminder With a Pre-Commit Check Whose Failure Message Tells the Agent What to Do](hooks/replace-an-agents-md-reminder-with-a-pre-commit-check-whose-failure-is-the-prompt.md): When an agent keeps bypassing a house library or pattern, add a git pre-commit check on the diff that fails the commit with a message naming the right module, rather than rewording the instructions file again, claude-code + codex.

## Claude Code

### Workflows

- [After an Agent Says 'Done,' Grep Every Usage of the Symbols It Touched](workflows/verify-touched-symbols-after-agent-says-done.md): On an established codebase, when Claude reports a task complete, search the repo for all other usages of the symbols it changed; if a callsite turns up that Claude never opened, the task isn't actually done.
- [After Each Session, Ask Claude to Update Your Knowledge Base](workflows/second-brain-after-session-knowledge-updates.md): At session end, prompt Claude to update project MD files with decisions made and lessons learned, this is compound engineering.
- [Agree Interfaces First, Then Split Implementation Into Parallel Waves, to Get Zero Merge Conflicts Across Sub-Agents](workflows/contract-first-parallel-waves-avoid-subagent-merge-conflicts.md): In a large autonomous build, structuring the work as contract-first (agreeing interfaces and schemas before implementation) and then dividing it into parallel waves of sub-agents produced zero merge conflicts.
- [Always finish a migration you start](workflows/finish-every-migration-you-start.md): Never leave a codebase half-migrated, partial migrations give the model contradictory signals and degrade its output. (Boris Cherny).
- [Ask /verify What It Needs From You Before Assuming End-to-End Verification Is Impossible](workflows/verify-skill-ask-what-it-needs.md): Instead of guessing why a change can't be verified end-to-end, ask the /verify skill directly what tools, credentials, or environment it would need to complete verification.
- [Audit the Agent's Transcript: Don't Trust the Success Claim](workflows/audit-agent-transcripts-dont-trust-the-success-claim.md): When an agent self-reports success (or a grader passes it), audit the actual transcript/artifacts to confirm it did the task instead of gaming the metric.
- [Background Long Codex Reviews With --background and Collect Them With /codex status and /codex result](workflows/background-codex-reviews-and-poll-with-status-and-result.md): Codex plugin reviews take 4-25 minutes on a real codebase. Append `--background` to get a job ID and keep working in Claude Code, then poll with `/codex status` and collect with `/codex result`, claude-code, drives codex.
- [Centre the Character in the Source Image So It Has Room to Turn Later](workflows/centre-the-hero-character-to-leave-room-for-motion.md): When generating a hero character that will later track the cursor or scroll, put it dead centre of the frame with clear space on both sides.
- [Chain Skills So Each One Hands the Next a Condensed Summary, Not Its Raw Work](workflows/chain-skills-with-condensed-handoff-summaries.md): When one skill's output feeds another (e.g. research to content to repurposing), have the upstream skill do its heavy lifting in its own context and return only a clean, condensed summary, so the downstream skill's context stays unpolluted by raw intermediate data.
- [Claude Code Snapshots a Checkpoint Before Every File Edit, With No Setup Required](workflows/checkpoints-created-automatically-before-every-edit.md): Before each file edit, Claude Code automatically saves a session-level checkpoint, so there is always a rewind point without you doing anything to enable it.
- [Click the Plus Button in the Web/Desktop Diff View to Send a Line-Referenced Comment](workflows/diff-view-plus-button-inline-line-comment.md): The web and desktop Claude Code diff view shows added/deleted lines, and clicking the plus button next to any line lets you type a suggestion or comment that gets sent as a new message tagged with that exact file and line reference.
- [Clone a Site's Feel Forensically Via Browser Control, Not Screenshots](workflows/forensic-site-clone-via-browser-dom-capture.md): Drive a real browser to capture per-scroll screenshots plus the DOM, scripts, and animation libraries; screenshots alone get you ~70% there and feel flat.
- [Closing a Worktree's Terminal Window Auto-Removes It, or Asks First if Work Would Be Lost](workflows/worktree-terminal-close-auto-cleans-or-asks.md): When you close the terminal window of a claude -w worktree session, Claude Code checks for changes: if nothing changed it deletes the worktree automatically, but if there's work to keep it asks you what to do before removing it.
- [Codify Two Verification Skills, One Rule-Based and One Taste-Based, Before Running Agents in Parallel](workflows/split-verification-into-rule-based-and-taste-based-skills.md): The blocker on going from one supervised agent to five is that you are the verifier; fix it by building two skills the agent runs on itself, a rule-based one for objectively checkable output and a taste-based one for judgement calls.
- [Combine an Archived Plan File With Git History to Cleanly Undo One Past Feature](workflows/use-archived-plans-plus-git-log-to-selectively-revert-one-feature.md): When you no longer want a feature you added several commits ago but want to keep everything shipped after it, find that feature's saved plan file, hand it to Claude Code with the git commit history, and ask it to undo just that feature.
- [Convert a Long Plan Into a Task List, Then Decide Subagent-Per-Task vs. Agent Team by Its Size](workflows/plan-into-task-list-then-choose-subagent-or-team-by-size.md): For a long spec-developer plan, ask Claude Code to turn it into an explicit task list first, then either have it complete each task inside a subagent, or spawn a full agent team when the list is long and complex enough to warrant it.
- [Default to HTML (Not Markdown) for Plans, Code Reviews, and Design Systems](workflows/html-as-default-artifact-not-markdown.md): Have Claude produce plans, reviews, reports, and design systems as interactive HTML artifacts, not Markdown, richer visuals drive better human review. (Thariq Shihipar).
- [Design Loops as the Unit of Work, Not Prompts](workflows/loops-as-abstraction-over-prompts.md): Stop thinking in prompts; design self-contained loops (spec → build → check → fix, with persisted memory and an exit condition), then compose outer loops that feed inner ones.
- [Design the Agent's Output to Be Verifiable Before You Build Evals](workflows/design-agent-output-for-verifiability-before-building-evals.md): Treat "hard to eval" as a design smell: make the artifact self-verifiable (a GIF/screen recording, before/after data diff, runnable demo) before writing evals, so correctness is confirmable at a glance.
- [Design Workflows With Human Decision Points, Not Full Automation](workflows/human-in-the-loop-checkpoint-design.md): Keep the human at the output checkpoint: Claude handles the repetitive grind (generating, finding, drafting), then stops for human review before anything ships.
- [Do Error Analysis and Split Failure Types Before Building an LLM Judge](workflows/error-analysis-before-llm-judge-no-blanket-score.md): When having Claude build evals for an AI feature, first sort real failures into distinct error types, then give each type its own evaluator (a code assertion where string or tool-call inspection can check it, an LLM judge only for the rest) instead of one blended score.
- [Don't Reuse CI/CD as Your Agent Sandbox - Pre-Bake an Image, and Isolate Tools From the Agent](workflows/pre-baked-machine-images-not-ci-for-agent-sandboxes.md): CI jobs are slow to start and short-lived while agents run for hours and mutate their own dependencies; bake the image on a cron so agent start is a `git pull`.
- [Don't Run Error Analysis One-Shot: Annotate Live and Let the Agent Learn Your Failure Taxonomy](workflows/annotate-live-so-the-agent-learns-your-failure-taxonomy.md): The failures no automated system caught were the ones that "looked correct" in the transcript; criteria drift means you don't know your criteria until you've read the traces.
- [Drive HyperFrames From Claude Code to Render HTML-Based Motion Graphics Videos](workflows/hyperframes-claude-code-video-rendering-pipeline.md): HyperFrames is an open-source, agent-drivable video framework: Claude Code writes HTML/CSS motion graphics, HyperFrames renders it through a headless browser into frames, then FFmpeg assembles the frames into an MP4.
- [Drive Phased-Build Frameworks One Phase Per Invocation or They Burn Your Whole Context Window](workflows/scope-phased-build-frameworks-to-one-phase-per-invocation.md): Multi-phase build skills/commands (e.g. Google Cloud's agent-skills library) will, if you just say 'build,' try to execute the entire phased plan in a single session and exhaust your context; explicitly scope each run to one phase (`build phase two`) and clear context between phases.
- [Edit the Plan Directly with `Ctrl+G` Before Approving It](workflows/edit-plan-in-editor-ctrl-g.md): In plan mode, press `Ctrl+G` to open the generated plan in your text editor and edit it in place before Claude executes, faster and more precise than negotiating changes turn-by-turn in chat.
- [End a Long Workflow With "Make an Artifact and Email It to Me" So You Can Walk Away](workflows/end-a-long-workflow-with-artifact-and-email-it-to-me.md): Naming the output format and the delivery channel in the same prompt is what makes closing the laptop safe.
- [Expect to Re-Run a Bulk-Fix Prompt Several Times, One Pass Never Closes Hundreds of Items](workflows/re-run-a-bulk-fix-prompt-until-the-score-stops-moving.md): A prompt that asks for hundreds of fixes lands maybe 400 of 500 on the first pass, and fixing one item frequently breaks another.
- [Export a Remixed Spline 3D Scene as Code and Hand It to Claude Code](workflows/export-spline-scene-as-code-into-claude-code.md): Spline's community library lets you remix an existing 3D scene, restyle its colors to match your brand palette, then use Export > Code > Copy to get a snippet you paste straight into a Claude Code prompt to embed it.
- [Fall Back to the Gemini CLI When a Site Blocks Claude's Fetching](workflows/gemini-cli-fallback-for-blocked-research-sites.md): In automated research workflows, route pages Claude cannot fetch through the Gemini CLI as a fallback, so blocked sites do not leave holes in the research.
- [Filter What You Record With the Shingo Test: Stable, Transparent, Manually Perfected](workflows/filter-recordable-processes-with-the-shingo-stable-transparent-perfected-test.md): Before recording a Cowork skill, require the process to be stable, transparent (you can say every decision out loud), and already perfected by hand; automating an unperfected process just makes bad work happen faster.
- [Force Explore-and-Read Before Implementing (Opus Under-Explores by Default)](workflows/force-explore-and-read-before-implementing.md): Add a rule/command that lists the dir, finds and reads related files, summarizes patterns, then codes, and never speculates a file's contents instead of reading it.
- [Free Generation Doesn't Weaken YAGNI: It Makes Speculative Code Cheaper to Commit, Which Is Worse](workflows/yagni-cost-persists-when-agents-generate-code.md): When an agent generates speculative structure instantly, reject the "for later" caching/abstraction/config it adds; typing cost is zero but optionality and timing costs remain. (Kent Beck).
- [Gate Every Feature on a Constitution File Rather Than Trusting Skills to Hold the Line](workflows/gate-every-feature-on-a-constitution-file.md): Skills alone are not a build system; run them inside a spec-driven process where a constitution.md of project principles has to validate before planning proceeds, and the process refuses to continue when a feature violates it.
- [Generate an Editable Whole-App Architecture Diagram to Orient Yourself and Point Claude at the Right Layer](workflows/generate-editable-architecture-diagram-to-orient-and-target-the-model.md): Use a draw.io/diagram skill to render an editable architecture diagram of your whole app (presentation → state → service → data → edge layers) so you build a real mental model and can point Claude at the exact layer a problem lives in instead of making it explore blindly.
- [Generate Both Ends of a Character's Turn as Stills Before Asking for the Video](workflows/generate-both-extremes-of-a-character-turn-as-stills.md): For a hero character that tracks the cursor, generate three stills (head 70 degrees left, centre, 70 degrees right) from the same original, then ask for a 5 second clip travelling from still one to still three.
- [Generate character sheets and storyboards before AI video, and QA-reject takes before delivery](workflows/generate-character-sheets-and-storyboards-before-ai-video.md): Reusable character images, then per-scene boards, then video from the boards, with a screenshot QA pass that rejects off-brand takes.
- [Generate Interactive HTML Artifacts for Design Variations and Data Exploration](workflows/interactive-html-artifacts-for-design-decisions.md): Ask Claude to make 10 design variations in one HTML file; use Bun for a hot-reloading interactive artifact with comment export.
- [Get a Cost Breakdown by Telling the Agent to Discover AgentsView Itself](workflows/have-the-agent-drive-agentsview-for-an-in-session-cost-breakdown.md): Point it at `uvx agentsview --help` and let it find the subcommand, rather than needing to know the CLI surface first.
- [Give /goal a Verifiable Finish Line and a Safety Cap](workflows/goal-verifiable-finish-lines-with-safety-caps.md): /goal needs a measurable, observable completion condition plus a turn/time cap, or the boss/worker loop never converges and burns tokens.
- [Give Visual Work a Self-Validation Loop (Screenshot + Grade + Iterate)](workflows/visual-self-validation-screenshot-grading.md): Have Claude drive the running app via `/chrome`, screenshot it, grade the result against explicit criteria, and iterate until it passes, backed by real screenshot/E2E tests and observable runtime state.
- [Have Claude check and fix a site at phone, tablet and desktop widths with the Playwright plugin](workflows/have-claude-verify-a-responsive-site-with-the-playwright-plugin.md): Install the Playwright plugin from /plugin, then ask Claude to check the site at each size and fix breakage, such as a hamburger menu on phone.
- [Have Claude maintain a tracker sheet of every generation batch (angle, model, cost), then feed performance data back in](workflows/log-generation-batches-in-a-tracker-sheet-and-feed-performance-back.md): Log each batch's angle, models, and cost in a sheet, then write real performance numbers back in so the next batch is steered by what worked.
- [Have Claude Score Candidate Video Takes Frame by Frame Instead of Judging Smoothness by Eye](workflows/score-video-takes-frame-by-frame-instead-of-eyeballing-smoothness.md): Generate several takes of a motion clip, then have Claude write a throwaway script that measures how much changes between consecutive frames and ranks them.
- [Have the Agent Quiz You on Its Own Diff Instead of Reading Every Line](workflows/have-the-agent-quiz-you-on-its-own-diff-instead-of-reading.md): Review agent-written code by making the agent examine you on it (quiz, diagram, presentation) rather than by scanning the diff line by line.
- [Implement a Heartbeat and Wrap-Up Pattern for Self-Maintaining Systems](workflows/agentic-os-heartbeat-and-wrap-up-pattern.md): At session start, a heartbeat scans skills vs CLAUDE.md and auto-registers new additions. At session end, a wrap-up skill collects feedback and commits learnings.
- [Insert a UX-Spec Stage Between the PRD and the Build](workflows/ux-spec-stage-between-prd-and-build.md): Add a UX-spec step between PRD and build (mental model → IA → affordances → every UI state) so the model doesn't decide them at game time and ship a generic UI.
- [Invest More in Behavioral End-to-End Tests: They're What Let the Agent Cook](workflows/invest-in-behavioral-e2e-tests-they-let-the-agent-cook.md): Tests are no longer just a safety net; behavior-level E2E tests (what the product does, not how) are what free an agent to refactor and iterate safely, so spend more bandwidth there.
- [Justify Refactoring by Measuring the Token Cost of the Same Prompt Before and After](workflows/measure-refactoring-payoff-in-tokens-with-a-fresh-subagent.md): Agents do not learn between runs, so a fresh subagent running an identical prompt makes the payoff directly measurable: 159,564 to 27,360 input tokens, an 83% drop.
- [Keep a Separate To-Do List for Claude Code, Distinct From Your Own](workflows/separate-todo-list-for-claude-code-vs-your-own.md): Maintain your personal to-do list (e.g. in Todoist) and a second list specifically for items you want Claude Code to execute itself, and tell it explicitly which items belong to which list.
- [Keep Specs Fuzzy and Let End-Stage Code Review Catch Edge Cases](workflows/keep-specs-fuzzy-let-code-review-catch-edge-cases.md): Over-specifying every edge case turns a spec into code-in-English and forces stronger models into worse deviations on contact with the real codebase; give only the goal plus hard 'don'ts', then run 2-3 rounds of end-stage /code-review to surface edge cases instead of pre-planning them.
- [Let a Claude Code Project Split Your Running Thoughts Into Threads Instead of Managing Sessions Yourself](workflows/let-claude-code-projects-split-thoughts-into-threads.md): Projects (beta, desktop and web, Pro and Max only) turn one conversation into an orchestrator: send tasks as they come and it splits them into threads that run as parallel cloud sessions sharing the project's repos, files, instructions and memory, with an Overview pane listing what is "Waiting on you".
- [Log Into the Vendor CLI Yourself, Then Let the Agent Do the Dashboard Work](workflows/authenticate-vendor-clis-so-the-agent-owns-the-deploy-plumbing.md): Run `vercel login` (or the equivalent) in the agent's terminal and it will create the project, load every environment variable, mark secrets as sensitive, run the two-step redeploy, and smoke-test the result - instead of you pasting keys into a web dashboard one at a time.
- [Make Autonomous-Loop Acceptance Criteria Exercise the Rendered UI](workflows/autonomous-ui-loop-acceptance-must-exercise-rendered-ui.md): In UI-building loops, require browser/UI testing and a commit per task, a loop trusts code-presence over behavior and will mark broken UI done.
- [Make Claude Draw the Current Process as a Mermaid Diagram, Then Challenge Every Step Before Changing Anything](workflows/diagram-the-current-process-before-proposing-improvements.md): Before asking for improvements to any existing system or process, have Claude map how it works today end to end as a mermaid flowchart, then walk each node and confirm it matches reality.
- [Make walkthrough docs quote code by executing cat/grep/sed, never by retyping snippets](workflows/make-walkthrough-docs-quote-code-by-executing-cat-grep-sed.md): When asking an agent to write a walkthrough, explainer, or review doc that contains code, instruct it to insert the snippets by running shell commands whose output is captured, so the quoted code is mechanically guaranteed to match the file.
- [Master /resume Keyboard Shortcuts for Fast Session Navigation](workflows/session-resume-keyboard-shortcuts.md): In /resume: P previews, B shows branches from compaction, R renames, A shows all projects, Ctrl+W shows all worktrees.
- [Measure agent over-engineering with net-new LOC and cyclomatic complexity](workflows/measure-agent-over-engineering-with-net-new-loc-and-complexity.md): When building an eval or audit for a coding agent's over-engineering tendency, start with two cheap objective metrics: net new lines of code added and cyclomatic complexity, the same starting point Anthropic's own Claude Code team used.
- [Model an Agent Task Board as 'Your Turn' / 'Agent's Turn,' Not a Linear Kanban](workflows/model-agent-dashboards-as-two-iterative-queues.md): When designing a Kanban-style board for managing agent work, use two facing queues (your turn to review, the agent's turn to act) instead of a one-way not-started/in-progress/done pipeline.
- [Name the Trigger Conditions for Human Review Instead of Reviewing Every Agent Diff](workflows/reserve-human-review-for-high-blast-radius-agent-changes.md): an unwritten policy degrades into skimming everything equally badly.
- [Name Your Sessions With `--name` So They Can Message Each Other](workflows/name-your-sessions-so-they-can-message-each-other.md): `claude --name backend` makes sessions addressable, so a cross-session fact costs one sentence instead of a re-paste, lighter than agent teams for two long-lived sessions.
- [Orient on a Large Agent-Written PR by Reading Its Documentation Edits First](workflows/review-agent-prs-by-reading-documentation-edits-first.md): When reviewing a big agent-written diff, read the doc/changelog edits before the code, they're the agent's own summary of intent and give you a map to check the implementation against.
- [Pair /goal With /loop (or Routines) for Autonomous Recurring Tasks](workflows/goal-and-loop-pairing-for-autonomous-recurring-tasks.md): /loop sets the cadence, /goal sets the completion condition; together a task runs on schedule and keeps working each run until done.
- [Pair an Over-Engineering Audit Skill With a Grill-Me Skill During the Pruning Phase](workflows/pair-an-over-engineering-audit-skill-with-grill-me.md): During context pruning after exploration, run a skill that audits the generated ideas for over-engineering alongside a 'grill me' style skill that interrogates you on real intent, then discard anything that doesn't need solving now.
- [Paste an Unfamiliar Tool's Repo Link Into Claude Code and Ask It to Fan Out Subagents Before Planning the Install](workflows/feed-unfamiliar-repo-link-for-fan-out-install-plan.md): To set up an unfamiliar open-source tool or repo, give Claude Code the raw GitHub link and explicitly ask it to inspect/audit it and fan out subagents to research the repo before producing an implementation plan tailored to your specific machine and environment.
- [Pay down cognitive debt with an animated HTML explainer of the algorithm](workflows/pay-down-cognitive-debt-with-an-animated-html-explainer-of-the.md): When a prose walkthrough still leaves an algorithm opaque, have the agent build a single-page animated visualisation of it, with pause, speed and frame-stepping controls.
- [Persist an Interactive Artifact's State in the URL Fragment So a Link Reproduces the Exact View](workflows/persist-artifact-state-in-the-url-fragment-so-a-link-reproduces-it.md): Ask for every control on a generated artifact (step, speed, filters, per-item decisions) to serialise into the #fragment, so you can send or paste back the exact view instead of describing it.
- [Pipe SAST Findings Into a Fix Command, Then Re-Scan to Verify](workflows/sast-scan-to-fix-command-loop.md): Scan AI code with Semgrep (OWASP Top 10), export findings to a fix command, then re-scan to zero, catches missing authz, hardcoded secrets, CORS *, debug mode.
- [Plan the Full Workflow Step-by-Step Before Starting the Claude Code Session](workflows/step-by-step-workflow-planning-before-session.md): Sketch the entire task in plain text or a browser chat first, then move to Claude Code only once you know what you're building and how to decompose it.
- [Point Claude at Lean (or TLA+) to formally verify a tricky state machine, not just review it](workflows/formally-verify-race-prone-code-with-lean-or-tla.md): For race-prone or state-machine-heavy code, ask Claude to formally model it in Lean (or TLA+) and hunt for counter-examples instead of doing a plain code review; Cherny got 16 bug/race-condition fixes on the Claude Agent SDK from a couple of prompts.
- [Prefer Well-Known Packages (e.g. tRPC, Next.js) Over Niche Ones for More Reliable First-Try Agent Output](workflows/prefer-well-known-packages-for-more-reliable-agent-output.md): Choosing widely-used libraries over niche ones increases the odds Claude Code implements them correctly on the first try, because there is far more training data for them.
- [Rank Large Candidate Sets With a Pairwise Tournament Workflow, Not Cold Absolute Scoring](workflows/tournament-pairwise-workflow-for-ranking-large-sets.md): For ranking many items (resumes, titles, product names), run a dynamic workflow of pairwise head-to-head comparisons in fresh-context agents with a deterministic loop holding the bracket - each round can carry its own rubric - instead of scoring each item cold in one session.
- [Read the agent-written PR description before you make anyone else read it](workflows/read-the-agent-written-pr-description-before-you-make-anyone.md): The first review pass on agent output, code *and* the generated PR description, is the author's job, not the reviewer's; ship evidence you did it.
- [Relay Claude's Markdown Output Through a Notion Page When a Destination App Mangles It](workflows/relay-through-notion-to-preserve-formatting-on-paste.md): For platforms other than the one you can prompt for natively, paste Claude's markdown output into a fresh Notion page first, then copy out of Notion into the target app; Notion preserves the markdown and converts it on copy-out.
- [Reserve Autonomous Loops for Disposable or Mechanically-Verifiable Work](workflows/match-autonomous-loops-to-disposable-or-verifiable-work.md): Point Ralph-style autonomous loops at throwaway or objectively-checkable tasks (porting, perf exploration, security scanning, research), not code you must understand and maintain.
- [Resume the Exact Session Behind a PR with `claude --from-pr`](workflows/resume-sessions-from-pr.md): Creating a PR via `gh pr create` auto-links the session to it; reopen that full context later with `claude --from-pr <number-or-url>` (or paste the PR URL into `/resume`) instead of starting cold on review feedback.
- [Route 'Plausible' Code-Review Verdicts to a Slack Message Instead of Auto-Fixing Them](workflows/plausible-verdict-routes-to-slack-not-autofix.md): When /code-review returns a 'plausible failure' verdict (as opposed to medium/high severity), don't auto-fix it: have the agent message you on Slack explaining the finding and why it matters.
- [Route planning to /grill-me when it fits one session and /wayfinder when it does not](workflows/route-planning-to-grill-me-when-it-fits-one-session-and.md): Pick the self-interview skill for work whose path you can already see, and the orchestrator skill for work where the end state is still unknown, using "does this fit in one planning session?" as the test.
- [Run /insights for a 30-Day Usage Report Card, Then Pipe Its Recommendations Into Rules and Automation](workflows/run-insights-for-a-usage-report-card-and-pipe-into-automation.md): /insights reads your last 30 days of session JSONL and generates an HTML report card - what you work on, where you fail (wrong-diagnosis loops, over-broad actions), quick wins, hook suggestions, and an 'on the horizon' section with ready-to-paste meta-prompts - which you then feed straight back into .claude/rules and a scheduled skill to compound self-improvement.
- [Run a New Loop in Training Mode (Pause-and-Approve Each Step) Before Unleashing It](workflows/run-new-loops-in-training-mode-first.md): Gate a new loop behind a training_mode flag that pauses for approval before each action, validate over a couple runs, then flip it off, so a bad condition doesn't burn tokens or fire side effects across a whole batch.
- [Run DeepSec as an Agentic Scan -> Process -> Report -> Fix -> Revalidate Security Loop](workflows/deepsec-agentic-scan-process-report-revalidate-security-loop.md): Vercel's DeepSec is an agent-powered vulnerability scanner whose npx pipeline (init -> scan regex-triages candidate files in <1s -> process runs a batched LLM deep-dive -> report groups findings by severity -> revalidate reads git history to confirm each fix) catches subtle AI-app holes a Semgrep pass or casual review misses.
- [Run Each Phase of a Plan in Its Own Session and Chain Them as GitHub Stacked PRs](workflows/one-session-per-plan-phase-chained-as-stacked-prs.md): Keeps each phase's context clean and each diff reviewable, and puts the dependency order into GitHub; paste the stacked-PR docs URL, it postdates training data.
- [Run Multiple Parallel Claude Sessions With System Notification Hooks](workflows/parallel-sessions-with-system-notifications.md): Open 5+ terminal tabs each running a separate task; use a Stop hook to notify you when each finishes so you can check in without babysitting.
- [Run the wait test: parallelize any workflow step that doesn't need its predecessor's output](workflows/wait-test-flags-workflow-steps-that-dont-need-their-predecessor.md): Ask of each step "does this need the previous step's result?"; every no is a false dependency to fan out into a diamond shape.
- [Run Your Traces Through Your Own Coding Agent Before Buying an Automated Eval Platform](workflows/run-traces-through-your-coding-agent-before-buying-an-eval-platform.md): On 100 masked production traces the coding agents scored within a few points of the dedicated platforms; what you're actually buying is the annotation surface and re-run loop, not finding quality.
- [Save Checkpoints Frequently and Reset (Not Rescue) When Sessions Drift](workflows/save-checkpoint-reset-on-drift.md): Save state after each major step, let Claude run, then either accept or start completely fresh, don't try to correct a drifting session mid-flight.
- [Send End-to-End Verification Recordings via a Slack MCP Server, Not Just an Attached File](workflows/send-verification-recording-via-slack-mcp.md): After a sub-agent finishes Playwright/Chrome-based end-to-end verification, have it push the recording straight to you on Slack through a Slack MCP server rather than only leaving it as a PR attachment.
- [Settle architecture choices by having the agent build the simulation and load test](workflows/settle-architecture-choices-by-having-the-agent-build-the.md): Instead of arguing a technology choice on paper, have agents build throwaway simulations of each candidate and load-test them in parallel, then pick on measurements.
- [Shift Verification to the Cheapest Rung and Let Claude Run the Eval](workflows/shift-verification-to-the-cheapest-rung.md): Catch issues with the cheapest deterministic check that can catch them (lint/type/test) rather than expensive human review, and when a metric exists, hand Claude the eval so it can optimize against it itself.
- [Split Long Work Across Fresh, Single-Purpose Sessions With File Handoffs](workflows/split-long-work-across-fresh-single-purpose-sessions.md): Don't /compact at the degraded end; summarize while the model is fresh, /clear, and chain single-purpose sessions (discovery → plan → execute) that each read the previous one's output file.
- [Split Work Into Engineer-Mode and Pirate-Mode by Blast Radius](workflows/engineer-vs-pirate-mindset.md): Give business-critical paths (auth, payments, data) slow reviewed treatment and low-risk work fast screenshot-and-iterate; equal rigor on both wastes review on safe work and under-tests dangerous work.
- [Steer the Agent Toward a Vertical Slice and Root Cause, Away From Brute-Force Fixes](workflows/steer-agent-to-vertical-slice-and-root-cause.md): Watch for three failure patterns mid-task, premature broad implementation, brute-force symptom fixes, and confident misdiagnosis, and redirect Claude toward one validated slice and the underlying cause.
- [Store Plans Inside Project Folders, Not Throwaway Directories](workflows/plan-file-persistence-in-project-folders.md): Write /plan output to a file in the repo so Claude re-reads it across context compactions instead of losing the nuance.
- [Structure Agentic Systems as Workflows, Agents, and Tools (Separate Reasoning From Execution)](workflows/wat-framework-separate-workflows-agents-tools.md): Split agentic systems into three layers: markdown workflow SOPs, an agent that coordinates, and deterministic code tools, keep probabilistic reasoning out of execution so failures are debuggable and self-healing.
- [Synthesize a Lean Bespoke Tool From Several Reference Repos Instead of Adopting and Wrangling One Boilerplate](workflows/synthesize-lean-bespoke-tool-from-reference-repos-not-a-boilerplate.md): Rather than spend hours bending a popular but bloated framework (e.g. OpenClaw and its forks) to your needs, clone several competing repos, have Claude Code catalog and compare their features into a pick-list, select only what you want, and have it build a minimal from-scratch version you fully understand and can audit.
- [Track Each Setup Layer's Decay Rate in a rot.md File to Set Its Own Audit Cadence](workflows/track-per-layer-decay-cadence-in-a-rot-md-file.md): Different layers of a personal Claude Code or agent setup go stale at very different speeds.
- [Trade Local Render Hours for a Cloud Render, Then Buy the Quality Back With a Local Upscaler](workflows/render-a-long-clip-in-the-cloud-and-upscale-the-cheap-render-locally.md): A hero clip that costs 4.5 to 9 hours on a high-spec laptop (and about two days on a Mac Mini) renders for roughly two dollars in the cloud, and a free local upscaler recovers most of the quality you traded away by rendering low.
- [Transcribe a Video First So an Agent Can Time Animations to What Is Said](workflows/transcribe-the-video-first-so-motion-graphics-land-on-the-spoken-words.md): For agent-driven video editing, make transcription the first step. It lets the agent land graphics at the right moments and also find mistakes and dead space to cut.
- [Trust Agent Diffs More on a Type-Safe Stack](workflows/trust-type-safe-stacks-more-in-diff-review.md): When reviewing an agent's diff by its shape rather than line-by-line, trust the result more on a type-safe stack (e.g. tRPC, Prisma) because a type error will surface a shape mismatch automatically.
- [Turn on the base guardrail stack before reaching for /loop, /batch and dynamic workflows](workflows/turn-on-the-base-guardrail-stack-before-reaching-for-loop.md): Claude Code's head of product splits the feature set into a base tier (self-verification, auto mode, default-on code review and security review, a multi-agent view) that comes first and an advanced tier (/loop, /batch, dynamic workflows, subagent worktree isolation) that only pays off once the base tier is in place.
- [Turn Your Throwaway CLIs Into Native GUI Apps: Agents Made Real UIs Nearly Free](workflows/wrap-throwaway-clis-in-native-guis-now-agents-make-them-cheap.md): Stop defaulting personal tools to TUIs; point the agent at an existing CLI and have it wrap a real native UI (menu-bar apps that survive months of daily use).
- [Use "Boring, Predictable, Accurate" as the Test for What an Agent Can Own End to End](workflows/outsource-writing-only-when-boring-predictable-and-accurate.md): It is a property of the genre, not the model: release notes and changelogs qualify, anything carrying voice or judgment does not.
- [Use /chrome to Give Claude Live Browser Access for End-to-End Testing](workflows/chrome-extension-for-browser-testing.md): /chrome connects Claude to your Chrome browser, it sees console logs, takes screenshots, and can navigate pages for real UI verification.
- [Use /copy to Copy Claude's Output With Full Formatting Preserved](workflows/copy-command-for-formatted-output.md): /copy puts the last response in your clipboard with markdown formatting intact, no manual selection needed.
- [Use `claude agents` (Agent View) Instead of Juggling Terminals](workflows/agent-view-native-orchestration-ui.md): `claude agents` opens a native multi-session dashboard (persists across terminal restarts, git-worktree-backed), the built-in answer to managing 5+ parallel sessions, replacing tmux/window juggling and the older build-your-own-Kanban command centers.
- [Use a Multi-Agent Verify Step to Eliminate False Positive Bug Reports](workflows/ultrareview-multi-agent-verification-pattern.md): Spin up 5 bug-hunting subagents from different starting points, then run an independent verifier to confirm real bugs before acting.
- [Use a Parity Agent to Port Features Across Symmetric Codebases Like iOS/Android](workflows/parity-agent-ports-features-across-symmetric-codebases.md): When two codebases share the same architecture, e.g. an iOS app and its Android counterpart, spawn one agent-team teammate to read the reference platform's code and generate a task list, then have an executing teammate implement those tasks on the target platform.
- [Use Dynamic Workflows (ultracode) for High-Value, Failure-Prone Work: With a Token Budget](workflows/use-dynamic-workflows-for-high-value-complex-work.md): Ask Claude to 'create a workflow' (or use the 'ultracode' keyword) to have it author its own multi-agent harness for complex work; gate with 'does this really need more compute?' and cap spend with a token budget. Width = workflows; depth = /goal.
- [Use OpenSpec So Every Change Reconciles Into a Living Spec That Flags Broken Requirements](workflows/openspec-living-spec-contract-breaks-surface-on-archive.md): OpenSpec is a spec-driven framework where each change lives in changes/ and its archive/sync step merges finished work back into a master openspec/specs/ directory - and if the change violates a requirement in an existing feature's spec, archiving surfaces the conflict and forces you to reconcile it, killing the stale-documentation drift that plagues Spec Kit/BMAD.
- [Use Plan Mode Before Every Non-Trivial Change](workflows/plan-mode-before-every-nontrivial-change.md): Enter plan mode for any change >10-15 lines; the AI drafts a detailed plan you review before a single line is written.
- [Use the /workflows JS Tool for Deterministic Multi-Agent Orchestration](workflows/workflows-js-deterministic-orchestration.md): Define a JavaScript workflow in `.claude/workflows/` to orchestrate sub-agents in code, results pass directly between agents without re-entering the main context, avoiding the orchestrator token tax. (Beta, env-var gated.).
- [Use the Claude Desktop App's Preview Pane for Quick Localhost Checks, VS Code for Full Project Visibility](workflows/desktop-app-preview-vs-vscode-for-video-projects.md): When doing agent-driven video editing, the Claude desktop app can preview a running localhost render inline during the conversation, while VS Code lets you see every project file, asset, and past render side by side.
- [Use Worktrees for Parallel Independent Tasks That Cannot Share Files](workflows/worktrees-for-parallel-independent-tasks.md): `claude -w <name>` creates an isolated worktree branch; multiple can run simultaneously without file conflicts.
- [Verify Scroll Animations by Screenshotting Frame by Frame Down the Page, a Single Screenshot Proves Nothing](workflows/screenshot-a-scroll-animation-frame-by-frame-not-once.md): One screenshot of an animated page is one arbitrary moment of it.
- [War-Game the Plan: Have Your Strongest Model Pre-Simulate Failure Paths for a Cheaper Executor](workflows/war-game-plans-with-strongest-model-for-cheaper-executor.md): Instead of a linear plan, ask your most capable model to 'war-game' the task - for each move write the expected success signal, the most likely failure plus its cause/signals, the counter-move, decision forks ('if you observe X, take route Y'), unresolved assumptions to flag, and abort conditions - and save it to a file a cheaper model then executes.
- [When Claude's code misses your quality bar, escalate model, effort, then CLAUDE.md and skills before rewriting](workflows/escalate-model-then-effort-then-claude-md-when-output-misses-the-bar.md): Boris Cherny gives an ordered ladder for fixing low-quality output on production code: newest model, higher effort, then CLAUDE.md and skills, then more steering, then have Claude pay down debt.
- [When the Agent Repeatedly Cannot Verify Something, Build It a CLI That Exposes the Signal](workflows/build-a-cli-to-expose-a-signal-the-agent-keeps-failing-to-verify.md): A verification the agent keeps failing at is usually a missing surface, not a missing instruction: write a small tool that prints the state it needs, and the loop closes.
- [Wire an External Auditing Tool In as the Scoreboard Before Asking for a Site-Wide Fix](workflows/give-bulk-fix-work-an-external-audit-score-as-its-target.md): For sprawling fix-everything work, connect a third-party auditor that emits a number (a site-audit score, a Lighthouse report) and make that score the target.
- [Wireframe UIs, Decks, and Schemas as ASCII Art in the Terminal Before Writing Any Code](workflows/ascii-wireframe-plan-in-terminal-before-coding.md): Before Claude writes code, have it render the landing page / dashboard / slide deck / DB schema as an ASCII-art diagram in the terminal, iterate on that text blueprint over multiple turns, then build only once you and it agree - instead of generating real code you overwrite.
- [Write the Numbered Task Lifecycle Into CLAUDE.md and Put Every Stage in a Subagent](workflows/encode-the-whole-task-lifecycle-in-claude-md-with-every-stage-in-a-subagent.md): Encode build → review → fix-loop → verify as numbered CLAUDE.md steps with each stage delegated to a subagent, so the main session only coordinates and never needs a context reset mid-task.
- [Write the README first, then hand it to Claude Code to build the tool](workflows/readme-driven-development-into-claude-code.md): Write the tool's complete README as the spec, dump it into Claude Code, and have it implement with red/green TDD. (Simon Willison).
- [Write the Why as a Proposal Artifact, Then Send the Model Back to It When It Drifts](workflows/write-a-proposal-artifact-the-model-can-be-sent-back-to.md): Before the design and the spec, write a proposal document capturing why you are building this, because it becomes the reference the model consults for decisions you never specified and the thing you point at when the build goes sideways.
- [Mock the new feature's UI with realistic fake data before planning the backend](workflows/mock-the-ui-with-fake-data-before-planning-the-backend.md): Edmund copies the layouts of successful apps, has Claude build the interface on mock data, and only then uses plan mode for the architecture.
- [Run /chrome to confirm the browser extension is connected before long UI sessions](workflows/run-chrome-to-confirm-the-extension-is-connected-so-claude-verifies-its-own-ui-work.md): Edmund runs /chrome to check the connection and permissions and follows the prompts until everything is green, so Claude can read the browser and test results before finishing.
- [Calibrate an LLM judge against about 100 human-labelled examples and optimise its prompt until agreement is high](workflows/build-a-judge-by-measuring-agreement-with-human-labels-then-optimising-its-prompt.md): Dave, a guest on Nate's channel) says to have a human review around 100 outputs, measure how often the judge agrees, then have an LLM rewrite the judge prompt in rounds and re-check occasionally for drift.
- [Do not accept a fast browser agent's 'done': hand the result back to Claude for an independent check](workflows/never-accept-a-fast-browser-agents-done-hand-the-result-back-to-claude-to-verify.md): In a Claude plus classifier-model browser loop, Claude must verify the goal when the classifier says done, because it can report a false done on an error page.
- [Point a Teardown at One Feature of an App You Like, Not the Whole App](workflows/reverse-engineer-one-feature-at-a-time-not-the-whole-app.md): Pick the specific behaviour you want (how Loom's camera bubble moves and resizes, say), have the agent investigate and rebuild that, and clone the functionality rather than the UI.
- [Turn Every Incident and Revert Into a Case in a Daily Eval for Your AI Reviewer](workflows/turn-every-incident-and-revert-into-a-daily-eval-case-for-your-ai-reviewer.md): Collect past incident PRs with their fix-forwards and reverts, have someone who knows the code confirm each bug, and score every change to the reviewer's prompt, tools or model against that set.
- [Tell Your AI Reviewer to Flag the Class of Bug So Every Instance Gets Fixed, Not One Line](workflows/ask-the-reviewer-to-flag-the-bug-class-not-one-line.md): A finding that names the bug class makes the author fix every instance in one round instead of one line per review pass.
- [When the Automated Reviewer Keeps Flagging the Same Thing, Fix It in the Skill, a Lint Rule or the Instruction File](workflows/fix-a-recurring-review-finding-at-its-source-not-per-pr.md): Turn a review comment that keeps recurring into a lint rule, a skill edit or an instruction-file line, rather than fixing it again in each PR.
- [Tell Claude to Keep Its Throwaway Test Scripts in the Unit Test Suite](workflows/keep-claudes-throwaway-test-scripts-in-the-test-suite.md): The scratch script Claude writes to check a new function is a regression test you have already paid for, so tell Claude to add it to the project's test suite instead of deleting it.
- [Replace Screenshot-Based Responsive Checks With a Playwright/CDP Audit Script, Then Wrap It in a Skill](workflows/replace-screenshot-responsive-checks-with-a-playwright-cdp-audit-script.md): Instead of having Claude resize a browser and read screenshots on every check, have it write a Playwright and Chrome DevTools Protocol script once that reports overflowing boxes, hidden text, broken or upscaled images and too-small tap areas without a model judging each page, then make a skill that points at the script.
- [When You Keep Doing a Step by Hand, Ask Yourself What Access Claude Is Missing](workflows/ask-what-claude-cannot-reach-when-you-keep-doing-a-step-by-hand.md): When you keep doing a task by hand that Claude could do, treat it as a sign Claude lacks the right access: ask whether it has the same information you do, and if not, connect what is missing.
- [Spend Verifier Agents on Work at the Edge of What the Model Can Do, Not on Routine Work](workflows/verify-at-the-edge-of-model-capability-not-everything.md): An Anthropic article from March 2026 found an evaluator adds lift only on parts of the build near the generator's limit.
- [After the First Review Rounds, Re-Review Only the New Diff and Make Findings Name the Bug Class](workflows/rereview-only-the-new-diff-and-flag-the-whole-bug-class.md): The Claude Code team's review loop re-reviews only the new diff after the first few passes, never repeats findings already posted, and flags the class of bug so the author fixes every instance, not one line.

### Agents

- [A Subagent's Whole Definition Lives in One File and Can Balloon to 1000+ Lines](agents/subagent-definitions-live-in-one-ballooning-file.md): Unlike a skill (main file plus lazily-loaded reference files), a subagent is defined entirely within a single file, so as you keep adding context it can grow to 500-1000+ lines with no progressive disclosure.
- [Add a CLAUDE.md Rule to Always Include a 'Why' in Subagent System Prompts](agents/include-a-why-in-every-subagent-system-prompt.md): Instruct Claude Code, at the user CLAUDE.md level, to always state the specific purpose behind a subagent it spawns, not just the task.
- [Agent-Team Teammates Don't Inherit Your Main Conversation History, Even Though They Share the Team Task List](agents/agent-team-teammates-dont-inherit-conversation-history.md): Spinning up teammates doesn't hand them the conversation history from your main session; you have to make sure Claude passes them enough context to actually do their job.
- [Agent-Team Teammates Now Inherit the Leader's Model: Name the Model in the Spawn](agents/agent-team-teammates-now-inherit-the-leader-s-model-name-the.md): The "Default teammate model" setting is gone; every teammate now runs on the leader's model unless the spawn call passes one explicitly.
- [Ask a Peer Session to Ping You When It Goes Idle With notify_when_idle Instead of Polling It](agents/ask-a-peer-session-to-ping-you-when-it-goes-idle-with-notify.md): Cross-session SendMessage takes a notify_when_idle flag that makes the other session send exactly one notice when it next goes idle, instead of you polling it for status.
- [Ask for the Foreground Explicitly When You Need a Subagent's Result This Turn](agents/subagents-now-run-in-the-background-by-default.md): Ordinary Task dispatches now run detached, so "dispatch a reviewer then act on its verdict" quietly continues without the verdict rather than erroring.
- [Assign Each Research Subagent Its Own Data Source When Mining for Pain Points](agents/fan-out-research-subagents-by-data-source.md): When researching a product idea from scratch, give each parallel subagent a distinct data source (owned community, YouTube comments, Reddit, X) rather than one subagent covering everything, then consolidate at the end.
- [At High Fan-Out, Skip Worktrees: Suggest-Only Agents Plus One Committing Orchestrator](agents/suggest-only-workers-one-committing-orchestrator-at-high-fan-out.md): Bun's 64-agent rewrite had workers propose changes without writing files while one orchestrator applied and committed, removing merge conflicts without per-agent worktree overhead.
- [Cap Parallel Subagent Concurrency With CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS (Default 20)](agents/cap-parallel-subagent-concurrency-with-max-concurrent-subagents.md): CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS caps how many subagents run at once (20 by default); set it in the settings env block whenever an unattended fan-out's cost or machine load matters.
- [Chain a Builder Subagent Into a Reviewer Subagent Through the Main Agent](agents/builder-validator-subagent-chain-via-main-agent.md): Since subagents cannot message each other directly, get a free build-then-review quality gate by having one subagent build something, letting its output return to the main agent, then handing that output to a second reviewer subagent.
- [Close a Go/No-Go With a Manager-Led Round Table Where Agents Are Allowed to Pass](agents/run-a-manager-led-round-table-where-agents-may-pass.md): For a judgment call that needs several agents' input, run one round where a designated manager asks each agent in turn for a contribution and lets agents with nothing to add pass, then records the next step.
- [Coordinate Parallel Agents Through Per-Section Plan Files, Kept Off the Commit Cadence](agents/give-parallel-agents-a-plan-file-blackboard-off-the-commit-cadence.md): driving the updates through frequent commits overloads CI and then starves the agents.
- [Debug an Agent Team by Reading ~/.claude/teams/<team-name>/ Directly on Disk](agents/agent-team-state-lives-under-claude-teams-directory.md): Claude Code's agent-teams feature writes its full state to disk under a per-team folder: a config file, a tasks folder, and an inboxes folder holding every message passed between teammates.
- [Delegate Noisy Tool Calls to Subagents; Avoid Subagents for Large Code Edits](agents/subagents-for-noisy-tool-calls-not-large-edits.md): Subagents keep main context lean by handling research, log analysis, and exploration; they are poor at large edits due to compression loss.
- [Disambiguate Overlapping Tool Descriptions With Explicit 'Do Not Use When' Clauses](agents/disambiguate-tool-descriptions-with-do-not-use-clauses.md): The most common cause of an agent calling the wrong tool is ambiguous, overlapping tool descriptions; fix it by having each description say precisely when to use the tool AND when not to - e.g. 'use get_customer for a customer ID and profile; do NOT use it when you have an order number, use lookup_order instead'.
- [Don't Assume Cross-Session SendMessage Auto-Delivers to a Bypassed-Permissions Session](agents/cross-session-sendmessage-spans-machines-but-holds-for-approval.md): Messaging now spans machines, but the unattended session most likely to need it holds inbound messages behind an approval dialog; tune `crossSessionInbound`/`dialogExpiry`.
- [Don't Poll Agent-Team Teammates: the Lead Is Notified Automatically When a Task Completes](agents/agent-team-teammates-auto-notify-dont-poll.md): You never have to manually check on individual teammates for completion; the team lead receives an automatic notification when a teammate finishes its task, so you can just watch the lead's pane.
- [Fan Out One Explore Subagent Per Audit Check When a Project Has 100+ Folders](agents/fan-out-one-subagent-per-audit-check-on-large-projects.md): For a knowledge-base audit skill running against a large project, spawn one Explore subagent per check (routing integrity, index truth, freshness, etc.), handing each the check's instructions verbatim plus the project root, then merge their reports.
- [Fanned-out subagents fail silently, so reserve fan-outs for steps where one worker's failure is cheap](agents/fan-out-subagent-steps-fail-silently-reserve-them-for-cheap-errors.md): A fan-out keeps going when one subagent fails; start new workflows as chains and parallelize only proven, genuinely independent steps.
- [Fix the Same Bug Across Sibling Projects With One Subagent Per Project](agents/cross-repo-parallel-fix-one-subagent-per-project.md): When you spot a bug in one project that you know exists in several other projects derived from the same template, fix it in parallel by spinning up one subagent per project.
- [Forked Subagents Are Cheap Despite Full History Because They Share the Main Session's Prompt Cache](agents/forked-subagents-share-main-session-prompt-cache.md): A forked subagent inherits the entire prior conversation, but because it reuses the same prompt cache as the main session, sending that history again doesn't cost nearly as much as a fresh, uncached prompt of the same size would.
- [Give a Custom Subagent omitClaudeMd: true to Run It Without Any CLAUDE.md](agents/omit-claude-md-from-a-subagent-with-omitclaudemd.md): Custom/plugin agent definitions (frontmatter or --agents JSON) can now declare omitClaudeMd to deliberately exclude user, project, and local CLAUDE.md from that subagent's context, while still loading managed (org) policy files.
- [Give Parallel Subagents a Shared Scratchpad File to Observe Them and Synthesize Without Sycophancy](agents/shared-scratchpad-file-for-subagent-observability-and-synthesis.md): Have each parallel subagent append its reasoning, key insights, and confidence to one shared markdown file, so you can watch otherwise-opaque subagents mid-run and then have the main session read the file and weigh their independent conclusions instead of rubber-stamping its own.
- [Give Standing Agents Seats, Not Sessions - Named Identity Plus Memory That Outlives the Run](agents/give-standing-agents-seats-not-sessions-named-identity-plus.md): Separate the ephemeral session ("a day in the life of an agent: wake up, do some work, go to sleep") from the durable seat - a named, addressable role carrying its own accumulated history - and attach memory to the seat rather than the session.
- [Give the Orchestrator Agent Broad Goals, Not a Narrow Checklist](agents/give-orchestrator-broad-goals-not-narrow-checklists.md): A coordinator/main agent that decomposes a task too narrowly makes its subagents miss whole categories even when each subagent does its job perfectly - e.g. 'research AI in creative industries' becomes only visual-arts subtasks, silently dropping music, writing, film, and games; give the orchestrator a broad goal and let the subagents figure out the breakdown.
- [Hand Each Agent-Team Batch Only 5-6 Tasks, Then Regroup Before the Next Batch](agents/batch-agent-team-tasks-in-small-increments.md): Even when you've planned a long list of subtasks up front, dole them out to the team in small batches of about 5-6, bring everyone back, then kick off the next batch rather than releasing the whole backlog at once.
- [Navigate Agent-Team Teammates With Shift+Up/Down, or Watch Them All via teamMode Plus tmux](agents/navigate-agent-teammates-with-shift-up-down-and-teammode-tmux.md): Inside an agent-team session, Shift+Up/Down cycles between teammates, and setting the teamMode option while running under tmux gives a split-pane view of every teammate at once.
- [On Opus 5, a Hidden "Do Not Call the AgentTool" Line Can Override Your Delegation Rules](agents/opus-5-injects-a-do-not-call-agenttool-line.md): An undocumented, server-gated system-prompt section tells Opus 5 not to spawn subagents or workflows unless asked; the original wording silently overrode a delegation-heavy CLAUDE.md, newer builds also accept a CLAUDE.md or skill but the old line can still be served, so probe with `claude -p` and ask per prompt.
- [Output Styles Don't Reach Subagents: Use a Fork When the Style Must Hold](agents/output-styles-don-t-reach-subagents-use-a-fork-when-the-style.md): A subagent ignores your output style because it builds its own system prompt; a `subagent_type: "fork"` inherits the parent's full system prompt and therefore the style.
- [Pair a Long-Running Worker Subagent With a Read-Only Observer That Catches Cheating and Drift](agents/pair-worker-subagents-with-observer-watchdog-agents.md): Launch with CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_OBSERVER_AGENTS=1 and add `observer: <agent>` to a worker's frontmatter so a read-only watchdog sees every tool call and course-corrects when it games/deletes tests or drifts from its constraints.
- [Pin `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH`: the Nesting Default Flipped Twice in Three Releases](agents/pin-subagent-spawn-depth-the-default-flipped-twice.md): Nesting went off in 2.1.217 and back to depth 3 in 2.1.219; a layered fan-out silently flattened and then silently tripled without you touching anything.
- [Pre-Aggregate Profiling/Runtime Data Before Handing It to an Agent](agents/pre-aggregate-profiling-data-for-agents.md): When you want a coding agent to fix slow code, don't dump raw profiler output, give it a single tool that aggregates samples (frequencies, percentiles) into an analyzed view, because LLMs are bad at doing those computations themselves.
- [Prove a New CLAUDE.md Rule Is Discoverable by Spawning Subagents Who Were Not Told About It](agents/blind-test-a-claude-md-change-with-naive-subagents.md): re-reading your own prose cannot tell you whether an instruction is findable.
- [Reject Agent Fixes That Add Tolerant Readers and Fallbacks: Make the Bad State Impossible](agents/make-bad-state-impossible-not-tolerant.md): When an agent hits a malformed state, its default is to pile on a tolerant parser, then a fallback, then a migration, then debug output. The correct fix is usually to enforce an invariant upstream so the bad state can never occur.
- [Require Every Delegated Worker to Write Its Progress Back to the Plan Document](agents/make-delegated-workers-write-progress-back-to-the-plan.md): Give the orchestrator the plan document as the single source of truth and require each delegated worker to write its progress back into it (or into its own status folder), so the orchestrator tracks state by reading the plan instead of re-reading the codebase.
- [Restate critical rules in subagent prompts: built-in Explore and Plan agents skip CLAUDE.md](agents/restate-critical-rules-in-subagent-prompts-explore-and-plan-skip-claude-md.md): Auto-delegated Explore/Plan subagents receive none of your CLAUDE.md rules, so must-hold constraints have to travel in the delegation prompt itself.
- [Run a Supervisor Agent Whose Job Is Unblocking the Workers, Escalating Only What It Can't Fix](agents/supervisor-agent-unblocks-worker-agents-and-escalates-by-exception.md): Fan-out stalls whenever a worker hits a question; give the supervisor the job of answering them itself and notifying you only on what it can't unblock.
- [Run Tool Execution in Its Own Process So a Tool Crash Does Not Kill the Agent Run](agents/run-tool-execution-in-a-process-separate-from-the-agent.md): A tool that loads a large CSV and runs out of memory should take down the tool process, not the hours-long agent trajectory it was serving.
- [Scope Agent-Team Tasks Small and Add an Adversarial Reviewer Teammate](agents/scope-team-tasks-small-add-adversarial-reviewer.md): Give teammates small, frequently-delivered tasks and add a security/devil's-advocate agent that critiques peers in real time (e.g. flags an unauthorized endpoint as it's created).
- [Shut Down Agent Teams Gracefully: Ask Teammates to Save First, Don't Force-Kill](agents/shut-down-agent-teams-gracefully.md): End an agent-team session by messaging each teammate to save and confirm, then close, force-killing mid-task leaves uncommitted work and partial outputs.
- [Spawn Agent-Team Teammates via the Agent name Param: No TeamCreate Step](agents/spawn-teammates-via-the-agent-name-param-no-teamcreate.md): TeamCreate/TeamDelete are gone; with CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1 every session has one implicit team, spawn teammates by passing a name to the Agent tool. (Old 'create a team first' guidance is obsolete.).
- [Spin Up a Persona-Diverse Agent Debate Panel for Unbiased Decisions](agents/agent-team-debate-panel-for-decisions.md): For a strategy/design call, create teammates with distinct personas (skeptic, customer, competitor, expert), have them research independently and debate to consensus, then mine the transcript.
- [Spin Up One Subagent Per Competing Implementation Idea, Let the Main Session Pick](agents/subagent-per-competing-approach-then-main-session-decides.md): When torn between a few ways to implement something, spawn a separate subagent per approach to research/explore each one, feed all results back to the main session, and have the main session decide and present a single plan rather than exploring every option in the main context.
- [Spin Up Persona Subagents for a Quick Gut-Check, Not a Full Debate](agents/quick-persona-subagents-for-first-impression-not-debate.md): When you just need signal on messaging or design, tell persona subagents to give only their first impression, explicitly skipping debate or deep analysis.
- [Start Agent Teams at 3-5 Teammates: Token Cost Scales Roughly Linearly Per Teammate](agents/cap-agent-teams-at-three-to-five-teammates.md): Running more than 3-4 teammates at once starts to feel like overkill because token spend scales close to linearly with headcount, even though nothing technically stops you from spawning more.
- [Stop Reaching for TaskOutput, It Was Removed; Read the Task's Output File Instead](agents/taskoutput-tool-removed-read-the-output-file-instead.md): The TaskOutput tool for reading a background task's/agent's output no longer exists as of 2.1.277; the only way to read a background task's output now is Read on its output file, and taskOutputMaxChars/TASK_MAX_OUTPUT_LENGTH are dead settings.
