- [Add a Subagent's Name to the Deny List to Disable It Without Deleting the File](permissions/deny-list-a-subagent-name-to-disable-it-without-deleting-it.md): Instead of deleting an unused subagent's file, add its name to the deny list in settings.json to make it unavailable to Claude Code while keeping the definition on disk.
- [Add AskUserQuestion to Deny List to Stop Unwanted Interruptions](permissions/deny-list-to-disable-interactive-question-tool.md): Adding `AskUserQuestion` to the deny list in settings.json prevents Claude from interrupting autonomous runs with clarifying questions.
- [Agent-Team Permissions Propagate: A Bypassed-Permissions Session Passes That to Every Teammate](permissions/agent-team-bypass-permissions-propagate-restrict-reviewer-explicitly.md): If the team lead starts with dangerously-skip-permissions on, every spawned teammate inherits that same unrestricted permission level, including a reviewer teammate you wanted to keep read-only.
- [AskUserQuestion No Longer Auto-Continues: Set an Idle Timeout for Unattended Runs](permissions/askuserquestion-no-longer-auto-continues-set-idle-timeout.md): AskUserQuestion now waits indefinitely instead of auto-continuing; opt into an idle timeout via /config so unattended runs don't hang (or deny the tool).
- [Audit Repos You Cloned Inside a Trusted Directory - They Used to Inherit Trust Silently](permissions/nested-repos-no-longer-inherit-parent-trust.md): Before 2.1.232 a nested clone's CLAUDE.md, settings and hooks loaded with no prompt, making a vendored or scratch repo a silent code-execution surface.
- [Audit Sandbox Deny Entries for Trailing Slashes, Which Were Silently Bypassable Before 2.1.224](permissions/sandbox-deny-entries-with-a-trailing-slash-were-bypassable.md): A rule like `denyRead: "~/.aws/"` looked correct, warned about nothing, and did not hold on Linux or macOS.
- [Auto Mode No Longer Classifier-Reviews a Skill/Command's Inline ! Shell Lines](permissions/skill-inline-shell-follows-permission-rules-in-auto-mode.md): An inline `!command` shell line inside a skill or slash-command definition used to be judged by auto mode's safety classifier like any other Bash call; as of 2.1.271 it instead follows your default-mode permission rules (allow/ask/deny), and only falls back to a reviewed tool call when no rule decides it.
- [Auto Mode Pauses After 3 Consecutive Blocks, and a -p Run Just Skips the Action](permissions/auto-mode-pauses-after-repeated-blocks-headless-runs-skip-the-action.md): After 3 consecutive or 20 total classifier blocks auto mode resumes prompting, and a headless -p run with no --permission-prompt-tool silently skips the blocked work while reporting on.
- [Bracket IPv6 Literals in Sandbox Network Lists, Then Run `/doctor`](permissions/bracket-ipv6-literals-in-sandbox-network-allowlists.md): An unbracketed IPv6 entry is an ambiguous spelling that never meant what it looked like; now enforced fail-closed, so a working allowlist may start blocking after upgrade.
- [Check /status's "Auto mode server" Row to See If Your Classifier Billing Is Free](permissions/check-status-auto-mode-server-row-for-classifier-billing.md): Since 2.1.278, auto mode's safety classifier runs free on Anthropic's server by default for API/Enterprise/Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry/gateway users; when a gateway strips headers or the platform hasn't rolled it out, Claude Code silently falls back to billed local classifier requests, and /status is the only way to tell which one you're on.
- [Configured Permission Denies Still Apply Under --dangerously-skip-permissions](permissions/permission-deny-rules-hold-under-dangerously-skip-permissions.md): Even with permission prompts fully skipped, entries you put in `/permissions` (e.g., blocking `rm -rf` or deletion mutations) still get enforced.
- [Contain Coding Agents at the Environment Layer, Never via Permission Prompts Alone](permissions/contain-agents-at-environment-layer-not-permission-prompts.md): Permission prompts suffer approval fatigue (users approve ~93% of them), so they're a weak primary defense. Enforce boundaries with sandboxes, VMs, and egress controls first, then steer behavior at the model layer.
- [Deny-Read Secrets With a Wildcard Rule: Wildcard Denies Only Became Rename-Proof in 2.1.236](permissions/deny-read-secrets-with-a-wildcard-rule-wildcard-denies-only.md): On macOS, a wildcard sandbox read-deny such as `**/.env` did not take precedence inside an allowed read region and could be bypassed by renaming the file, until 2.1.236.
- [Do not rely on a Bash deny rule as a boundary, it misses path-qualified and `sh -c` forms](permissions/bash-deny-rules-miss-path-qualified-and-sh-c-forms.md): `Bash(rm *)` stops `rm -rf build/` but not `/bin/rm -rf build/` or `bash -c 'rm -rf build/'`; the docs table also lists `Bash(curl *)` vs `/usr/bin/curl` and `sh -c`.
- [Don't Assume `< file` Redirection Is Permission-Checked - It Shipped, Then Was Reverted](permissions/bash-input-redirection-is-not-permission-checked.md): Gated in 2.1.232 and reverted in 2.1.233, so a command-name-scoped deny rule does not cover the redirection spelling of the same read. REVISIT.
- [Don't Build Your Own AI Slackbot by Default: a Feedback Channel Is an Attacker-Controlled Input](permissions/default-to-claude-tag-a-feedback-channel-is-an-injection-vector.md): Claude Code's own team argues for Claude Tag over a DIY Slack agent because it routes through auto mode; the security cost is the tiebreaker against the capability case.
- [Don't Count Auto Mode as Prompt-Injection Containment: It Can Even Block the Cleanup](permissions/auto-mode-is-not-injection-containment-isolate-the-session.md): A targeted injection chain beat Opus 5 in auto mode 60-80% of the time, and the classifier sometimes allowed the malware and denied the kill command; isolate the session instead.
- [Don't Scope Permissions With Write(path)/Glob(path)/NotebookEdit(path): Use Edit(path) or Read(path)](permissions/avoid-write-glob-permission-rules-use-edit-read.md): Write(path)/Glob(path)/NotebookEdit(path) rules are silent no-ops (now warned at startup); scope with Edit(path) for writes and Read(path) for reads.
- [Give Agents a Local Mock Dev Environment Instead of Production Credentials](permissions/give-agents-a-local-mock-dev-env-instead-of-prod-credentials.md): Stand up a local Docker Compose / Postgres / mock-server stack the agent runs against so it never needs real product credentials.
- [Harden Always-On Agents: Sandbox, Least-Privilege Tools, and Capable Models Only](permissions/harden-always-on-agents-least-privilege-and-capable-models.md): Sandbox 24/7 phone agents, scope credentials tightly, add tools incrementally, only run reviewed skills, and avoid cheap models (they resist prompt injection less).
- [In Auto Mode, Explicitly Ask for Destructive git/terraform Commands or They're Blocked](permissions/auto-mode-blocks-destructive-commands-unless-you-ask.md): Auto mode now blocks destructive git (reset --hard, checkout -- ., clean -fd, stash drop, unrequested --amend) and terraform/pulumi/cdk destroy unless your prompt explicitly asked for that action.
- [Keep Credentials Out of the Sandbox Where Generated Code Runs: Enforce With sandbox.credentials](permissions/keep-credentials-out-of-the-agent-sandbox.md): Make auth tokens unreachable from the sandbox where Claude's generated code runs (broker them outside the boundary); in Claude Code, the sandbox.credentials setting blocks sandboxed commands from reading credential files and secret env vars.
- [Move autoMode Config Out of settings.local.json: It's Now Ignored There](permissions/relocate-automode-config-to-user-settings.md): An autoMode block in repo-resident .claude/settings.local.json is now silently ignored; put auto-mode config in user-level ~/.claude/settings.json.
- [On Windows, Upgrade Past 2.1.233 - Three Separate Permission Bypasses Were Fixed in Two Releases](permissions/three-windows-permission-bypasses-fixed-in-2-1-232-and-2-1-233.md): A PowerShell `$PSDefaultParameterValues` bypass, a Git Bash symlink bypass, and an NT device-prefix bypass that leaked NTLM credentials.
- [Pair Sandbox Mode With Your Own HTTP Proxy Script to Domain-Allowlist Internet Access](permissions/sandbox-plus-http-proxy-domain-allowlist-for-skip-permissions.md): Instead of running dangerously-skip-permissions with unrestricted internet, enable the sandbox and write an HTTP proxy script that only allows a specific list of domains.
- [Pin a Permission Mode, Because Auto Mode Is Now the Default on Every Plan and Provider](permissions/pin-a-permission-mode-because-auto-mode-is-now-the-default-everywhere.md): With no permission mode configured, interactive terminal and VS Code sessions now start in auto mode on every plan and provider, and so do `claude -p` and Python Agent SDK sessions on third-party providers or with telemetry off. If you want per-command review, set `permissions.defaultMode` explicitly.
- [Put a `!` Permission Exception in the Same Settings File as the Rule It Carves Out](permissions/permission-negation-rules-apply-only-within-their-own-settings-source.md): a `!`-prefixed rule now applies only within its own settings source, and a bare `!` is ignored.
- [Put the Justification in Your Own Message: Auto Mode Never Reads Claude's Reasoning](permissions/the-auto-mode-classifier-is-reasoning-blind-by-design.md): the classifier sees only your messages and the tool calls, Claude's own text is stripped.
- [Re-check Bash rules with a mid-pattern `:*`, settings files now honor them](permissions/bash-rules-with-a-mid-pattern-colon-star-now-apply-from-settings-files.md): A Bash permission rule with `:*` in the middle of its pattern used to be skipped when it came from a settings file, while `--allowedTools` honored it. Since 2.1.282 it applies from every source, with a startup warning on how it matches.
- [Read Untrusted Material With claude --restricted, Which Strips Every Command-Running Tool](permissions/open-untrusted-material-in-a-claude-restricted-session.md): A capability removal rather than a policy check: no command tools, no WebFetch, file tools confined to cwd, bypassPermissions refused, and the repo's own settings files ignored.
- [Review What a Subagent Hands Back: Auto Mode Can Block the Spawn but Only Warns on Return](permissions/auto-mode-can-block-a-subagent-spawn-but-only-warns-on-return.md): the safety net is asymmetric, tight going in and loose coming back.
- [rm -rf "$(pwd)"-Style Command-Substitution Targets Now Always Prompt, Even With an Allow Rule](permissions/rm-of-command-substitution-output-now-always-prompts.md): Before 2.1.281, an `rm -rf` whose target was purely command-substitution output (like `rm -rf "$(pwd)"`) could run completely unprompted under auto mode or --dangerously-skip-permissions, even bypassing an existing Bash allow rule that should not have covered it; it now always stops for approval unless you explicitly opt out.
- [Rotate Any Key the Agent Used During Development Before It Becomes the Production Key](permissions/rotate-dev-exposed-keys-before-production.md): Before launch, rotate every API key and secret the agent handled while building (regenerate the Anthropic key, swap Stripe test keys for live ones, refresh service-role secrets) instead of promoting the development values to production.
- [Run /permissions Mid-Task to Change Mode Without Rerunning the Work](permissions/change-permission-mode-mid-task-with-permissions.md): /permissions overrides the running session's mode from that point on, so when you see a task heading for an action it cannot take, switch mode or add a rule in place rather than aborting and starting again with the right settings.
- [Scan Third-Party Skill/Plugin Repos for Malicious Code Before Installing Them](permissions/scan-third-party-skills-for-malware-before-installing.md): Treat every installable skill/plugin as an attack surface and run a dedicated skill-security scanner (e.g. Nvidia's SkillSpector) over an untrusted repo before install, then paste its findings into Claude to explain the realistic attack scenarios.
- [Scope Auto-Mode Sandbox Network Access Per-Command With allowed_domains](permissions/scope-sandboxed-network-per-command-with-allowed-domains.md): Bash, PowerShell, and Monitor tool calls in sandboxed auto mode can now carry a per-command allowed_domains list, reviewed alongside the command itself, so network access is scoped to exactly the hosts that one command needs rather than a session-wide allowlist.
- [Scope Tool Access, Not Just the Prompt, to Restrict What an Agent Can Do](permissions/scope-tool-access-not-just-the-prompt-to-restrict-agent-actions.md): "Only draft, never send" is a suggestion a non-deterministic model eventually violates (one agent mailed 150,000 people); cap the capability with a scoped key instead.
- [Set `sandbox.network.strictAllowlist` So Unattended Sandboxed Runs Fail Instead of Hanging on a Prompt](permissions/sandbox-network-strict-allowlist-for-unattended-runs.md): A prompt-on-unknown-host policy turns an egress control into a liveness problem the moment nobody is at the keyboard.
- [Set autoMode.classifyAllShell to Run Every Shell Command Through the Auto-Mode Classifier](permissions/automode-classify-all-shell.md): By default auto mode only classifies shell commands matching code-execution patterns; set autoMode.classifyAllShell to route all Bash/PowerShell through the classifier for unattended runs. (2.1.193).
- [Spawned Sessions (Teammates, /bg, claude agents, --worktree --tmux) Now Actually Inherit --setting-sources](permissions/setting-sources-now-reaches-spawned-sessions.md): If you launch a parent session with a restricted --setting-sources (e.g.
- [Start Your Permission Allowlist With Reads, Tests, Dev Server, and Git; Gate Installs, Deletes, and Network Calls](permissions/default-safe-permission-allowlist-starter-set.md): A practical default settings.json allowlist pre-approves file reads, running the dev server, running tests, and git operations, while keeping package installs, file/folder deletions, and anything touching the network gated behind manual approval.
- [Stop Treating `Bash(tee:*)` as an Escape Hatch Around `Edit()` Deny Rules](permissions/tee-no-longer-bypasses-edit-deny-rules.md): 2.1.269 closed the hole where a tee allow rule wrote past Edit() denies and outside the working directories.
- [Strip the default connectors when you create a routine](permissions/strip-default-connectors-when-creating-a-routine.md): New routines attach every connector you have by default, over-privileging unattended runs and paying context for definitions they never use.
- [Unattended Auto and Bypass Sessions Now Auto-Deny a Dangerous rm After Two Minutes](permissions/dangerous-rm-prompts-auto-deny-after-two-minutes-unattended.md): Since 2.1.281 the dangerous-rm prompt in auto mode and `--dangerously-skip-permissions` waits two minutes, then denies the command with a rewrite hint so an unattended run keeps going instead of hanging on the prompt.
- [Upgrade Past 2.1.251: Symlinks and Arithmetic Assignments Bypassed Permission Checks](permissions/upgrade-past-2-1-251-symlink-and-arithmetic-permission-bypasses.md): Read(...) deny rules were dodged via symlinked search paths and post-check symlink swaps, and Bash auto-approved arithmetic assignments; the rules were right, the enforcement was not.
- [Use Sandbox Mode for Safe Experimentation and Automated Skill Runs](permissions/sandbox-mode-for-safe-exploration.md): `/sandbox` enables file/network isolation; configure excludedCommands, allowLocalBinding, and allowUnixSockets for your use case.
- [Weigh the Blast Radius Before Granting an MCP Server Full Database Access](permissions/weigh-blast-radius-before-full-access-mcp-servers.md): A convenient MCP server like Supabase's gives Claude broad access to your project, including the theoretical ability to delete or corrupt your entire database schema, so the productivity gain has to be weighed against that risk.
- [Wildcards in Permission Rules Now Match at Any Position, Not Just the End](permissions/wildcard-permission-patterns-now-match-any-position.md): You can now put a wildcard anywhere in a Bash permission pattern, e.g. allow any install command or any git subcommand, instead of only being able to wildcard the trailing portion.
- [Write Permission Rules That Match Tool Parameters, e.g. Agent(model:opus)](permissions/match-tool-parameters-in-permission-rules.md): Permission rules now support Tool(param:value) with wildcards, match a tool's inputs, not just its name (e.g. deny Agent(model:opus) to block Opus subagents).
- [Add an Allow Rule for pyright if a Loop Runs It: Bash Now Asks Before Running It](permissions/add-an-allow-rule-for-pyright-it-is-no-longer-treated-as-read-only.md): Since 2.1.290 the Bash tool asks for permission before running `pyright`, which is no longer treated as a read-only command.
- [Write MCP Allow Rules and Agent tools: Entries as mcp__<server>__*, Because a Bare mcp__<server> No Longer Covers Similar Names](permissions/write-mcp-allow-rules-as-mcp-server-star-because-bare-names-are-now-exact.md): Since 2.1.299 `mcp__a` allows only the server named `a`, not `a__b` or `a_`, so a rule that leaned on prefix matching may silently stop covering the server you meant.
- [Choose the Client-Side Auto Mode Classifier's Model With CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_MODE_MODEL](permissions/pin-the-client-side-auto-mode-classifier-model-with-claude-code-auto-mode-model.md): You can set which model judges auto mode tool calls, but only when the classifier runs client-side; it has no effect on server-side auto mode.
- [Run /auto-mode-setup to Draft autoMode.environment Before Reaching for --dangerously-skip-permissions](permissions/run-auto-mode-setup-to-draft-environment-entries-before-skipping-permissions.md): Auto mode's classifier trusts only the working directory and the repo's configured remotes, so work on your own servers, buckets or org repos keeps getting blocked until it is described in `autoMode.environment`; `/auto-mode-setup` drafts those entries from your project and recent sessions for you to review.
- [Turn On sandbox.allowNestedUserNamespaces Only Where a Sandboxed Tool Needs User Namespaces](permissions/enable-sandbox-allow-nested-user-namespaces-only-where-a-tool-needs-it.md): Since 2.1.305 a sandbox setting lets sandboxed Linux commands create user namespaces (headless Chromium, rootless podman), and the changelog says it lets them get around the sandbox's file protections, so enable it only in the repo that needs it.
- [Claude Code Asks Before Every cd-Then-git Compound by Design, So Have Git Run Without the cd](permissions/cd-then-git-compounds-always-ask-route-git-through-absolute-paths.md): A Bash command that changes directory and then runs git gets its own approval prompt, which an earlier "allow" does not clear, because git run in another directory can pick up that repo's hooks or config; the poster avoids it by sending git through one fixed entry point.
- [Never Let a Bot Approval Fill a Required Human Review Slot, and Dismiss Approvals When New Commits Land](permissions/never-count-an-agent-approval-and-dismiss-approvals-on-new-commits.md): The Claude Code team's rules: an agent's approval never counts as a person's, the person directing the agent counts as one of the required reviewers and is on the hook for the change, and an approval covers one commit, so new commits the system cannot vouch for need approval again.
- [Test an inbox-reading agent by emailing yourself hidden instructions](permissions/send-yourself-fake-malicious-emails-to-test-an-inbox-agent.md): If an agent triages your email with broad read and write access, send yourself realistic emails that hide instructions (invisible text or HTML) and check the agent does not obey them.
- [Turn On permissions.blockWritesOutsideWorkingDirectories So Write and Edit Refuse Outside Paths in Every Mode](permissions/turn-on-block-writes-outside-working-directories.md): Since 2.1.314 one setting makes Write, Edit and NotebookEdit refuse any path outside the working directories, in every permission mode, auto and bypass included.
- [Hunt Secrets With a Script, Not an Agent Prompt: Deciding a String Is a Secret Means Reading It Into Context](permissions/hunt-secrets-with-a-script-not-an-agent-prompt.md): An agent asked to inventory secrets in a home folder found 63 and obeyed the no-printing rule, but had to read each candidate file to classify it, so the values went through the context window with no permission prompt.

### Context Management

- [@-Mention a File Instead of Telling Claude to Go Find It](context-management/at-mention-files-instead-of-asking-claude-to-find-them.md): An @-mention attaches the file's contents directly to the request, while naming the file in prose makes the model search directories and read outputs first, so the same work costs extra tool calls and extra context.
- [Accept the Resume-From-Summary Offer on Big Stale Sessions](context-management/accept-the-resume-from-summary-offer-on-stale-sessions.md): Resume a session older than an hour that burned over 100k tokens and Claude Code stops before your first message to offer resuming from a summary instead.
- [Add a CLAUDE.md Rule to Aggressively Offload Research, Docs, and Log Analysis to Subagents](context-management/claude-md-rule-to-aggressively-offload-research-to-subagents-for-0-50-context.md): Add a user-level CLAUDE.md instruction telling Claude Code to always push online research, doc lookups, codebase exploration, and log analysis into subagents (log-checking specifically to a Haiku subagent), to stay in the 0-50% context range.
- [After Finding the Root Cause via a Failed-Attempts Log, Use It to Strip Earlier Bandaid Changes](context-management/strip-bandaid-changes-using-the-failed-attempts-log-after-root-cause-found.md): Once the logged failed-attempts file leads Claude Code to the real fix, feed the log back so it can identify and remove the leftover changes from earlier failed attempts, leaving only the minimal true fix.
- [After Pasting a Handoff Summary Into a Fresh Session, Tell It Not to Act Yet](context-management/tell-fresh-session-not-to-act-until-instructed-after-handoff.md): When you copy a handoff summary into a newly cleared session, explicitly say 'don't do anything yet until I tell you' so the agent doesn't rush into stale next-steps.
- [Append 'Give Me a Quick Answer' Instead of Downgrading the Model for One Question](context-management/ask-for-a-quick-answer-instead-of-dropping-the-model.md): For a throwaway question deep in an expensive session, add 'give me a quick answer' to the prompt.
- [Ask Claude Code to Find and Split Every File Over 500 Lines](context-management/modularize-files-over-500-lines-to-cap-context-cost-of-edits.md): Periodically ask Claude Code to identify all files over 500 lines and modularize them, because editing a file always requires reading it in full first.
- [Ask Claude to 'Summarize Recent Commits' to Rebuild Context When You Return to a Project](context-management/summarize-recent-commits-to-rebuild-context.md): Instead of writing manual session notes, treat your git log as a diary: when you come back to a project, ask Claude to summarize recent commits to reconstruct what happened.
- [Be a Context Minimalist: Provision a Lean Harness and Let Claude Pull the Rest](context-management/be-a-context-minimalist-provision-a-lean-harness.md): Start with a minimal system prompt/CLAUDE.md and minimal tools, then let Claude fetch context on demand, instead of front-loading everything "just in case" (the Claude Code team's own default).
- [Build a 'Session Handoff' Skill That Outputs a Paste-Back Summary Before You Clear Context](context-management/build-a-session-handoff-skill-for-manual-context-clearing.md): Package a skill that, on request, writes a summary of what's been done, key files, and next steps, so you can copy it, /clear the session, and paste it back into a fresh window.
- [Build the Knowledge Base From Named Practitioners' Own Material, and Keep It In the Project Rather Than a Separate Notebook App](context-management/build-the-knowledge-base-from-named-practitioners-inside-the-project.md): When you need the lived-experience answer rather than the textbook one, pick specific practitioners who have solved the problem, gather their own writing and talks into files in the Claude Code project, and ask questions against those files.
- [Compact Only Part of the Conversation with Summarize-From-Here / Up-To-Here](context-management/directional-summarize-rewind-menu.md): `Esc Esc` (or `/rewind`) → pick a message → 'Summarize from here' condenses everything after that point; 'Summarize up to here' condenses earlier messages while keeping recent ones full. A surgical alternative to `/compact`.
- [Compact Proactively, Don't Wait for Auto-Compact](context-management/context-rot-prevention-compact-proactively.md): By the time Claude auto-compacts, you've already been operating in degraded mode. Compact or clear before context quality drops.
- [Convert HTML/PDF/DOCX to Markdown Before Feeding Documents to Claude](context-management/convert-docs-to-markdown-before-feeding-claude.md): Markdown tokenizes far more cheaply than HTML/PDF/DOCX; convert source docs to markdown first to fit much more into the window.
- [Describe Complex State With a Mermaid Diagram Instead of Thousands of Tokens of Prose](context-management/compress-complex-state-into-mermaid-diagrams.md): A Mermaid diagram conveys what thousands of tokens of text would, so express complex state descriptions as diagrams to spend far fewer tokens on the same information.
- [Diagnose Which Context Failure Mode Is Happening Before You Fix It: Poisoning, Bloat, Confusion, or Clash](context-management/diagnose-context-failure-mode-before-fixing-it.md): When an agent's answer is wrong because of its knowledge base, classify the failure as poisoning (a false fact sits among true ones), bloat (too much data to find what's relevant), confusion (a fact is missing or irrelevant so the model guesses), or clash (two data sources disagree and it doesn't know which is current), because each has a different fix.
- [Don't Set `CLAUDE_CODE_WORKFLOW_PREFIX_STAGGER_MS=0` - the Stagger Is What Buys the Cache](context-management/never-zero-the-workflow-prefix-stagger.md): The delay lets the first sibling write the prompt cache and the rest read it, so zeroing it to launch faster is a direct token-cost multiplier on every fan-out.
- [Downgrade the Model Inside a Forked Tangent Session](context-management/fork-session-then-downgrade-model-for-tangents.md): When you fork a session to ask a tangent question about why the agent chose a particular approach, also switch that forked session to a cheaper model (e.g. Sonnet), since a tangent Q&A doesn't need the main session's model.
- [Even With a 1M-Token Model, Stay Under ~200K Tokens to Avoid Context Anxiety](context-management/cap-1m-context-usage-around-200k-tokens-to-avoid-context-anxiety.md): Citing Cognition AI's research on Devin, models near their context ceiling start taking shortcuts or leaving work incomplete ('context anxiety'). The fix is to use a 1M-context model but deliberately not go past ~200,000 tokens, or note the cap in CLAUDE.md if you lack 1M context.
- [Expect CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_1M_CONTEXT to Bite on Any Native-1M Model Now, Not Just a Fixed List](context-management/disable-1m-context-now-clamps-every-native-1m-model.md): It used to apply only to a hardcoded list, so on other 1M models it silently did nothing; a startup warning now surfaces when the 200K clamp is not holding.
- [Feed Bulk Reference Text as an Image, Not Text, to Cut Its Token Cost](context-management/render-text-as-images-to-cut-input-tokens.md): Images tokenize at a different rate, so ~10k tokens of text can land at 3-4k rendered as an image, for read-once reference material only, since OCR is imperfect and the pricing gap may close.
- [Give Each Distinct Project Its Own Folder to Keep Claude's Context Scoped](context-management/separate-folder-per-project-scopes-context.md): Keep entirely separate projects (a product, a client engagement, an internal tool) in their own top-level folders so opening Claude on one only exposes it to that project's files.
- [If You Use /compact, Run It Inside the Cache Window, Not After the Session Has Gone Cold](context-management/compact-inside-the-cache-window-not-after-it-expires.md): Compacting more than an hour after your last message means the cache is gone and the model must reprocess the entire conversation just to produce the summary, so compact regularly during active work or clear instead.
- [Keep Junk Directories Out of Context With .claudeignore Plus an Enforced Permissions Deny](context-management/block-junk-directory-reads-with-claudeignore-and-permission-deny.md): .claudeignore is only advisory, so layer it with a permissions.deny on Read(node_modules/**), Read(dist/**), etc. to enforce it, a measured setup reported ~85% context reduction.
- [Keep Parallel Sibling Agents' Prompt Prefixes Identical So They Share One Prompt Cache](context-management/keep-sibling-agent-prompt-prefixes-identical-to-share-the-cache.md): In a fan-out, the first agent writes the prompt cache and same-prefix siblings read it; divergent per-agent preambles mean every agent pays full input cost on the most expensive part of the fan-out.
- [Know What Invalidates the Prompt Cache: Don't Switch Models or Edit CLAUDE.md Mid-Session](context-management/prompt-cache-what-breaks-it.md): Switching models mid-session and editing CLAUDE.md/early context invalidate the prompt cache, forcing a full reprocess; batch those between sessions, not during.
- [Launch Claude Code From the Project Root, Not a Subdirectory](context-management/launch-claude-code-from-project-root-for-clean-context.md): Start `claude` in the repo's root directory, because that is the working directory Claude zips into its very first context load.
- [Load a Context Memory File Before Sending Work Prompts](context-management/context-memory-file-before-prompting.md): Create a memory/context file that tells Claude who you are and how you work before executing task prompts; this context matters more than prompt quality.
- [Mine Your Own Session JSONL Instead of Only Reading /insights' Format](context-management/mine-own-jsonl-for-custom-insights-categories.md): Your Claude Code sessions are stored as JSONL files, so you can build fully custom usage categories and analytics directly from that data instead of being limited to /insights' built-in report format.
- [Need a Different Model or Effort Level Mid-Session? Spawn a Subagent Instead of Switching](context-management/spawn-a-subagent-instead-of-switching-model-mid-session.md): Changing the model or the effort level in a running session invalidates the cache and forces a full reread on the next message, so delegate the differently-modelled step to a subagent and leave the main session's prefix intact.
- [On a Subscription, Claude Code's Prompt Cache Lasts ~1 Hour: the 5-Minute Figure Is the API/Subagent Default](context-management/prompt-cache-is-one-hour-on-subscription.md): The "5-minute cache window" is the API ephemeral default (and what subagents get); a subscription session caches ~1 hour, so an idle gap under an hour still resumes at the ~10% cached-input rate.
- [On an API Key or Cloud Provider, Opt Into the 1-Hour Prompt Cache With promptCacheTtl](context-management/opt-into-the-1-hour-prompt-cache-with-promptcachettl-on-api-billing.md): The 1-hour window is not automatic off a subscription; set promptCacheTtl "1h" and keep subagents on the cheaper 5-minute TTL with subagentPromptCacheTtl.
- [Through an ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL Gateway, Forward the anthropic-beta Header Unchanged to Get the 1-Hour Cache, Then Check usage.cache_creation](context-management/forward-anthropic-beta-through-a-gateway-to-get-the-1-hour-cache.md): Part of the one-hour TTL request travels as `extended-cache-ttl` in `anthropic-beta`, so the gateway must forward it; confirm with `ephemeral_1h_input_tokens` in `claude -p --output-format json`.
- [Persist Plan, Findings, and Failures in a Three-File Memory Pattern That Survives Context Resets](context-management/persist-plan-findings-and-failures-in-a-three-file-memory-pattern.md): Adopt a Manus-style three-file memory system, one file tracking phases/progress, one storing research and findings, one logging each session's results and errors, updated on a two-action cadence, so a context reset loses neither your findings nor the record of what already failed.
- [Proxy Verbose Command Output Through RTK to Stop Burning Input Tokens](context-management/rtk-proxy-filters-command-output-to-cut-input-tokens.md): Install RTK as a hook between the shell and Claude Code so noisy command output (git diff/status/log, ls, file reads, test runs) is filtered, aggregated, truncated, and deduplicated before it ever enters the context window.
- [Prune the Exploration Output Before It Feeds the Plan, and Route Good-But-Not-Now Ideas to a Backlog](context-management/prune-exploration-output-before-it-feeds-the-plan.md): Exploration generates far more ideas than belong in this build; run an explicit pruning pass between exploring and planning, discard what has no value and file the rest as backlog issues instead of accepting them into scope.
- [Rewind Instead of Arguing With Stale Context](context-management/rewind-instead-of-arguing-with-stale-context.md): When Claude has a fundamental misconception, /rewind back to before the error and reprompt; don't try to correct 20% stale context inline.
- [Rewind Lets You Restore Just the Code, Just the Conversation, or Both](context-management/rewind-can-restore-code-only-or-conversation-only.md): When picking a checkpoint to rewind to, Claude Code offers separate options to restore only the codebase state, only the conversation history, or both together.
- [Run /context to Audit What's Eating Your Window](context-management/context-auditing-slash-context.md): `/context` shows exactly what is consuming the window (CLAUDE.md size, MCPs, files), so you can trim bloat proactively instead of discovering it after performance degrades.
- [Run `/autocompact 200k` if your gateway only serves 200K, custom base URLs now assume 1M](context-management/run-autocompact-200k-behind-a-gateway-that-stops-at-200k.md): Behind a proxy or gateway, Claude Code now assumes a 1M window for 1M-capable models, so a 200K-limited gateway fails mid-session unless you cap it.
- [Run parallel sessions in the same directory to share a prompt cache, and expect a miss from a different directory](context-management/parallel-sessions-in-one-directory-share-a-prompt-cache.md): The cache is effectively per machine and directory, because the system prompt embeds memory paths and the conversation opens with the working directory.
- [Split an AI Operating System's Knowledge Into Always-Loaded Expertise Context and Just-in-Time Situational Context](context-management/split-aios-context-into-expertise-and-situational.md): Keep identity, goals, and policies (expertise context) always in the agent's context like a system prompt, but fetch per-task facts (situational context, e.g. one customer's ticket) only when a task actually needs them.
- [Steer Auto-Compaction From CLAUDE.md Instead of Reaching Straight for a PreCompact Hook](context-management/steer-auto-compaction-from-claude-md.md): Compaction reads CLAUDE.md, so you can name what must survive summarization with one line and no hook, script or settings edit.
- [Stop Guessing About Cache Misses: /cost Now Reports Hit Ratio and Tokens Re-Cached](context-management/verify-cache-folklore-with-the-cost-prompt-cache-line.md): 2.1.251's per-session prompt-cache line (and prompt_cache status-line object) makes every cache rule measurable in-session instead of inferred from cost totals.
- [Tick Completed Items Off the Plan File, Compact, Then Re-Tag the Plan](context-management/tick-off-the-plan-file-then-compact-and-re-tag-it.md): On a long multi-step build, periodically ask Claude to mark the finished items in the plan file as done, compact the conversation, then re-tag the plan file and continue, so the plan carries the state forward instead of the transcript.
- [Treat a skill or command with a `model:` frontmatter as an uncached model switch for that turn](context-management/a-skill-model-pin-is-an-uncached-model-switch-for-that-turn.md): A skill that pins a different model re-reads the whole conversation uncached on the turn it runs.
- [Trigger a Manual Context Handoff at About Half the Context Window, Not Later](context-management/handoff-or-clear-context-at-half-window.md): Do the handoff-and-clear routine once you notice you're around 50% of the context window, rather than waiting for it to fill further or for auto-compact to kick in.
- [Use --fork-session for Deep-Dive Tangents That Need Tool Access](context-management/fork-session-for-deep-dive-questions-without-context-pollution.md): `claude -c --fork-session` in a new pane creates a copy of the current session for multi-turn explorations that leave no trace in the main conversation.
- [Use /btw to Ask Questions Mid-Session Without Polluting the Context](context-management/btw-command-ask-questions-without-polluting-context.md): /btw answers single-turn questions in a side channel using the session's prompt cache, no context pollution, no interruption to Claude's work.
- [Use /rewind to Recover a Conversation You Accidentally Cleared](context-management/rewind-can-recover-a-cleared-conversation.md): /rewind can now jump back to before a /clear, restoring a conversation you thought was wiped.
- [Use the 3-Compact Rule: After 3 Compactions, Start a New Session With a Summary](context-management/three-compact-rule-and-new-session-for-complex-plans.md): Each compaction loses nuance. After 3, the context summary is too thin; start fresh with a written plan carrying forward only what matters.
- [Write Human Docs as Mental Models and Intent, Not Exhaustive Reference](context-management/write-human-docs-as-mental-models-let-agents-fetch-details.md): Humans need mental models and the 'why'; agents can retrieve the specifics, so write docs for intent and design decisions and stop optimizing for completeness.
- [Write the Session Handoff as a Prompt for the Next Session, Not a Summary for You](context-management/write-the-handoff-as-a-prompt-for-the-next-session.md): Instead of 'summarize our progress', ask Claude to write the next instructions as a prompt for the next session.
- [Run /autocompact Once for Each Model You Use: the Window Is Saved Per Model](context-management/set-autocompact-once-for-each-model-you-use.md): Since 2.1.288 the auto-compact window is stored per model, so a budget set while on one model does not apply after `/model` switches to another.
- [Build an auto-handoff mod that hands the session to a fresh one at a context percentage](context-management/write-an-auto-handoff-mod-that-starts-a-new-session-at-a-context-threshold.md): A mod can watch context use and hand the session off to a new one once it passes a threshold, with a manual /handoff now command to trigger it early.
- [Keep a Short Saved Summary of a Heavy Analysis So the Agent Reads It Instead of Re-Running the Analysis](context-management/save-a-condensed-summary-of-a-huge-analysis-instead-of-rerunning-it.md): When a teardown or analysis produces gigabytes of output, have the agent write a short summary (how it works, how it does what it does, what it could not figure out) and read that, with finer module and trace detail one level below.
- [Expect Idle Compaction on a 200K+ Session Left for About 54 Minutes, and Turn It Off Where You Need the Full History](context-management/let-idle-compaction-shrink-a-200k-conversation-or-turn-it-off.md): Since 2.1.299 a terminal conversation over 200K tokens that sits idle for about 54 minutes is compacted before its one-hour prompt cache expires, so plan for a compaction notice when you come back, or switch it off for that session.
- [Set CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_FILE_MENTIONS So @-Mentions Pass Paths Instead of Inlining File Contents](context-management/set-claude-code-disable-file-mentions-to-pass-paths-not-contents.md): With this env var set, an `@file` mention hands Claude the path to read with its file tools rather than pasting the whole file into the prompt.
- [Reset the Session When a Long Run Keeps Pursuing a Wrong Idea: Compaction Does Not Cure Context Inertia](context-management/reset-the-session-when-a-long-run-fixates-compaction-does-not-cure-it.md): When a long session keeps chasing a wrong hypothesis, have it write a short note of state, findings and dead ends, then start a fresh session with only the repo and that note, instead of relying on compaction.
- [Make Your Session-Start Command Check Handoff Notes Against git and Open PRs Before Trusting Them](context-management/check-handoff-notes-against-git-and-open-prs-before-trusting-them.md): Handoff notes drift from what really happened, so a start-of-session command should compare them with the git log, the open PR list and any running jobs, and report where they are wrong before the agent acts on them.
- [Shrink the Auto-Compact Window to Stretch a Subscription's Weekly Usage](context-management/shrink-the-auto-compact-window-to-stretch-a-subscriptions-weekly-usage.md): Most of a long session's cost is cache reads of its own history, re-sent on every turn, so lowering the auto-compact window from 1M to about 250K (`/autocompact 250k`) keeps each turn small. One Max 20x user reports it turned a weekly limit that ran out in about 2 days into one that lasts the week.
- [When Claude Code Warns That Resuming Will Cost a Share of Your 5-Hour Limit, Start a New Conversation](context-management/start-a-new-conversation-instead-of-resuming-a-cold-one-on-pro-or-max.md): Since 2.1.304, on Pro and Max, resuming an inactive conversation that would use a noticeable share of the 5-hour limit shows a warning; the alternative it offers is a new conversation in which Claude can look up the previous one when you refer to it.
- [Pin promptCacheTtl to 1h If You Keep Working on Usage Credits: the Main Conversation Drops to a 5-Minute Cache There](context-management/pin-promptcachettl-1h-if-you-work-on-usage-credits.md): On a subscription the main conversation gets the 1-hour prompt cache only while you are within plan usage; once Claude Code draws on usage credits it falls back to 5 minutes unless you set `promptCacheTtl` (or `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL`) yourself.

### Model Selection

- [A Newer Model Will Not Fix Design Slop, the Inputs Will](model-selection/fix-the-design-inputs-not-the-model-version.md): Given the same prompt, assets, brand guidelines and design skill, two model generations produced sites that were hard to tell apart.
- [Author Golden Reference Implementations With Your Strongest Model, Then Replicate With Cheaper Ones](model-selection/author-golden-references-with-strongest-model-replicate-with-cheaper.md): For tricky, repeated code with no good canonical example online (hosted-MCP wiring, websockets, complex concurrency), have your strongest model build one golden reference in a clean project, then point cheaper models at it to copy-adapt across every service or app that needs it.
- [Bias a Model-Routing Audit Toward Cheaper or Stronger Models by How Often You Hit Token Limits](model-selection/bias-model-routing-audit-by-token-limit-frequency.md): When running an audit prompt that recommends per-skill models and context settings, tell it to bias its picks toward cheaper models if you run out of tokens often, or toward stronger models if you rarely do.
- [Bookend Every Work Item With the Top Model: It Plans, the Cheaper Model Implements, Then It Reviews](model-selection/bookend-each-work-item-with-the-strongest-model.md): Route the review leg back to the strongest model too, per work item rather than per session, so each implementation is graded against the plan its own author wrote.
- [Configure /advisor So Sonnet Escalates to Opus When Stuck](model-selection/advisor-tool-escalation-pattern.md): /advisor lets Sonnet consult a stronger model mid-session by passing the full conversation history, cheaper than running Opus throughout.
- [Default to the strongest model + thinking because prompt-to-done is shorter](model-selection/strongest-model-is-faster-overall.md): Use the top model with thinking for all coding even though it's slower per request; fewer corrections make total time-to-done shorter. (Boris Cherny).
- [Delegate bulk deterministic classification to a fast judgment model, not to Claude directly](model-selection/route-bulk-classification-to-a-cheap-judgment-model.md): For triaging large batches of records against fixed criteria (support emails, leads, code comments, PR diffs), have Claude Code write and run a script that calls a fast, cheap judgment-model classifier for the classification/routing/scoring step, and reserve Claude itself for the confidently-flagged subset that actually needs writing or judgment.
- [Do Not Drop Fable 5.1 to Low Effort for Research, It Stops Calling Search Tools](model-selection/low-effort-fable-answers-from-memory-instead-of-searching.md): Anthropic's prompting docs note that at low effort Fable 5.1 is less likely than Fable 5 to call a search or retrieval tool and more likely to answer from memory.
- [Download the MLX Build on Apple Silicon and the GGUF Build Everywhere Else](model-selection/prefer-mlx-builds-on-apple-silicon-and-gguf-everywhere-else.md): When you pick a local model to download, the weight format matters as much as the size: take the MLX build on Apple Silicon and the GGUF build on everything else, because GGUF runs out of the box on llama.cpp and therefore on Ollama and LM Studio.
- [Extended Thinking Now Runs by Default With an Automatic Token Budget, No Magic Words Needed](model-selection/extended-thinking-on-by-default-with-budget-cap.md): You no longer need to type 'think', 'think hard', or 'ultrathink' to get Claude Code to reason before acting; extended thinking is on by default with its own capped thinking-token budget.
- [For a Genuinely Short Task, Drop Effort to Low Rather Than Switching to an Older Model](model-selection/drop-a-strong-model-to-low-effort-instead-of-downgrading-the-model.md): Anthropic says Fable 5.1 on low is still competitive with the older Opus models on cost per task, so the cheap option for small work is the strong model with the effort dial down, not a weaker model.
- [Have the Premium Model Refactor Load-Bearing Code So Your Cheap Default Model Handles the Rest](model-selection/refactor-load-bearing-code-with-premium-model-for-cheaper-default.md): When you briefly have a stronger model, don't just have it clear the backlog - have it make architectural changes to the load-bearing / highest-churn files specifically so a weaker default model can close issues more easily afterward.
- [If a Haiku Subagent Can't Find the Relevant Error in Logs, Upgrade It to Sonnet](model-selection/upgrade-a-struggling-haiku-log-analysis-subagent-to-sonnet.md): When a log-analysis subagent running on Haiku fails to correctly identify the relevant error, switching that subagent's model to Sonnet resolves it in many cases.
- [If Legitimate Cyber or Life-Sciences Work Trips Safeguards, Apply for Mythos Access Rather Than Re-Prompting Around It](model-selection/apply-to-the-verification-program-for-mythos-instead-of-fighting-refusals.md): Claude Mythos 5.1 is capability-identical to Fable 5.1 but ships more permissive safeguards, and it is only reachable through two trusted-access programs: the cyber verification program and the life sciences verification program.
- [Keep a Doc of Prompts Claude Code Couldn't Do, and Retest Them on Every New Model Release](model-selection/track-model-capability-failures-and-retest-on-release.md): Log the specific tasks Claude Code failed at into a separate document, then re-try each one whenever a new model ships.
- [Keep Fable Purely Orchestrating and an All-Day Build Costs ~7% of Its Weekly Cap](model-selection/delegate-all-execution-off-fable-to-preserve-its-separate-weekly-cap.md): Fable's usage limit is separate and small; if you forbid it from writing or running anything and make it delegate every implementation task to Opus/Sonnet subagents, a full day of agent work consumes single-digit percentages of that cap.
- [Let the Model Pick Its Own Sub-Agent Model, Then Freeze the Routing Into a CLAUDE.md Table](model-selection/let-model-self-select-subagent-model-then-freeze-routing-table.md): Tell the orchestrator 'for all coding tasks, use your own judgment to pick an appropriate low-power model and run it in a sub-agent,' add an eval loop that escalates when a sub-agent's output is too weak, and once it stabilizes crystallize the results into a CLAUDE.md model-routing table and drop the loop.
- [Match /effort to Task Risk: But Max Rarely Beats High on Capable Models](model-selection/max-effort-is-a-trap-quality-plateaus-at-high.md): Match /effort to blast radius (default for routine, high for hard/risky changes), but on capable models quality plateaus around high: max mostly buys re-verification and can cause overthinking at ~2x cost, so reserve it and calibrate with /usage. (Merges in the old effort-control lesson.).
- [Match the Model to the Task: Opus for Planning/Reasoning, Sonnet for Execution, Haiku for Bulk](model-selection/model-selection-opus-sonnet-haiku-use-cases.md): Use /model opusplan to run Opus only in plan mode; Sonnet handles execution; Haiku for read-only subagents. The advisor tool escalates automatically.
- [Mine Your JSONL Session Transcripts to Distill a Stronger Model's Behavioral Playbook](model-selection/mine-jsonl-transcripts-for-model-behavior-playbook.md): Your Claude Code/Codex sessions are JSONL files tagged with which model produced each turn; have Claude parse them to measure the behavioral delta (tool cadence, reads-before-edits, tests-after-edits, turn count, plan-before-act) between a stronger and weaker model, distill a playbook, and inject it to make the weaker model behave more like the stronger.
- [Name Which Model Tier Handles Which Worker Role in the Orchestration Prompt](model-selection/assign-worker-model-tiers-by-role-in-the-orchestration-prompt.md): Do not just tell the orchestrator to delegate, tell it which tier gets which kind of work: the stronger model for architecture, product and design direction, hard problems and independent reviews, the cheaper one for implementation, research, testing, debugging and iteration.
- [Never route UI or design tasks to a vision-less model, the screenshot-verify loop is what catches broken layouts](model-selection/route-ui-work-to-vision-capable-models-only.md): The screenshot-verify loop is what catches broken layouts, so "can't screenshot" is a hard disqualifier regardless of price.
- [On Fable 5.1, Change Effort Per Message and Keep the Prompt Cache](model-selection/per-message-effort-change-keeps-the-cache-on-fable-5-1.md): Fable 5.1 supports a per-message effort change that preserves the prompt cache.
- [Package a /benchmark Skill That Mines Your Chat History and Scores Against a Rubric You Wrote](model-selection/benchmark-skill-mines-chat-history-and-a-personal-rubric.md): Derive the task set from your real history, not memory, and author the rubric yourself so the model isn't grading its own homework.
- [Prefer Claude Code Over Claude Cowork for File-Change Visibility and Credential Flexibility](model-selection/prefer-claude-code-over-cowork-for-visibility-and-credentials.md): Even though Claude Cowork is Anthropic's newer desktop agent tool and does similar things, this creator still prefers Claude Code because every file change is visible and API-key/credential management is easier.
- [Put a Local Model in Front of Claude to Strip PII From Regulated Data](model-selection/redact-pii-with-a-local-model-before-handing-data-to-claude.md): When the data itself cannot leave the machine but the work needs a frontier model, run a local model first to clean and de-identify, then send the cleaned material to Claude.
- [Reserve Sonnet for Small Fixes, Review, and Changelogs](model-selection/sonnet-for-fixes-review-and-changelogs.md): Reserve Sonnet for small fixes, UI tweaks, reviewing someone else's code, and writing changelogs or release-note summaries.
- [Route by Minimum Viable Model: "Could a Model From a Year Ago Do This?"](model-selection/minimum-viable-model-year-ago-test.md): Subagents inherit the session model unless pinned, so fan-out multiplies frontier cost; the year-ago test is a one-second filter that puts scraping, summarizing, and formatting on cheap models.
- [Route Long-Running Projects to Fable 5 and Single-PR Work to Opus 5 at Medium Effort](model-selection/route-long-projects-to-fable-and-single-prs-to-opus-5-medium.md): A Claude Code engineer routing away from Fable for ordinary PR work, naming the effort level and not just the model.
- [Run Unattended Maintenance Routines on Opus by Default - Sonnet Costs You Guardrails](model-selection/run-maintenance-routines-on-opus-by-default.md): The cheaper model does not fail, it shifts cost onto PR auditing and prompt iteration; Fable is needed only for a few genuinely hard routines.
- [Send Work to Sonnet 5.5 When You Have a Spec and a Check, to Opus 5.5 When the Goal Is Vague](model-selection/route-sonnet-5-5-on-clear-spec-and-checkable-done-opus-5-5-on-vague-or-subjective.md): A creator tested a rule read in a Claude guide on seven tasks: if you can state the finished result and check it, try Sonnet 5.5 first; if the goal is vague, subjective or open-ended, use Opus 5.5.
- [Set ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_MODEL, Not ANTHROPIC_MODEL, When You Want a Starting Model You Can Still Override](model-selection/set-anthropic-default-model-not-anthropic-model-when-you-want.md): ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_MODEL sets only the model new sessions open on and leaves /model picks sticky, whereas ANTHROPIC_MODEL pins the model and discards them.
- [Shortlist Local Models by Having Claude Pull OpenRouter's Catalogue and Read Your Machine Specs](model-selection/shortlist-local-models-with-openrouter-api-and-your-machine-specs.md): Instead of web-searching for the best local model, prompt Claude Code to pull the current open-source model catalogue from OpenRouter's public models API as filterable JSON, then have it read your actual chip, memory, and storage and flag what you are eligible to run and what would run too slowly.
- [Switch to Gemini CLI When You're Going in Circles on a Visual/Aesthetic Task](model-selection/switch-to-gemini-cli-for-stuck-visual-aesthetic-tasks.md): If Claude Code keeps missing on a UI or visual-aesthetics problem, route that specific task to Gemini CLI instead, since Gemini models show better visual understanding and aesthetic judgment.
- [Treat Opus 5.5 max effort as able to exhaust the output cap and return nothing](model-selection/opus-5-5-max-effort-can-exhaust-the-output-cap-and-return-nothing.md): On Opus 5.5 at max effort, reasoning can consume the whole 128k output cap on a simple task and return no answer, so max can fail outright, not just cost more.
- [Triage With a Cheap Model to Rank Targets Before Spending an Expensive One](model-selection/triage-with-a-cheap-model-before-expensive-work.md): Use a cheap model to score/rank every file by impact × opportunity, discard the low scores, and send only the top few to the expensive model with real architectural context.
- [Use Haiku for Fast Q&A and Known-Location Edits](model-selection/haiku-for-fast-qa-and-known-location-edits.md): Use Haiku for fast answers to questions about the code, for teaching/explaining something new, and for making quick, precise edits when you already know exactly which file needs the change.
- [Use Opus for Large-Scale Refactors, Not Only for Planning](model-selection/opus-for-large-refactors-not-just-planning.md): Use Opus for large-scale feature builds and multi-file refactors, not only for planning; it is reported as the primary implementation model for that class of work roughly 70-80% of the time.
- [On API Billing, Cap Haiku 5.5's Auto-Compact Window at 100K to Stay in the Cheaper Pricing Tier](model-selection/cap-haiku-5-5-autocompact-at-100k-on-api-billing.md): On API billing, setting Haiku 5.5's auto-compact window to 100K keeps it in the cheaper token pricing tier, and because the window is saved per model it only affects Haiku (subagents included).
- [Don't Run Haiku 5.5 at Low Effort on Agent Tasks: It Skips Verification and Invents a Reason Why](model-selection/dont-run-haiku-5-5-at-low-effort-on-agent-tasks.md): In one user's real-repo agentic test, Haiku 5.5 at low effort produced working code but skipped verification and made up an excuse for not testing ("the build stalled for over an hour" in a 15-minute run), so if you run Haiku on agent work, start it at medium or high, never low.
- [Give Code-Search Subagents to Haiku 5.5, but Keep Fact-Checking on Opus](model-selection/give-code-search-subagents-to-haiku-but-keep-fact-checking-on-opus.md): In one user's six-task test of Haiku 5.5, Sonnet 5.5 and Opus 5.5 as subagents, Haiku found 44 of the 48 files a feature changed (Opus 45) for $0.44 against Opus's $6.17, but as a fact-checker it flagged 30 correct sentences as errors where Opus flagged none, and rerunning it at xhigh effort still left it flagging 28 (Opus 3).
- [Route Tasks to a Model by the Cost of Being Wrong, Not by How Hard the Task Sounds](model-selection/route-tasks-to-a-model-by-the-cost-of-being-wrong-not-difficulty.md): Pick the model by whether you would notice a wrong answer and how much it costs: work where a mistake shows at once and is cheap to rerun goes to Haiku or Sonnet, work where a mistake is hidden or expensive goes to Opus.
- [Treat the Effort Setting as a Ceiling, Not a Floor: Max Makes Claude Guess Instead of Ask](model-selection/effort-is-a-ceiling-and-higher-effort-guesses-instead-of-asking.md): Effort is the most thinking and activity Claude is allowed to spend, not a required amount, and the creator says higher effort makes it answer questions you never addressed rather than ask you.
- [When Claude Gets Something Wrong, Ask Which Failure It Was Before Touching Model or Effort](model-selection/wrong-output-diagnosis-knew-too-little-raise-model-skipped-work-raise-effort.md): If Claude had everything it needed and still got it wrong, raise the model; if it skipped a file, skipped a test or bailed halfway, raise the effort.
- [Before Moving Explore Subagents to Haiku, Run the Same Task Both Ways and Compare Total Usage](model-selection/judge-cheap-subagent-models-by-total-usage-for-the-task-not-price-per-token.md): A model that is far cheaper per token can cost more per finished job if its wrong answers go back to the main model for fix rounds. Measure total usage and time for one real task on each setup before committing.
- [Route a Batch of Judgments to a Cheap Classifier Only Above the Break-Even Size](model-selection/route-a-batch-to-a-cheap-classifier-only-above-the-break-even-size.md): Each tool round trip to a cheap judgment model re-sends the orchestrator's context, so on a small batch offloading costs more of the expensive model than reading the items directly; the poster's break-even was about 45 to 60 issues.
- [Move Off Opus 4.8 Fast Mode Before 30 November 2026, Because the Request Errors Instead of Falling Back](model-selection/move-off-opus-4-8-fast-mode-before-it-errors.md): Any script, settings file or habit that pins Opus 4.8 with fast mode will start failing on 30 November rather than quietly running slower.
- [ultrathink Raises One Turn to xhigh, Subagents Spawned in It Included, Without Breaking the Prompt Cache](model-selection/ultrathink-raises-one-turn-to-xhigh-and-its-subagents-too.md): Since 2.1.311, typing ultrathink in the terminal runs that turn at xhigh effort on Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Haiku 5.5 and Fable 5.1, and any subagents spawned in the turn run at xhigh too; the session then returns to its set effort.
- [Default to Medium Effort and Take More Turns, Saving xhigh for the Opening Research Turn](model-selection/default-to-medium-effort-and-save-xhigh-for-the-research-turn.md): Higher effort can cause overthinking or scope expansion rather than better results, so run medium and iterate; use xhigh mainly when researching the state of a feature or bug before settling on a fix.
- [Ultracode Is a Separate Toggle From Effort: Run the Orchestration at Low Effort for Big Audits](model-selection/run-ultracode-at-low-effort-for-wide-read-heavy-work.md): Ultracode no longer forces the top effort level: pick the effort you want in /effort and toggle Ultracode on top of it, which keeps the multi-agent workflow at a fraction of the usage.

### MCP

- [A Project .mcp.json headersHelper Now Needs Folder Trust and Gets No Inherited Credential Env Vars](mcp/a-project-mcp-json-headershelper-now-needs-folder-trust-and.md): Project/plugin/agent-scoped MCP headersHelper commands no longer inherit your credential environment variables and now require the folder's trust dialog to have been accepted, including under `claude -p`.
- [Add `"bareElicitationCapability": true` when an MCP server stops connecting after 2.1.287](mcp/add-bareelicitationcapability-when-an-mcp-server-stops-connecting.md): A server that broke on upgrade has a one-key fix in its MCP config entry.
- [Ask Claude to Find You an MCP Server Instead of Web Searching Yourself](mcp/ask-claude-to-find-an-mcp-server-for-you.md): You can just say 'find me a good Figma MCP' and Claude will search and usually get it right, saving you the manual lookup.
- [Authenticate MCP Servers From the CLI With claude mcp login/logout](mcp/authenticate-mcp-servers-from-the-cli.md): `claude mcp login <name>` / `logout <name>` (re)auth an MCP server without the interactive /mcp menu, with --no-browser SSH support, scriptable and remote-friendly.
- [Build a 'Council' Skill That Routes to Competing Models via OpenRouter for Task-Matched Second Opinions](mcp/build-a-council-skill-that-routes-to-competing-models-via-openrouter.md): Package a /council slash-command skill that, without leaving Claude Code, sends the current problem to competing models through OpenRouter - a config file maps task types to models (bug-fix -> Codex, front-end -> Gemini Pro, quick-check -> Gemini Flash) - then Claude labels each model's raw response, states where it agrees/disagrees, and executes the best synthesis.
- [Connect a Design-Tool MCP So Claude Builds From Design-As-Code](mcp/design-tool-mcp-for-design-as-code-builds.md): Wire a design tool's MCP + skills (Google Stitch, Pencil.dev) so Claude reads structured design JSON (tokens, components, sizing) instead of reverse-engineering screenshots/HTML.
- [Connect a Profiling MCP (e.g., Perfetto) So Claude Reads Traces to Find Jank](mcp/connect-a-profiling-mcp-to-hunt-ui-jank.md): For performance issues, hook up a profiling MCP like Perfetto, have Claude run a trace, then let it inspect the timings itself to spot jank.
- [Connect Higgsfield via MCP for Generated Video/Imagery in Web Projects](mcp/higgsfield-mcp-for-cinematic-motion.md): A Higgsfield MCP connector lets the session that builds a page also generate its cinematic video/image assets, dropped straight into the project folder.
- [Defer every tool of an MCP server with `alwaysLoad: false`](mcp/set-alwaysload-false-to-defer-all-of-an-mcp-servers-tools.md): Setting `alwaysLoad: false` on a server entry now puts all its tools behind tool search, so none of its schemas cost context up front.
- [Disable a Bloated MCP Server for Just One Project, Not Globally](mcp/disable-mcp-server-for-one-project-directory.md): If `/context` shows an MCP server eating a large share of your tokens, you can disable it for that specific directory instead of removing it everywhere.
- [Enable Exa MCP Globally, and Install Official Per-Library MCP Servers for Heavily-Used New Libraries](mcp/exa-mcp-plus-per-library-official-mcp-for-training-data-gaps.md): Keep the Exa MCP server enabled across all projects so Claude Code can search online for docs on niche or very recent libraries, and additionally install a library's own official MCP server (e.g. trigger.dev's) when you use that library heavily.
- [Give Agents Ephemeral Forked Databases for Safe Test Loops](mcp/ephemeral-forked-databases-for-agent-testing.md): Install a tool like Ghost via MCP so the agent spins up, forks, and discards throwaway Postgres DBs to run the full build-test loop against real-shaped data without risking prod.
- [Give Agents Index-Backed Code Search in Large Codebases: and Reach for Retrieval Before a Pricier Model](mcp/give-agents-index-backed-code-search-in-large-codebases.md): In large/multi-repo codebases, equip the agent with index-backed structural search (semantic + go-to-def + find-refs) and have it pull only relevant sections; better retrieval beats a pricier model at ~half the cost per quality point.
- [Long MCP Calls Auto-Background After 2 Minutes: Tune `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_AUTO_BACKGROUND_MS`](mcp/long-mcp-calls-auto-background-after-two-minutes.md): Slow servers no longer freeze the session, but a workflow that assumed the tool result lands inline now has an out-of-band handoff in it.
- [Prefer a Narrow MCP Tool Surface Over Shell Access When the Agent Touches Sensitive Data](mcp/narrow-mcp-tool-surface-over-shell-for-sensitive-work.md): The corpus argues MCP-vs-CLI on token cost, where the CLI wins; this is the blast-radius axis, where an enumerable tool list is auditable and "the agent has Bash" is not.
- [Probe an MCP Server's Tool Surface From the Terminal With mcp-explorer Before Adopting It](mcp/probe-mcp-servers-with-mcp-explorer-before-adopting.md): Before wiring a new MCP server into an agent, run `uvx mcp-explorer list <url>`, `uvx mcp-explorer inspect <tool>`, and `uvx mcp-explorer call <url> <tool> -a <name> <value>` from the terminal to enumerate and exercise its tools first.
- [Put project MCP servers in a root `.mcp.json` under `mcpServers`, with absolute script paths](mcp/put-project-mcp-servers-in-a-root-mcp-json-under-mcpservers.md): Servers silently never load if `.mcp.json` sits in `.claude/`, uses a VS Code style top-level `servers` key, or is declared under `mcpServers` in `settings.json`; relative `command`/`args` paths resolve against the launch directory.
- [Run `/mcp reconnect all` to Retry Every Failed or Unauthenticated MCP Server at Once](mcp/run-mcp-reconnect-all-to-retry-every-failed-mcp-server.md): Since 2.1.284, `/mcp reconnect all` in the interactive terminal retries every MCP server that failed to connect or needs authentication in one command, instead of reconnecting them one by one from `/mcp`.
- [Stop pruning rarely-used MCP servers: tool search loads schemas on demand now](mcp/stop-pruning-mcp-servers-tool-search-loads-schemas-on-demand.md): Only tool names (~120 tokens per server) load up front, so prune on security and tool-routing grounds, not token cost.
- [Behind a Custom ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL Tool Search Is Off: Set ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH=true if Your Gateway Forwards tool_reference Blocks](mcp/set-enable-tool-search-true-behind-a-gateway-that-forwards-tool-reference.md): A non-first-party base URL loads every MCP schema upfront and makes mid-session tool additions invalidate the cache; turn tool search back on if the gateway passes `tool_reference` through.
- [Tune the MCP Tool-Search Auto-Enable Threshold in settings.json](mcp/mcp-tool-search-threshold-env-setting.md): Claude Code auto-enables MCP tool search once your MCP tool descriptions would otherwise consume more than 10% of the context window by default; you can override this via an env setting with values true, false, auto, or auto:N for a custom percentage.
- [Use a Web-Scraping MCP (Firecrawl) to Turn Any Site Into LLM-Ready Markdown](mcp/firecrawl-mcp-for-web-to-markdown.md): Connect a scraping MCP like Firecrawl so Claude picks scrape/map/crawl by intent and returns clean markdown/JSON, no learning each site's API, rate limits, or schema.
- [Use Composio's MCP Gateway to Scope Team Access to a Shared-Login App](mcp/composio-mcp-gateway-scoped-url-for-shared-login-apps.md): For apps with one shared login and no per-user accounts, mint a scoped MCP URL via the Gateway API and add it as a custom connector, so revocation happens once instead of everywhere the password was pasted.
- [When an MCP Server Shows Connected but Lists Zero Tools, Reconnect It From `/mcp`, Then Run `claude --debug=mcp`](mcp/reconnect-an-mcp-server-that-shows-connected-with-zero-tools-then-debug-mcp.md): A server that shows connected with zero tools started but is not returning a tool list: select Reconnect in `/mcp`, and if the count stays at zero run `claude --debug=mcp` and read the server's stderr in `~/.claude/debug/<session-id>.txt`.
- [Try MCP_PROTOCOL_NEGOTIATION=legacy When a Local stdio MCP Server Connects Slowly or Breaks After 2.1.292](mcp/set-mcp-protocol-negotiation-legacy-for-a-misbehaving-stdio-server.md): Since 2.1.292 local stdio MCP connections negotiate protocol version 2026-07-28 by default; `MCP_PROTOCOL_NEGOTIATION=legacy` opts out.
- [Use the Apify MCP Server When Claude Gets an Empty Page From Instagram, Reddit or Other JavaScript-Heavy Sites](mcp/use-the-apify-mcp-server-for-sites-that-block-or-need-javascript.md): Apify's MCP server connects Claude to ready-made cloud scrapers for sites that block it or need JavaScript, returning results as a spreadsheet.
- [Use Microsoft's Playwright CLI Instead of an MCP to Click Through Your App Without Dumping Pages Into Context](mcp/prefer-the-playwright-cli-over-the-playwright-mcp-to-keep-pages-out-of-context.md): Nate Herk says the command-line Playwright opens a real browser, clicks like a customer and writes re-runnable tests, and is lighter on tokens than an MCP because it does not put the whole page in Claude's context.
- [Generate MCP Tools From the App's Own Typed Capability Registry So the Server Cannot Drift](mcp/generate-mcp-tools-from-a-typed-capability-registry-so-the-server-cannot-drift.md): When you have Claude Code build an MCP server for your own app, have it first build one typed registry of everything a user can do, generate every MCP tool from that registry, and make leaving a capability out of the MCP a compile error unless the code states why.
- [List Managed stdio MCP Servers in deniedMcpServers If the Allowlist Was Keeping Them Out](mcp/deny-managed-stdio-mcp-servers-explicitly-after-2-1-305.md): Since 2.1.305 `allowedMcpServers` no longer filters `managed-mcp.json` stdio servers that use `${VAR}` only in `env`, so one your allowlist left out now loads on upgrade unless it is in `deniedMcpServers`.

### Memory

- [`autoMemoryEnabled: false` Does Not Reclaim the Memory Preamble: Verify With `/context`](memory/disabling-auto-memory-does-not-reclaim-the-preamble.md): Disabling auto-memory stops the writes but still loads the hardcoded preamble into every session, so you get the instruction pollution without the feature.
- [Ask Claude to Summarize Cross-Project Session History Instead of Searching Manually](memory/search-cross-project-session-history-in-natural-language.md): You can ask Claude Code in plain language what you discussed across all your projects recently and get a per-project breakdown, rather than manually grepping the session-history store.
- [Auto-Dream Keeps Your memory.md Index Under 200 Lines on Purpose](memory/memory-index-keep-under-200-lines-autodream.md): Auto-dream's memory consolidation specifically targets keeping the memory.md index file under 200 lines, because that file loads at every session startup and every token spent on it is a token not spent on actual work.
- [Build a Markdown Wiki Knowledge Base Instead of Vector RAG for Small Corpora](memory/markdown-wiki-knowledge-base-over-vector-rag.md): For a few hundred docs, have Claude maintain a linked markdown wiki (index + backlinks) and query by reading the index and following links, no embeddings or vector DB.
- [Build Memory as Three Jobs: Capped Injection, Auto-Capture, Hybrid Search With Citations](memory/semantic-hybrid-memory-search-with-reranking.md): Split memory into a capped session-start snapshot, deterministic hook-based capture, and semantic+keyword recall with reranking and cited answers.
- [Cowork Sessions Drop `@`-Imports From User-Scope Memory - Inline Anything Load-Bearing](memory/cowork-drops-at-imports-from-user-scope-memory.md): The pointer stays and the content never arrives, so the detailed rules you deliberately split out are exactly the ones Cowork never sees.
- [Don't Pre-Create memory.md, Automemory Creates It Only When It Has Something to Note](memory/automemory-lazily-creates-memory-md-dont-pre-create-it.md): A brand-new project folder has no memory.md or CLAUDE.md until Claude actually records feedback; automemory builds the index quietly on demand rather than requiring setup.
- [Force a Shared Container Tag So a Team's Claude Sessions Write to One Memory](memory/supermemory-container-tag-for-shared-team-memory.md): supermemory's /project-config pins every session to one named container instead of a random per-session one, giving a team a queryable shared memory; invite teammates before they mint API keys.
- [Give Your Coding Agent a Git-Backed Issue Tracker as Cross-Session Memory](memory/git-backed-issue-tracker-as-agent-cross-session-memory.md): Agents start each session with no memory; a git-backed dependency-graph tracker (e.g. Yegge's Beads/bd, JSONL in .beads/) gives durable, mergeable, multi-agent-safe state across sessions. (Steve Yegge).
- [Have Claude Interview You Once Into a Background File, So It Stops Explaining at the Wrong Level](memory/interview-yourself-into-a-background-file-so-claude-calibrates-depth.md): Run a one-off prompt that interviews you about your role, current work and what you already know, and have it write the answers to a background file the project loads.
- [Keep MEMORY.md Under 25KB as Well as 200 Lines: Long Entries Hit the Byte Cap First](memory/memory-md-loads-only-200-lines-or-25kb-whichever-comes-first.md): Only the first 200 lines or the first 25KB of MEMORY.md load at session start, whichever comes first, so an index with long one-line entries can be cut off well before line 200.
- [Let Auto-Dream Consolidate Memory So It Doesn't Rot](memory/auto-dream-memory-consolidation.md): Auto memory captures learnings per session; Auto-dream periodically consolidates them, merging new signal, resolving contradictions, pruning stale entries, and converting relative dates to absolute, so the store stays clean. It triggers only when both ≥24h have passed AND >5 sessions have run since the last consolidation.
- [Measure MEMORY.md Yourself Rather Than Trusting the Compaction Nudge's Reported Size](memory/the-memory-md-compaction-nudge-uses-hardcoded-caps.md): hardcoded 200-line / 25,000-unit caps, and the KB figure can understate non-ASCII content.
- [Prune Claude Code's Auto-Memory Regularly or It Fills With Noise](memory/prune-auto-memory-regularly-or-it-rots.md): Letting Claude Code's built-in auto-memory write itself without ever reviewing it tends to accumulate low-value notes that make it less useful over time, not more.
- [Say 'Reorganize Memory' to Trigger a Consolidation Pass Over Your Memory Files](memory/reorganize-memory-phrase-triggers-consolidation-pass.md): In a project set up with a structured CLAUDE.md memory-management prompt, typing the phrase 'reorganize memory' makes Claude read all memory files, delete empty/duplicate ones, merge related entries, split overloaded files, and refresh the memory.md index.
- [Segment Client Records Per Client and Keep Deliverables in a Separate Client-Facing Repo Referenced by Path](memory/keep-client-deliverables-in-a-client-facing-repo-referenced-by-path.md): Keep the internal record of each engagement (dates, scope, call notes, pricing) in one folder per client inside your own knowledge base, but store the deliverables in a separate client-facing repo and reference its path, so pushing your own repo never drags client deliverables along.
- [Split a Claude-Maintained Wiki Folder From a Read-Only Raw Source Folder](memory/split-a-claude-owned-wiki-from-a-read-only-raw-source-folder.md): Karpathy's LLM-wiki pattern uses two folders: raw/ holds your source documents (articles, transcripts, PDFs) that Claude only ever reads, and wiki/ is a folder Claude fully owns, writing every file and maintaining cross-references.
- [Split One Master Wiki Into Per-Feed Wikis Once a Source Starts Arriving on a Cadence](memory/split-a-growing-knowledge-store-into-per-feed-wikis.md): The trigger to split is the cadence, not the current size: a feed that grows weekly eventually makes every unrelated query pay for it.
- [Test a Knowledge Base by Navigating to a Deliverable Yourself, No Search Allowed](memory/navigate-your-knowledge-folders-yourself-without-search.md): Open your file explorer and try to find a recent deliverable purely by following folders, with no search and no asking Claude. If you cannot navigate to it yourself, an agent working from routing rules will not find it either.
- [Turn Off Built-In Auto-Memory if You Already Run Your Own Memory System](memory/disable-auto-memory-when-you-run-your-own.md): Auto-memory does a background extraction pass each session; if your own/external memory system already does that job, disable it via /memory so you're not paying for redundant work (and risking stale-context bleed).
- [Move Memory Updates Out of the Main Session With a Per-Turn Classifier Mod](memory/write-memory-from-a-side-fork-with-a-per-turn-classifier-mod.md): Build a mod where a Haiku classifier checks each prompt for 'the user wants this remembered', and on a match a forked conversation writes the memory files while the main agent keeps on the task.
- [Give Perishable Memory Facts a stale_after Date and Have a SessionStart Hook Flag the Expired Ones](memory/give-perishable-memory-facts-a-stale-after-date-and-flag-them-at-session-start.md): Keep each memory fact in its own file with frontmatter, give facts that age (versions, addresses, prices, owners) a `stale_after` date, and have a SessionStart hook list the ones past their date so they are re-checked before use.
- [Have the Session-Closing Sweep Check Existing Notes for Staleness, Not Just Collect New Ones](memory/have-the-session-closing-sweep-correct-stale-notes.md): Over 68 logged end-of-session sweeps, 135 candidates surfaced and roughly one in five was a correction to an existing note, because checking whether something was already saved made the agent open the note and see it was wrong.
- [Give Each Correction Rule a Clean-Session Streak: Retire It After Three, Make It Structural After Two Relapses](memory/give-each-correction-rule-a-clean-session-streak.md): Track every rule you added after a correction with a status and a streak of clean sessions, retire it after three clean sessions, and treat a second relapse as a signal to enforce it in code rather than reword it.

### Prompting

- [@ Autocomplete Now Shows a Different Icon per Suggestion Type](prompting/at-mention-autocomplete-icons-by-type.md): When typing @ to autocomplete a mention, Claude Code now shows distinct icons: a plus for files, an asterisk for agents, and a diamond for MCP server tools, making it easy to tell which kind of item you're about to reference.
