- [Add a short explicit instruction not to game the task, because it measurably cuts reward hacking](prompting/add-a-one-line-instruction-not-to-game-the-task.md): A one-line instruction forbidding cheating on impossible or hard tasks reduced reward hacking by about two thirds in an Anthropic system card result.
- [Annotate the Image in the Desktop App Instead of Describing Where to Move Things](prompting/annotate-the-image-instead-of-describing-the-change.md): For a change that is easier to point at than to say ('move this up here'), draw on the screenshot in the desktop app and prompt against the mark: 'remove the red circle'.
- [Ask Claude for the absolute path of a file it built and paste it into the browser](prompting/ask-claude-for-the-absolute-path-to-open-what-it-built-in-the-browser.md): For an HTML slideshow, dashboard or page, ask Claude to open it in Chrome or give the absolute path, then paste that path into the address bar.
- [Ask Claude to Generate a Fact Verification Table for Any Claims It Makes](prompting/fact-verification-table-for-claude-claims.md): When Claude states facts in research/analysis, ask it to generate a verification table with the claim, source, and confidence level.
- [Ask for Named Parallax Planes Moving at Different Scroll Rates, Not for 'Depth'](prompting/ask-for-named-parallax-planes-to-get-depth.md): Spell out the layers you want and what is on each one (far background, mid background, subject, foreground), and say they move at different rates as the page scrolls.
- [Ask for Probability-Ranked Variants to Escape Safe, Repetitive Output](prompting/sample-probability-ranked-variants-to-escape-mode-collapse.md): Append "generate five responses with their corresponding probabilities" to pull creative asks from the distribution tails instead of the bland aligned average.
- [Ask for the Destination App's Native Format Before Copying Output to Clipboard](prompting/request-platform-native-format-before-clipboard-paste.md): When you're going to paste Claude's output into an app that doesn't render markdown (e.g. LinkedIn), ask Claude to write in that platform's native format (no markdown headers, emoji bullets instead) before you copy it, rather than letting the markdown get mangled on paste.
- [Ask the Agent for "Irrefutable Proof" Instead of Verifying Locally](prompting/ask-the-agent-for-irrefutable-proof-instead-of-verifying.md): When the agent has a sandbox it can install into freely, the two words "irrefutable proof" are the prompt lever that makes it produce the evidence itself, which is what removes the need for your own local dev setup.
- [Ban React in artifacts so the code copies straight out to static hosting](prompting/ban-react-in-artifacts-so-the-code-copies-straight-out-to.md): Put a standing "no React, plain HTML/vanilla JS/CSS with minimal dependencies" instruction in the project you use for building artifacts, so every prototype is a file you can paste onto static hosting unchanged.
- [Brief /design Like a Client: Who It Is For, the One Job of the Page, and How Many Sections](prompting/state-the-pages-one-job-and-section-count-in-the-design-brief.md): A /design brief that names the audience, the single action the page must produce, and an explicit section count returns structured concepts instead of guesses.
- [Brief website design by the visitor's emotional journey, not a feature list](prompting/brief-design-prompts-with-the-visitors-emotional-journey.md): Scroll order, one belief statement, calm vs intense zones, and a signature move, then have Claude evaluate the draft from the visitor's seat.
- [Build a Word-Remapping Glossary in Your Voice-to-Text Tool for Terms It Keeps Mishearing](prompting/voice-to-text-word-remapping-glossary.md): If your voice-to-text tool consistently mishears a specific term you use often (product names, technical vocabulary), add a word-mapping entry in the tool itself so what reaches Claude matches what you actually said.
- [Check a Prompt by Asking Whether a Colleague With Zero Context Could Run It](prompting/colleague-with-zero-context-test-for-prompts.md): Anthropic's check from its best-practices guide: show the prompt to a colleague who knows nothing about the task, and if they would be confused, Claude will be too.
- [Constrain Side Effects With an Explicit Assessment-Only Rule](prompting/assessment-only-mode-for-diagnostic-tasks.md): For questions/diagnostics, name the forbidden actions ("report what you find and stop; don't fix, send, edit, or delete until I say go") so it doesn't silently take action.
- [Delete 'Double-Check' and 'Use a Subagent to Verify' Lines From Prompts Written for Older Models](prompting/delete-double-check-and-verify-again-instructions-from-prompts.md): Claude 5 models already check their own work, so a standing double-check or verify-with-a-subagent instruction buys a second pass over work that was already verified, at full cost.
- [Delete 'No Bullets, No Tables' Style Bans: Fable 5.1's System Prompt Already Trims Them](prompting/delete-formatting-bans-the-system-prompt-already-trims-them.md): Anthropic put instructions to use fewer bullets, less bold and fewer tables into Fable 5.1's own system prompt, so your negative formatting rules are surplus tokens that can fight the system prompt instead of helping.
- [Delete Examples and "Do Not Do X" Lists From Your CLAUDE.md: Frontier Models Are Hurt by Both](prompting/delete-examples-and-negative-constraints-from-claude-md.md): Examples cap the model below its own creativity, and hard negatives are a strong enough impulse to override your own later, contradicting instruction.
- [End Every 'Explain This' Prompt With 'In Terms of the Project I'm Already Working On'](prompting/climb-a-three-rung-explanation-ladder-ending-in-my-project.md): When learning an unfamiliar concept inside a session, ask for it at three escalating rungs: explain it like I'm 5, 12 or 18; then give me a level-two analysis of why it matters here; then explain it in terms of what I am already working on and already understand.
- [Explicitly Tell Claude to Slow Down and Work Step-by-Step](prompting/slow-down-instruction-one-step-at-a-time.md): Add "Go one step at a time, don't expect everything to work first try" to your context file or initial prompt.
- [Fix Design Bias by Starting in an Empty Folder](prompting/fix-code-bias-with-empty-folder-isolation.md): When existing code anchors Claude to poor patterns, start Claude in an empty folder to generate clean solutions, then port back.
- [For a Large Build, Start Minimal and Ask Claude to Interview You With AskUserQuestion](prompting/ask-claude-to-interview-you-with-askuserquestion-before-a-large-build.md): When you cannot state what done looks like, do not write a better prompt: give a minimal one and ask Claude to interview you using the AskUserQuestion tool, then let the answers become the brief.
- [Give agents a fixed PR template so the summary never outgrows the diff](prompting/give-pr-writing-agents-a-template-to-cap-summary-length.md): Left unguided, agents write PR summaries and test plans as long narrative prose.
- [Give Narrow Tasks an Explicit Definition of Done, Including Output Length](prompting/write-a-definition-of-done-with-a-length-cap-for-narrow-tasks.md): Claude 5 models expand scope by default, so for a narrow task state what done looks like and how long the output should be, in the prompt itself.
- [Have Claude Interview You With a 10-20 Question Brand-Voice Quiz for a Fast Starting Profile](prompting/brand-voice-quiz-for-fast-voice-profile.md): The fastest way to bootstrap a brand-voice profile from zero is to have Claude ask you 10-20 questions about how you talk and what you'd never say, rather than starting from scraped examples.
- [Have Your Alignment Skill Ask Questions in Batches, Not Round by Round](prompting/batch-alignment-questions-instead-of-round-by-round.md): Round-by-round questioning makes you the latency bottleneck; emitting 2-10 questions at once is speculative decoding applied to the human in the loop.
- [Interrupt Claude the Moment It Says 'I'm Not Really Sure'](prompting/interrupt-claude-when-it-hedges-mid-response.md): Treat hedging language or visible uncertainty in Claude's thinking/output as a trigger to interrupt (Escape) and course-correct rather than letting it guess.
- [Learn the ~40 Technical Terms, Not the Code: One Right Word Fixes Claude's Output](prompting/learn-technical-vocabulary-for-precise-prompts.md): Claude is trained on precise terms, so "debounce the search input" or "use a websocket" beats describing behavior in prose; a few dozen terms cover most requests.
- [Make a long one-shot build previewable from the first commit](prompting/make-a-long-one-shot-build-previewable-from-the-first-commit.md): When you set an agent loose on a long unattended build, instruct it to land a runnable index.html on the branch first and to "commit and push as often as possible", so a GitHub Pages preview shows progress mid-run instead of only at the end.
- [Make Every Autonomous PR Carry Its Own Proof - Require `/verify`, a Repro, and a Truth Table](prompting/make-every-autonomous-pr-carry-its-own-proof.md): The load-bearing clause of the prompt behind 180 merged PRs is the evidence requirement, not the task description, so spend the specificity budget there.
- [Name GSAP Explicitly When Asking Claude Code to Animate a Claude Design Handoff](prompting/name-gsap-for-claude-code-scroll-animations.md): When handing a Claude Design export to Claude Code, naming the GSAP animation library in the build prompt gets scroll-triggered entrance animations, floating buttons, and moving stat counters layered onto the static export automatically.
- [Open every session on an existing project with "First run the tests"](prompting/open-every-session-on-an-existing-project-with-first-run-the.md): Begin any session against an existing codebase with the four-word prompt "First run the tests" so the agent discovers the suite, learns the invocation, and stays in a testing mindset for the rest of the session.
- [Paste a Screenshot Instead of Describing a UI Problem](prompting/screenshot-paste-instead-of-describing-ui-problems.md): When a layout bug is hard to word, paste the screenshot; Claude reads the image and locates the exact problem more accurately than prose.
- [Place long reference documents above your question, not after it](prompting/put-long-reference-documents-before-the-question.md): For long-context prompts (roughly 20k tokens or more: a transcript, a spec, a spreadsheet pasted into a session), put the document near the top and your question and instructions at the bottom.
- [Point the agent at a reference codebase by cloning it to /tmp](prompting/clone-reference-codebase-to-tmp.md): Tell Claude Code to git clone an existing repo into /tmp and study it as the reference, instead of explaining a complex pattern in prose. (Simon Willison).
- [Put an Evidence Clause in Long-Running Prompts: Audit Each Claim Against a Tool Result](prompting/evidence-clause-in-long-running-prompts.md): Anthropic's Fable prompting docs give the wording: before reporting progress, audit each claim against a tool result from the session, report only work you can point to evidence for, and say explicitly when something is not yet verified.
- [Replace persona preambles with where-to-look, done criteria, and a self-check](prompting/replace-persona-preambles-with-pointers-done-criteria-and-a-self-check.md): 162 personas across ~2,500 prompts moved nothing; spend the words on context pointers, a definition of done, and "before you finish, verify against X".
- [Request Post-Tool Summaries to Turn Claude Into a Teacher](prompting/request-post-tool-summaries-to-learn-from-claude.md): Newer models are terse; a system-prompt rule to summarize what it did and why after tool use restores the learnable rationale they otherwise skip.
- [Restyle an existing site by pinning copy and brand and freeing only the presentation](prompting/restyle-an-existing-site-pinning-copy-and-brand.md): Keep copy and branding verbatim, transform only structure, styling, and interaction; anything else that changed is a bug.
- [Say "in an appropriate subdirectory" instead of naming the path yourself](prompting/say-in-an-appropriate-subdirectory-instead-of-naming-the-path.md): When you know a repo has a convention for where a kind of file lives but cannot remember what it is, deliberately underspecify the location and let the agent read the convention out of the tree.
- [Screenshot Designs from Mobbin and Constrain Claude to Color Scheme + Font Only](prompting/steal-designs-from-mobbin-with-color-constraint.md): Take a screenshot of a design you like, tell Claude 'use only the color scheme and font from this image', prevents wholesale copying.
- [Sharpen the Spec Before Reaching for a Higher Effort Level](prompting/sharpen-the-spec-before-reaching-for-more-effort.md): Reasoning effort is a reflection budget, not an intelligence dial; when output is weak, tighten the prompt/spec first, a good spec lifts low-effort output to near-max far more cheaply than cranking effort.
- [Size UI Screenshots So Surrounding Text Is Visible, Not Just the Broken Element](prompting/right-size-screenshots-so-claude-can-locate-the-code.md): A screenshot that's too tightly cropped gives Claude Code nothing to text-match against; one that's too large gives no clear focal point. Pair a full-context screenshot with a smaller cropped zoom of the exact element when referencing competitor UIs.
- [Soften Absolute Rules in CLAUDE.md Until They're True 100% of the Time](prompting/soften-absolute-rules-until-they-are-true-every-time.md): The Claude Code team dropped its own "always verify front-end changes" line for a conditional: you hand the model the prompt on 100% of turns, so a rule that's right 80% of the time misfires on the rest.
- [State the Breadth of a Change Explicitly to Control Scope Creep](prompting/state-change-breadth-explicitly-maximal-or-minimal.md): Say whether you want a maximal build-out or a minimal surgical change; unspecified, Claude either under-delivers or over-engineers and breaks working code.
- [Stop Hand-Tuning Prompts: Search for Them Against an Eval Metric](prompting/search-prompts-against-evals-not-hand-tuning.md): Once you can score prompt candidates with evals, treat the prompt as something to search for (DSPy/GEPA), not hand-craft, it's maintainable and lets you swap models freely.
- [Tell a Long Autonomous Run to Keep Going Until It Has Objective Evidence, Not a Plausible Implementation](prompting/demand-objective-evidence-not-a-plausible-implementation.md): In the goal prompt, write the definition of done and forbid stopping at something that merely looks built: keep orchestrating until there is objective evidence the complete product works.
- [Tell Astra to Take a Bias Toward Action, Claude 5 Needs the Opposite Constraint](prompting/tell-astra-to-make-reasonable-assumptions-claude-needs-the-opposite.md): GPT-6 Astra stops to ask rather than assuming, so state that you want a bias toward action and reasonable assumptions.
- [Tell Claude to Write Its Final Message as a Re-Grounding, Not a Continuation](prompting/tell-claude-to-re-ground-its-final-message-after-unattended-runs.md): Shorthand that is good between tool calls leaks into the one message a user reads cold after an overnight run; instruct it to lead with the outcome and drop invented vocabulary.
- [Tell the Agent the Final Deployment URL Up Front](prompting/tell-the-agent-the-final-deployment-url-up-front.md): Give the agent the exact production URL at the start so it bakes correct paths/links into the build instead of needing post-deploy fixes.
- [Tell the Model to Act Once It Has Enough Context](prompting/instruct-model-to-act-once-context-is-sufficient.md): Replace "research everything and plan first" with "when you have enough information to act, then act" so high-effort runs stop burning minutes over-planning.
- [Treat Claude Like a Junior Developer With ADHD: Specific Tasks, Tight Context](prompting/treat-ai-junior-dev-specific-tight-context.md): Give one specific task at a time with tight context constraints; Claude will guess confidently and take you to error hell if you don't.
- [Use a Cautious Multi-Hypothesis Prompt for Diagnosis and Debugging](prompting/multi-hypothesis-prompt-for-diagnosis.md): For "why did X happen," force multiple hypotheses, if/then reasoning, and what-evidence-would-confirm-or-falsify so it doesn't lock onto one confident false cause.
- [Use Directive Prompts, Not Aggressive Ones, Include Your Reasoning](prompting/directive-not-aggressive-prompting.md): Anthropic's internal guide: be explicit about scope, include the 'why' behind rules, avoid aggressive tone, and never use 'think' when thinking mode is off.
- [Use the Spec Developer Workflow: Have Claude Ask Clarifying Questions Before Starting](prompting/spec-developer-workflow-with-clarifying-questions.md): Tell Claude 'you are a spec developer; ask me questions to write a complete spec before writing any code.'.
- [Use Voice Dictation for Prompts, They Come Out Longer and More Context-Rich](prompting/voice-dictation-for-longer-richer-prompts.md): Typed prompts are constrained by typing speed; voice prompts are longer, more specific, and give Claude better instructions.
- [When the Agent Can't Find Data It Has, Make It Retrace Its Own Search Before You Fix Anything](prompting/make-the-agent-retrace-a-failed-search-then-fix-the-routing.md): "Never do that again" has nothing to attach to on a retrieval-path failure; making it replay where it looked and where it should have hands you the actual routing defect.
- [When Unsure of Impact, Ask for a Few Options Before Any Edits](prompting/ask-for-options-before-changes-to-gauge-blast-radius.md): For a change whose scope you can't predict, prompt 'give me a few options before making changes', a lightweight, read-only probe that surfaces the blast radius before the agent touches files.
- [Write Spec Acceptance Criteria as When-This-Happens-Then-That Pairs, They Are What Judges a Long Unattended Build](prompting/write-acceptance-criteria-as-when-then-pairs-to-judge-an-unattended-build.md): Per-behaviour criteria in when-then form are the only thing that lets you decide whether a fifteen-hour unattended run actually delivered, so write them before the run rather than reconstructing a standard afterwards.
- [Prompt With Three Things: What You Want, How Much Effort to Spend, and How to Verify It](prompting/prompt-with-the-goal-the-effort-and-how-to-verify.md): Boris Cherny: drop heavy scaffolding and tell Claude the goal, the effort you want it to spend, and how it should verify it did the right thing.
- [Require an exact quote and source for every rule you distil from a person's material into an agent](prompting/require-an-exact-quote-and-source-for-every-rule-extracted-into-an-agent.md): When Nate built a teaching agent from Karpathy's writing, each rule had to link to an exact quote and where it came from, and the agent must say when it is inferring.
- [After a Big Change, Ask 'How Can I Manually Verify This?' and Get a Personal Verification Plan](prompting/ask-the-agent-how-you-can-manually-verify-a-big-change.md): Austin says most people still verify by hand but neglect to have the AI help with that step, so he asks it for a manual verification plan.
- [Ask for the Verification Method as Part of the Plan on Technical Tasks](prompting/put-tell-me-how-you-will-verify-into-the-plan-step.md): Austin tells the agent 'Tell me how you'll verify the output as part of the plan' so it knows the target finished product.
- [Brief a Decision Panel With the Decision, 2-3 Options, Real Numbers, Limits, a Definition of Success and Your Current Lean](prompting/brief-a-decision-panel-with-options-numbers-limits-success-and-your-lean.md): Herk's checklist for a council question: the decision itself, at least two or three options, real figures, hard limits, what success looks like, and which way you currently lean and why.
- [Write a /goal Condition That Can Be Proved From the Conversation, Because the Checker Reads Only the Transcript](prompting/write-goal-conditions-whose-proof-appears-in-the-transcript.md): After each turn a small model judges whether the /goal condition is met, and it judges from the conversation only.
- [Reject Vague Prompts With a Haiku 'Prompt Bouncer' Mod Before They Cost a Full Turn](prompting/prompt-bouncer-mod-rejects-vague-prompts-before-the-main-model-runs.md): A mod can send each prompt to Haiku first and bounce it back with a reason if it is too unclear (for example 'fix the thing on the settings page'), and can use a project glossary to say which term to use.
- [Have the Agent Tag Every Finding From a Teardown as Seen, Inferred or Unknown](prompting/tag-reverse-engineering-findings-as-seen-inferred-or-unknown.md): When an agent studies an existing app to rebuild a feature, make it label each finding: seen (found and cited), inferred (the evidence points that way, a guess) or unknown (could not be observed), so you do not build on guesses as if they were facts.
- [Rewrite a Prompt to Go Deeper, Not Bigger: Ask What a Top Professional Would Do and Which Tools They Use](prompting/ask-for-a-deeper-version-of-a-prompt-using-the-professionals-method-and-tools.md): Ask Claude to name what a top professional would do that you are not asking for, the rawest starting material, the methods and tools, and what would independently show the result is right, then rewrite your prompt.
- [Ask for a Target Several Times Beyond Reasonable and Push Back When Claude Says It Is Impossible](prompting/set-an-unreasonable-target-and-tell-claude-it-is-possible-when-it-says-impossible.md): If you want a 5 second load, ask for 200 ms, and answer an 'impossible' with 'it is possible, keep going'.
- [Turn the Task Noun Into a Verb, Then Ask Claude Which Jobs Already Solve That Problem](prompting/swap-a-noun-for-a-verb-and-ask-claude-which-professions-already-solve-your-problem.md): Describe what the artifact must do, not what it is, and ask Claude to interview you and name professions that face a problem of that shape.
- [Have Claude Diagnose What Users Do With a Requested Feature Before Building It](prompting/diagnose-the-symptoms-behind-a-feature-request-before-building-it.md): For a request like CSV export, ask Claude to find the underlying symptom, as a doctor would, instead of building the button.
- [Reskin an Existing App From a Working HTML Reference, With the Extracted Tokens Written as CSS Variables First](prompting/reskin-an-app-from-a-working-html-reference-with-tokens-as-css-variables-first.md): To change how a working app looks, give Claude Code a single-file HTML page as the visual source of truth, have it write the extracted design tokens out as CSS variables for you to review, and only then apply them across the project while explicitly keeping content, routes and logic.
- [Hand Claude the Goal Plus Your Idea and Ask for Options, Not the Solution as an Instruction](prompting/give-claude-the-goal-and-your-idea-and-ask-for-options.md): Say what you want to improve, offer your idea as one example, and ask what to do; a solution-shaped prompt caps the result at your answer.
- [Skip Plan Mode on Opus 5.5 and Put Four Lines in the First Message: Done, Check, Out of Scope, Guardrails](prompting/skip-plan-mode-put-done-check-scope-guardrails-in-first-message.md): Plan mode mostly surfaced the assumptions Claude guessed from a thin brief. Instead, state what plan mode would have exposed up front: what done looks like, how it will be checked, what is out of scope, and what Claude must not touch.
- [Give Claude Design References as Single Sections From Several Sites, Not Five Whole Websites](prompting/give-claude-individual-sections-from-several-sites-as-design-references-not-whole-sites.md): With no direction, Claude builds a safe generic site (centred layout, default font, emojis). Hand it three to five saved reference links, each a specific section such as a hero or a pricing block, so it can mix ideas into something new.
- [Name Your Auth, Email, Storage and Job Providers in CLAUDE.md, or Claude Code Hand-Rolls Them](prompting/name-your-auth-email-storage-and-job-providers-in-claude-md.md): In the poster's 118 headless runs on blank Next.js and FastAPI repos with Sonnet 5.5, Claude Code picked Stripe and Sentry every time but wrote its own auth, caching, email sending and background jobs unless the prompt named a provider.
- [Cap an open-ended 'make it better' loop and review its milestones, because forced changes turn into bloat](prompting/cap-force-ship-improvement-loops-and-pick-a-stop-round.md): A repeated 'make it better' prompt only keeps going if you force a shipped change every round, but late rounds add clutter and break earlier features.
- [Generate far more candidates than you need, then filter in two passes: hard constraints first, then a judge against a rival bar](prompting/overgenerate-then-filter-in-two-stages-mechanical-then-judge-against-a-rival-bar.md): Ask for a wide batch (Jono generates 150 ads), run a mechanical constraint pass, then have Claude judge survivors against your competitors' best and reject any that would not beat the competitors nine times out of ten.
- [Upload a reference HTML file so Claude builds a dashboard with the same layout](prompting/upload-a-reference-html-file-so-claude-builds-the-dashboard-to-match.md): To get a repeatable dashboard look, hand Claude an example HTML file and build on it instead of describing the layout in words.
- [Tell Skills and CLAUDE.md to Write in ASD-STE100 to Stop Verbose, Cryptic Output](prompting/name-asd-ste100-as-the-output-style-in-skills-and-claude-md.md): Naming ASD-STE100 (Simplified Technical English, the aerospace maintenance-manual writing standard) as the output format in skills and CLAUDE.md gives the model a concrete style target instead of "be concise".

### Automation

- [/chrome Can Bypass Many Captchas and Bot-Detection Checks, and You Can Jump In to Help It](automation/chrome-tool-bypasses-captchas-and-you-can-assist-it-mid-task.md): The /chrome browser tool can get past a lot of captchas and other anti-bot challenges on its own, and when it can't, you can manually assist it mid-task rather than starting over.
- [`--max-budget-usd` Is Now a Real Hard Stop for Background Subagents, Not Just the Main Loop](automation/max-budget-usd-now-halts-background-subagents.md): Before 2.1.217 the cap didn't guard the most expensive part of an unattended fan-out; if you tried it then and concluded the flag was useless, re-test.
- [A $200/Month Claude Code Plan Yields Roughly $2,000 of API-Equivalent Usage for Headless Batch Work](automation/subscription-headless-batch-work-is-roughly-10x-cheaper-than-metered-api.md): Running headless Claude Code instances through your Anthropic subscription for bulk background tasks (receipt validation, hourly research skills, etc.) costs far less than the same volume of work billed as metered API usage, roughly a 10x difference by the speaker's estimate.
- [Add Lint, Type-Check, and Test Runs to Every Ralph Loop Task's Acceptance Criteria](automation/ralph-loop-require-lint-typecheck-test-in-acceptance-criteria.md): By default a Ralph loop task can be marked done just because the files exist; explicitly require it to run linting, type-checking, and tests before accepting a task as complete, or it will happily mark broken code as finished.
- [Bound Autonomous PR Monitoring to a Fixed Window and Act Only on Comment or Merge](automation/bound-pr-monitoring-window.md): After an agent opens a PR, tell it to watch the PR for a fixed window (e.g. 6 hours) with the Monitor tool, fixing any left comments and ending the session once it's merged or the window expires.
- [Claude in Chrome Reads Console Logs to Self-Debug](automation/claude-in-chrome-reads-console-logs-to-self-debug.md): When Claude Code drives Claude in Chrome, it can read the browser's console logs, so it notices and self-debugs a JavaScript error it just caused while clicking around the page.
- [Cloud Scheduled Tasks / Cowork Run Sandboxed: Reach Data via Connectors, Not Local Files or Pasted Keys](automation/wire-cloud-scheduled-tasks-through-connectors-not-local-files-or-keys.md): The no-setup cloud scheduled-tasks/Cowork tier runs in a destroyed-after-run sandbox with no local files/CLIs/secrets and won't act on a pasted API key; wire apps via connectors (or Composio/self-hosted MCP) and put files in Google Drive.
- [Drive Playwright From Claude for Self-Healing Browser Automation and Authenticated Scraping](automation/drive-playwright-for-browser-automation-and-authed-scraping.md): Give Claude Playwright to run real browser flows, fill forms, screenshot, self-correct on UI changes; launch with your existing Chrome profile to reuse logged-in sessions instead of re-authing.
- [Drop Session-Restart Workarounds for the 200-Subagent Cap, and Bound Spend Explicitly Instead](automation/subagent-spawn-cap-of-200-per-session-removed.md): The per-session spawn cap is gone, which also removes a brake on runaway fan-out that some setups were leaning on without knowing it.
- [Encode the Patterns Agents Get Wrong as Lint Rules That Fail the PR: With the Fix in the Error Message](automation/encode-agent-mistakes-as-lint-rules-with-the-fix-in-the-message.md): Turn the shortcuts/anti-patterns and architectural rules agents violate into custom lint/Semgrep rules that fail the PR, and write each error message to state the exact fix so the agent self-corrects in one pass.
- [Exempt Scheduled Tasks From Your Report-and-Stop Rule](automation/exempt-scheduled-tasks-from-the-report-and-stop-rule.md): The assessment-only rule that stops Claude taking unrequested actions is the wrong default for scheduled runs: Anthropic gives those the opposite instruction, to operate autonomously and proceed on reversible actions.
- [Forward Subagent Text and Thinking Into Headless stream-json With --forward-subagent-text](automation/forward-subagent-text-in-headless-stream-json.md): Subagent text/thinking are omitted from stream-json by default; pass --forward-subagent-text (or CLAUDE_CODE_FORWARD_SUBAGENT_TEXT) to get real-time observability into headless fan-outs.
- [Gate Agent-Authored PRs on a Test That Fails Without the Patch and Passes With It](automation/gate-agent-prs-on-a-test-that-fails-without-the-patch.md): Bun requires a red/green test pair before an agent may open a PR and auto-rejects test-less PRs, converting "the agent says it's fixed" into machine-checkable proof.
- [Give a Ralph Loop a Single Literal Completion String as Its Only Exit Condition](automation/ralph-loop-literal-completion-string-for-exit.md): A Ralph loop should only be allowed to stop when it prints one specific, literal word (e.g. 'complete'), checked by the outer wrapper after it re-verifies every task's acceptance criteria; anything else sends it back to the start with a fresh context window.
- [Guard Unattended Agent Runs With a Stall Watchdog and Bounded Retry](automation/guard-unattended-agent-runs-with-a-stall-watchdog-and-bounded-retry.md): Wrap headless/scheduled agent runs with a no-event stall timeout that kills and retries the worker, cap retries with backoff, and bound concurrency, in Claude Code use CLAUDE_CODE_RETRY_WATCHDOG (MAX_RETRIES now caps at 15).
- [Hand Computer Use a Product's Setup Docs and Let It Click Through the Integration Wizard](automation/point-computer-use-at-a-product-s-docs-to-self-configure-it.md): Multi-step connect-your-sources onboarding is documented, repetitive and low-judgment; it stops for approval at each new connection scope by default.
- [Have Claude generate a labels reference file with IDs, descriptions and example emails for a triage routine](automation/generate-a-labels-reference-file-with-ids-descriptions-and-examples-for-a-triage-routine.md): Ask Claude to pull your real Gmail labels into a markdown file with name, label ID, description and example emails, and point the routine at it.
- [Have Claude Write the Routine's Prompt Before You Schedule It](automation/routines-with-claude-generated-prompts.md): Ask Claude in a working session to draft the routine prompt (it covers inputs, edge cases, and output format you'd forget), test it once, then schedule it.
- [Have the Dead-Code Routine Log Suspected Code First and Delete It the Next Day](automation/prove-suspected-dead-code-with-a-logging-canary-before-deleting.md): Statically unreachable code can go immediately, but code the agent only suspects is dead should get a log line on one run and be deleted on the next if nothing fired, instead of being deleted on a guess.
- [Make Automation Receipts Report Exact Counts, Not Vague Confirmations](automation/explicit-numeric-receipts-for-automations.md): End every automated run with an explicit numeric receipt ("141 emails reviewed"), never a vague "done", the number is a cheap tripwire that flags when the job did too little or too much.
- [Make Every Automation Check Its Connections First and Abort With the Fix](automation/preflight-check-connections-and-abort-with-a-fix-message.md): Open each automation skill with a pre-flight check that every connector, key and path it needs is live.
- [Make Reporting Its Own Skill and Attach It to Every Routine](automation/make-the-status-report-its-own-skill-and-attach-it-to-every-routine.md): Instead of writing reporting instructions into each scheduled routine, build one small skill (for example send-slack-update) and add it to the end of every routine so all autonomous work lands in a single channel you actually check.
- [Monitor Watches Now Auto-Expire at ~30 Minutes and Can Drop Events in the Re-Arm Gap](automation/monitor-watches-expire-at-30-minutes-and-can-drop-events.md): The Monitor tool's `persistent: true` option (no-timeout background watching) was replaced with a hard ~30-minute deadline that requires Claude to notice the expiry and re-arm; GitHub issue #94393 shows the re-arm has a gap where events from an external process can be lost entirely, not just delayed.
- [Never Automate a Process You Haven't First Proven as a Hand-Run Skill](automation/prove-a-process-as-a-manual-skill-before-automating-it.md): Before reaching for AI on any inefficiency, run the delete-first order (question the requirement, delete it if you can, then simplify), and only after a surviving process has been done repeatedly as a Claude skill you actively use should you promote it to an automation, gating the 'should I build this automation?' decision behind a dedicated sign-off skill.
- [Pair a Review Routine With an Apply Routine the Next Day, or Approved Changes Sit Until the Next Cycle](automation/pair-a-review-routine-with-a-next-day-apply-routine.md): When a scheduled self-improvement run produces a change list you have to sign off, schedule a second run a day later in apply mode.
- [Pass `--no-session-persistence` to Headless Runs You Don't Want Resumable on Disk](automation/no-session-persistence-for-batch-headless-runs.md): Every `claude -p` writes a resumable session, so a batch loop over 2,000 files leaves 2,000 transcripts holding whatever code and secrets passed through.
- [Point Each Cloud Routine at a Lean Dedicated Repo, Not Your Big AI-OS Repo](automation/dedicated-lean-repo-per-cloud-routine.md): A cloud routine clones a repo and re-reads its CLAUDE.md every run, so aim each one at a small purpose-built repo instead of your giant context repo, which just burns tokens and usage limits.
- [Point Every Routine at a Skill, Never at a Pasted Prompt](automation/point-the-routine-at-a-skill-not-a-pasted-prompt.md): A scheduled routine should invoke a skill by name.
- [Prefer /chrome Over Full Computer-Use Dispatch for Anything Browser-Based](automation/prefer-chrome-over-computer-use-dispatch-for-browser-tasks.md): For web tasks, /chrome is faster and higher-fidelity than dispatching a full computer-use agent to click around the screen; reserve computer-use dispatch for desktop programs that have no browser or API path at all.
- [Raise the Workflow Fan-Out Cap Only When the Bottleneck Is Inference, Not the Machine](automation/raise-workflow-fan-out-with-workflow-max-concurrent-agents.md): the ceiling worth raising and the one that crashes your session are different ceilings.
- [Read the Background Session's Final Report, Because a Draft PR Is No Longer Guaranteed](automation/background-sessions-now-open-a-draft-pr-only-when-the-task-calls-for-one.md): Since 2.1.221 a background session opens a draft PR only when the task calls for one and follows your CLAUDE.md git instructions, so a PR-queue-based review process now has a hole in it.
- [Replace a Puppeteer MCP Server With Claude in Chrome](automation/puppeteer-mcp-replaceable-by-claude-in-chrome.md): If you're using a Puppeteer MCP server for browser automation, consider replacing it with Claude in Chrome now that it's tightly integrated with Claude Code.
- [Require a Git Commit Per Completed Ralph Loop Task for a Reviewable Audit Trail](automation/ralph-loop-require-git-commit-per-task.md): Ralph loop tools don't commit per task by default; explicitly instruct the loop to generate a git commit after each completed task so there's a running, inspectable log of exactly what changed and why.
- [Restrict Unattended Headless Runs to Read-Only Tools as a Guardrail](automation/headless-restrict-to-read-only-tools-as-guardrail.md): For a headless -p run you won't be watching step by step, pass an allowed-tools restriction limited to read-only tools so the run can't take destructive, hard-to-undo actions if something goes wrong.
- [Route a Self-Improvement Loop's Changes Into Auto-Approve / Sign-Off / More-Context Buckets](automation/route-self-improvement-loop-changes-into-three-approval-buckets.md): When a loop edits your own agent system, have it bucket each proposed change into auto-approve (low-risk), needs-sign-off (skill edits/structural changes - written to a checkbox review file with approve/reject/approve-and-don't-ask-again), and more-context-needed, and log every applied change to a changelog.md.
- [Run `claude --init-only` to Fire the Init Hook Without Starting a Session, for CI/CD](automation/claude-init-only-flag-for-ci.md): `claude --init-only` runs the same init hook as `claude --init` but skips actually launching a Claude Code session, which is useful when you just want the setup side effects (e.g. in a CI/CD pipeline) without an interactive agent.
- [Run a Portfolio of Daily Maintenance Routines, and Tune the Routine Rather Than the PR](automation/run-a-portfolio-of-daily-maintenance-routines.md): Crash fuzzer, dup unifier, dead-code remover and abstraction police opened 388 PRs with 180 merged; when one comes back wrong, edit the routine's prompt, not that PR.
- [Run a Standing Background Agent That Submits Small Refactor PRs Continuously](automation/run-a-standing-refactor-agent-to-pay-down-drift.md): Schedule a background agent to scan for convention deviations and open tiny auto-mergeable refactor PRs, paying down drift continuously instead of letting it pile into a big migration.
- [Run True Context-Reset Ralph Loops With ralph-tui or a Bash Script](automation/run-true-ralph-loops-with-ralph-tui-or-a-bash-script.md): For Ralph loops that genuinely spawn a fresh context window per iteration, use a bash script or ralph-tui (bun install -g ralph-tui), which adds a guided create-PRD to task-list flow and a per-run iteration cap you can raise or lower to pull the plug when a loop stalls.
- [Schedule a Routine for the Moment You Will Act on Its Output, Not When the Data Is Ready](automation/schedule-a-routine-for-when-youll-act-on-it-not-when-the-data-is-ready.md): Pick a routine's run time by asking when you will actually read and act on the result.
- [Spike the Whole Automation Chain End to End Before Polishing Any One Step](automation/spike-the-whole-automation-chain-before-polishing-any-step.md): Before building an automation properly, hand-run the ugliest version that touches every step of the chain, to prove no step is impossible.
- [Split Unattended Work as "Crons Watch, Models Act" - Put Detection in launchd/systemd](automation/crons-watch-models-act.md): A working fleet needed ~45 OS-level units whose only job is to notice a condition and wake an agent; the model is for judgment, never for polling.
- [Start an email-handling routine on drafts only, never auto-send](automation/start-an-email-routine-on-drafts-only-never-send.md): Tell the routine to create drafts for replies and not send mail, at least while the automation is new.
- [Stop Hooks Stop Blocking After 8 Consecutive Rejects: Raise the Cap or Add a Real Exit](automation/stop-hook-8-block-cap.md): A Stop hook that keeps blocking is force-overridden after 8 consecutive blocks and the turn ends with a warning; raise `CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP` if you need more, but don't rely on the hook alone to gate completion.
- [Treat `claude --bare` as truly bare now: named MCP servers only, no system reminders, no background tasks](automation/claude-bare-now-skips-config-mcp-servers-and-system-reminders.md): `--bare` runs now drop config-file MCP servers, system reminders and background tasks, and timed-out shell commands are killed instead of backgrounded.
- [Trigger an Agent on Pipeline Failure to Read the Error, Fix, and Redeploy](automation/trigger-an-agent-on-pipeline-failure-to-self-heal.md): Wire a production workflow's failure path to POST the error to a headless Claude session that reads it, fixes the code, and redeploys, unattended self-healing.
- [Trigger an Auto-Reproduce Agent on Every Filed Issue, Then Fix in a Fresh Container](automation/trigger-a-repro-only-agent-on-every-filed-issue.md): Splitting reproduce from fix filters non-bugs before spending fix tokens, and only a successful repro spawns the isolated container that attempts the PR.
- [Turn Any Open-Source Tool Into a Claude-Drivable CLI With CLI Anything](automation/cli-anything-wraps-any-repo-as-an-agent-drivable-cli.md): Point the /cli-anything command at a cloned open-source repo and it inspects the tool's front and back end and generates a CLI wrapper, making non-agent-native software (Excalidraw, a captioner, a Mermaid editor) something Claude Code can operate directly.
- [Use /loop for Recurring In-Session Tasks; Scheduled Tasks for Persistent Automation](automation/loop-command-for-recurring-in-session-tasks.md): /loop fires a prompt on a cron schedule in your current session (max 3 days). For daily/weekly persistence, use scheduled tasks in the desktop app.
- [Use a Slack Thumbs-Up Reaction as an Approval Gate Before an Agent Builds a Suggested Feature](automation/slack-thumbs-up-as-approval-gate.md): When an autonomous agent proposes a feature from monitored feedback, have it wait for a thumbs-up emoji reaction on its Slack message before starting the build, instead of building automatically or requiring a written reply.
- [Use Headless Mode (-p Flag) for Batch Processing and Scheduled Automation](automation/headless-mode-for-batch-automation.md): `claude -p 'your prompt'` runs Claude with no interaction, full permissions assumed, pipe it to cron or Mac scheduler for autonomous workflows.
- [Use Hooks for Automated Formatting, Notifications, and Security Guardrails](automation/hooks-for-automation-and-guardrails.md): PreToolUse, PostToolUse, and Stop hooks run shell commands at key lifecycle points, use them for linting, Slack pings, or skill-scoped permissions.
- [Use PostToolUse `continueOnBlock` to Coach Claude Instead of Killing the Turn](automation/feed-posttooluse-block-back-with-continueonblock.md): Set `continueOnBlock: true` on a PostToolUse hook to feed the hook's rejection reason back to Claude and continue the turn, instead of just hard-blocking, turning a guardrail into a self-correcting nudge.
- [Use the Obsidian CLI to Let Claude Write Into a Vault](automation/obsidian-cli-for-agent-vault-writes.md): Install the Obsidian CLI so Claude Code can write reports or notes directly into an Obsidian vault as part of a skill, instead of you copy-pasting content in manually.
- [Version-Control Your Agent State With a Daily Git Sync](automation/version-control-agent-state-with-a-daily-git-sync.md): Schedule a nightly commit+push of .claude/, skills, and memory files to a private repo, disaster recovery for learned agent state and a way to sync it across machines.
- [Write Hook Matchers as Exact Names or Regexes: They No Longer Substring-Match](automation/hook-matchers-exact-match-not-substring.md): Hook matchers now exact-match the tool name (2.1.195), so `mcp__brave-search` silently stops firing, use a regex like `mcp__brave-search__.*`; re-check comma-separated matchers too.
- [Cap How Long Unattended Retry Mode Waits Out 429 and 529 Errors](automation/cap-unattended-retry-waits-with-claude-code-retry-watchdog-max-wait-ms.md): With `CLAUDE_CODE_RETRY_WATCHDOG` on, unattended runs wait out rate-limit and overload errors with no upper bound; since 2.1.295 `CLAUDE_CODE_RETRY_WATCHDOG_MAX_WAIT_MS` sets one.
- [Trigger a Claude Routine From an API Call or a GitHub Event Instead of a Timer](automation/trigger-a-claude-routine-from-a-web-request-or-github-event.md): A cloud routine is not limited to a schedule: it can start from a web request (you get a URL and token) or a GitHub event such as a new pull request, so any tool that can send a request can wake an agent.
- [Cap How Long Unattended Retry Mode Waits for a Usage Limit With CLAUDE_CODE_RETRY_WATCHDOG_MAX_USAGE_LIMIT_WAIT_MS](automation/cap-the-usage-limit-wait-in-unattended-retry-mode.md): With `CLAUDE_CODE_RETRY_WATCHDOG` on, this variable ends a request at a usage limit that resets too far away instead of waiting for it.
- [Give Each Critical Component Its Own Agent That Watches Only That Component's Telemetry and Logs](automation/give-each-critical-component-a-superfan-agent-watching-its-telemetry.md): Alongside normal alerts, Anthropic runs one Claude per component, each in its own channel with access to that component's telemetry and logs, briefed to look for anything strange.
- [Set CLAUDE_CODE_RETRY_WATCHDOG_USAGE_LIMIT_RECHECK_MS So Unattended Retry Mode Notices a Lifted Usage Limit](automation/set-the-retry-watchdog-usage-limit-recheck-for-unattended-runs.md): With unattended retry mode (`CLAUDE_CODE_RETRY_WATCHDOG`) on, a run waiting out a usage limit can now re-check on an interval and resume when the limit lifts before its stated reset.
- [Pass --trust-workspace When You Script claude ultrareview](automation/script-claude-ultrareview-with-trust-workspace.md): Since 2.1.309 `claude ultrareview` needs workspace trust: it asks on a terminal and refuses otherwise, so a script or CI job must pass `--trust-workspace` (after the PR number or base branch) or set `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ULTRAREVIEW_TRUST_CHECK=1`.

### Remote Access

- [Build Your Own Slack Bot Harness When Claude Tag Can't Hold the Tools Your Lifecycle Needs](remote-access/build-your-own-slack-harness-when-claude-tag-cannot-hold-your-tools.md): A self-hosted Slack bot running Claude Code and Codex on a cloud box can install the extra CLIs and second provider the hosted integration can't, so a two-hour lifecycle runs with your laptop closed.
- [Enable Remote Control at User Scope: Repo-Local Settings Can Only Turn It Off Now](remote-access/remote-control-can-no-longer-be-enabled-from-repo-settings.md): A cloned repo or installed plugin can no longer flip on remote control of your session by shipping a settings file.
- [Pick Discord Over Telegram for Remote Claude Code if You Need Conversation Memory](remote-access/discord-vs-telegram-channel-persistence-and-accept-edits-default.md): Remote Claude Code channels differ by platform: Telegram has no history API (no memory across messages) and a 50MB attachment cap, while Discord can fetch the last 100 messages for persistence; both default to accept-edits mode (not bypass permissions), so file sends and actions get blocked with permission prompts until you explicitly loosen them.
- [Prefer Remote Control Over Discord/Telegram Channels Unless You Specifically Need Images](remote-access/prefer-remote-control-over-channels-unless-you-need-images.md): Claude Code's Channels feature (Discord/Telegram messaging) is experimental and adds setup complexity mainly justified by one benefit over Remote Control: the ability to receive images back in the reply.
- [Routing to a Non-Anthropic Base URL Disables Remote Control](remote-access/remote-control-off-with-non-anthropic-base-url.md): Pointing ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL at OpenRouter/Ollama/a proxy switches Remote Control off (2.1.196), reserve cheaper/local routing for terminal-only sessions, keep the Anthropic endpoint for ones you drive from your phone.
- [Run Claude Code on a VPS in tmux With a Telegram Bridge for Always-On Access](remote-access/vps-tmux-telegram-persistent-sessions.md): A cheap VPS + tmux keeps the session alive 24/7; the Telegram plugin (paired then locked) lets you dispatch tasks from your phone.
- [Set disableRemoteControl:true, Don't Trust the /rc Toggle, After a Silent Server-Side Default Flip](remote-access/set-disable-remote-control-rather-than-trusting-the-rc-toggle.md): Remote Control silently turned itself on for existing sessions in August via a server-pushed GrowthBook-style flag combination (remote_control_auto_enable, remote_control_auto_on_by_default, ide_rc_auto_enable_gate), independent of your local /rc setting; the durable fix is the disableRemoteControl:true settings.json key, not the in-app toggle.
- [Update Any Idle-Detection Script That Keyed Off "Waiting for Your Input"](remote-access/background-agents-no-longer-report-sessions-as-waiting-for-input.md): the string changes meaning on upgrade, and the failure is quiet.
- [Use /remote-env to Manage Remote Environments, and a Trailing & to Route a Prompt to the Default One](remote-access/remote-env-command-and-ampersand-suffix-routes-to-remote.md): /remote-env lists and lets you create the same remote environments visible in the Claude Code desktop app or cloud, including environment variables; marking one as default and suffixing a prompt with & sends that prompt to run in the remote environment instead of locally.
- [Use `/teleport` to Move a Running Session Between Cloud/Web and Your Terminal](remote-access/teleport-sessions-between-cloud-web-and-terminal.md): Run `claude --teleport` or `/teleport` to continue an already-running cloud/web session on your local machine (and push the other way), start work on your phone in the morning and pick it up at your desk.
- [Use Cloud Managed Agents for Webhook/Cron-Triggered Automation With No Local Session](remote-access/managed-agents-for-webhook-triggered-automation.md): Anthropic's managed (cloud-hosted) agents run with MCP servers, credential vaults, and webhook/cron triggers, use them for event-driven automation that can't depend on your laptop being open.
- [Use Dispatch's One Persistent Thread to Auto-Route Tasks to Claude Code or Claude Co-work by Type](remote-access/dispatch-routes-tasks-by-type-desktop-must-stay-open.md): Dispatch gives you a single ongoing conversation thread from your phone; describe a task and it decides whether it's a coding job (routed to Claude Code) or knowledge work (routed to Claude Co-work), running each independently and pushing a notification when done, but your desktop app must stay open the whole time or the session is lost.
- [Use Remote Control or Channels to Send Tasks from Phone](remote-access/remote-control-channels-for-phone-access.md): `claude rc` gives browser/phone access to your running session; Channels connects Telegram/iMessage/Discord as two-way interfaces.
- [Use the Slack Integration to Delegate Tasks From Team Channels](remote-access/slack-integration-for-team-task-delegation.md): @Claude in a Slack channel delegates tasks to Claude Code; connect Sentry or logging MCPs to let it respond to production events automatically.
- [Start a Cloud Session From the Terminal With claude --cloud, Then Pull It Back With --teleport](remote-access/start-a-cloud-session-from-the-terminal-with-claude-cloud-flag.md): A task can run on a cloud machine instead of yours: start it with `claude --cloud`, close the laptop, and bring the conversation back later with `claude --teleport`.
- [Use /remote-control Together With a Keep-Awake Setting So Long Local Builds Continue While You Are Away](remote-access/pair-remote-control-with-keeping-the-computer-awake-for-long-builds.md): Remote control lets your phone drive local sessions, but the laptop must not sleep.

### Commands

- [/rename Also Retitles the Terminal Tab and Input Bar, Not Just the Session List](commands/rename-command-syncs-session-tab-and-input-bar-name.md): Running /rename doesn't just label the conversation in /resume; it also renames the terminal tab (e.g. in Warp) and the input bar, which helps when juggling many sessions side by side.
- [`/commit-push-pr` No Longer Auto-Approves `--force`, `--amend` or `--no-verify`](commands/commit-push-pr-no-longer-auto-approves-dangerous-flags.md): It now prompts, so an unattended loop ending in that command hangs on a permission prompt instead of completing, with no obvious signal under `-p`.
- [`/fork` Now Spawns a Background Session: the In-Session Forked Subagent Is `/subtask`](commands/fork-is-now-a-background-session-subtask-is-the-in-session-fork.md): Typing `/fork` from muscle memory now gets a detached session in `claude agents`; the naming moved twice between June and July, so verify with `/help` before hardcoding it.
- [Build a Custom Slash Command That Asks Clarifying Questions First](commands/custom-clarifying-questions-slash-command.md): Package the "ask before you build" habit into a reusable /command that captures the task, asks ~5 questions, plans, and waits for approval.
- [ctrl+enter / ctrl+x ctrl+s Now Backgrounds Running Tools Instead of Cancelling the Turn](commands/send-now-backgrounds-running-tools.md): The "send now" key combo (ctrl+enter, or ctrl+x ctrl+s), added in 2.1.275 to interrupt the current turn and send all queued messages at once, changed behavior in 2.1.281: it now backgrounds whatever tools are still running instead of killing the current turn outright.
- [Cycle Opus Effort (Low/Medium/High) With the Arrow Keys Mid-Session](commands/claude-code-effort-arrow-key-toggle.md): Claude Code lets you switch Opus between low, medium, and high effort using the arrow keys, useful when the model is overthinking a simple task.
- [Default to `/code-review low` - It Spawns Zero Agents and Costs Under a Cent](commands/default-to-code-review-low-it-spawns-no-agents.md): Low effort uses no subagents at all, higher levels use up to 10: a 10x fan-out difference hidden behind one word, and Anthropic's evals put low ahead of other models' reviews.
- [Exit Claude Code by Pressing Ctrl+C Twice](commands/exit-claude-code-with-ctrl-c-twice.md): Ctrl+C twice quits Claude Code from the terminal; a single Ctrl+C does not exit.
- [Hit Save, Then Hand Claude Code the Artboard's Name to Start the Build](commands/save-the-artboard-and-hand-claude-code-its-name.md): Each /design artboard carries a name at the top of the canvas.
- [Invoke `/verify` and `/deep-research` Yourself: Claude Does Not Start Them on Its Own](commands/invoke-verify-and-code-review-manually-they-no-longer-auto-run.md): `/verify` (since 2.1.215) and `/deep-research` (since 2.1.218) start only when you invoke them, so a workflow that relied on Claude reaching for them unprompted silently skips verification. `/code-review` went manual in 2.1.215 too, but since 2.1.246 Claude can start it on its own again.
- [Pre-compute context with inline bash inside slash commands](commands/inline-bash-in-slash-commands.md): Embed bash in a slash command to pre-compute state (e.g. git status) so the model gets the context for free instead of round-tripping for it. (Boris Cherny).
- [Press Ctrl+B to Move Every Running Bash Command and Subagent to Background at Once](commands/ctrl-b-backgrounds-all-running-bash-and-subagents-at-once.md): If you have multiple foreground bash commands or subagents running simultaneously (e.g. five parallel explore subagents), Ctrl+B backgrounds all of them in one keystroke so you can keep chatting with Claude Code.
- [Press Ctrl+T to View the Agent Team's Current Task List](commands/ctrl-t-shows-agent-team-task-list.md): Ctrl+T pops up the list of tasks the agent-team orchestrator has assigned across teammates, letting you see what's in flight without switching panes.
- [Press Escape Twice to Open the Checkpoint/Rewind Menu](commands/double-tap-escape-opens-rewind-checkpoint-menu.md): Double-tapping Escape in Claude Code's terminal UI brings up a list of recent prompts/checkpoints you can rewind to, without typing a slash command.
- [Press Shift+Tab to Cycle Plan Mode and Accept-Edits Mode](commands/shift-tab-toggles-plan-and-accept-edits-mode.md): Shift+Tab switches Claude Code between plan mode and accept-edits mode without typing a command.
- [Run /checkup (aka /doctor) to Audit Setup Health and Kill Context Bloat](commands/run-checkup-doctor-to-audit-setup-health-and-context-bloat.md): /checkup audits MCP servers, agents, skills, plugins, and CLAUDE.md against a battery of checks and quantifies how much context budget each extension eats, with clickable fixes.
- [Run /doctor prompt-audit on Your Own CLAUDE.md, Skills, Agents and Commands After a Model Upgrade](commands/run-doctor-prompt-audit-on-your-own-config-after-a-model-upgrade.md): `/doctor prompt-audit` (also `/checkup prompt-audit`) audits your CLAUDE.md files, skills, agents and commands for prompting patterns written for older models, and its report leads with stale paths, stale commands and contradicting instruction files.
- [Run /keybindings to List and Customize Claude Code's Key Bindings](commands/claude-code-keybindings-command.md): Claude Code has a /keybindings command that shows the current key bindings and lets you change them, particularly relevant in chord-capable terminals like Ghostty.
- [Run /memory to Inspect Claude Code's Automemory Structure](commands/slash-memory-command-inspects-automemory-structure.md): The /memory command shows imported, project, and user memory, and lets you auto-open the automemory folder to browse the index and its linked files.
- [Run /mobile to Get a QR Code for the Mobile App](commands/mobile-command-shows-qr-code.md): Run /mobile in Claude Code to display a QR code that links straight to the iOS or Android app download.
- [Run /powerup, the Built-In 10-Lesson Interactive Tutorial, to Onboard Beginners](commands/powerup-built-in-interactive-tutorial-for-beginners.md): /powerup launches a built-in 10-lesson interactive ASCII tutorial inside Claude Code; run new users through it to get them to roughly 80% before pointing them at advanced tooling like /insights.
- [Search Every Prompt You Have Written With Ctrl+R, Then Widen the Scope](commands/search-past-prompts-with-ctrl-r-then-widen-the-scope.md): Ctrl+R opens a search over the prompts in the current session.
- [Set 'Left Option Key Is Meta' So Alt+T Toggles Thinking Mid-Session](commands/set-option-as-meta-so-alt-t-toggles-thinking.md): In your terminal preferences, set the left Option key to act as Meta; Alt+T then toggles Claude Code's thinking mode mid-session, no restart or re-prompt needed.
- [Set a 100-Line Threshold in CLAUDE.md to Auto-Pick /code-review's Effort Level](commands/code-review-line-count-effort-threshold.md): Write a CLAUDE.md rule that runs /code-review at medium effort for changes under 100 lines and high effort for anything bigger, so effort selection stops being a manual guess.
- [Slash Commands and Skills Are Now the Same Mechanism Internally](commands/slash-commands-and-skills-merged-internally.md): Your commands folder still works exactly as before, but Claude Code now treats slash commands and skills as the same underlying construct behind the scenes rather than two separate systems.
- [Stop Hand-Isolating Forked Sessions: /fork Now Creates Its Own Worktree](commands/fork-now-gets-its-own-worktree.md): Before 2.1.221 a forked background session could edit the same checkout your foreground session was using.
- [Switch Execution Environment (Local / Cloud / SSH) to Match the Workload](commands/environment-switching-local-cloud-ssh.md): The environment selector picks where work runs: local for interactive speed, cloud for routines that outlive your laptop, SSH for a specific remote box.
- [Type the Effort Level Explicitly, Because Bare /code-review Reuses Your Last One](commands/code-review-reuses-your-last-effort-level.md): `/review` is now an alias of `/code-review`, and a bare invocation silently repeats your last level, so one `ultra` pins cloud reviews until you change it.
- [Type Your Next Prompt While Claude Is Still Working: It Queues](commands/queue-follow-up-prompts-while-claude-works.md): You don't have to wait for Claude to finish before typing the next instruction; Claude Code queues and sequences multiple entered prompts logically.
- [Use /design to Iterate UI as Editable Artboards Before Asking Claude to Implement One](commands/iterate-ui-as-design-artboards-before-implementing.md): The /design research preview generates variant artboards on artifacts; pick and tweak one, then implement from an approved design instead of tweak-the-implementation loops.
- [Use /export to Save the Whole Session as a File to Hand to a Client](commands/export-command-saves-full-session-to-a-file-for-clients.md): When you want to share an entire diagnostic session rather than a single response, /export saves it to a file you can send directly, so the recipient can follow the full reasoning trail.
- [Use /plan to Toggle Planning Mode, and Run It Again to Recall the Existing Plan](commands/slash-plan-toggles-and-recalls-the-current-plan.md): /plan turns Planning Mode on as an alternative to the Shift+Tab-twice shortcut; if you already have a plan built in the session, running /plan again redisplays that same plan instead of starting a new one.
- [Use `claude -w <name>` to Launch a Session in Its Own Built-In Worktree](commands/claude-w-flag-for-built-in-worktree-scoped-sessions.md): The `-w` flag (e.g. `claude -w feature-1`) starts a session scoped to its own git worktree, useful specifically when two features being built in parallel touch overlapping parts of the codebase.
- [Use Arrow Keys, Not Tab, to Switch Between Tabs in Multi-Tab Command Output](commands/use-arrow-keys-not-tab-for-tabbed-command-output.md): Commands with tabbed output (like /stats) are now navigated with the left/right arrow keys instead of repeatedly pressing Tab to cycle through them.
- [Use the /v Voice Command, Hold Spacebar to Speak, Works in 20+ Languages](commands/voice-mode-v-command-hold-spacebar-20-languages.md): Claude Code's built-in voice mode is invoked with /v, you hold the spacebar while speaking your instruction, and it now supports more than 20 languages.
- [When an Agent Hits Friction, Build a CLI Tool: Not an MCP](commands/cli-tools-over-mcp-for-tokens.md): Prefer giving agents small CLI tools over MCPs: a CLI is read once then driven by command, is deterministic, self-documents via `--help`, and costs far less context than an MCP's loaded tool schemas.
- [Pass /code-review --max-findings all for a Full Sweep, Then Reset It With --max-findings default](commands/code-review-max-findings-is-sticky-reset-it-with-default.md): `/code-review --max-findings <n>|all` raises or lowers the findings cap, and the choice carries over to every later review until you pass `--max-findings default`.
- [Expect /code-review at Medium on Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 to Also Flag Cleanup and CLAUDE.md Convention Findings](commands/code-review-medium-now-reports-cleanup-and-claude-md-convention-findings.md): Since 2.1.290, a medium-effort `/code-review` on models without tuned review settings (including Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5) reports cleanup and CLAUDE.md-convention findings, not only correctness bugs.

### Gotchas

- ["Explain Your Reasoning" Phrasing Can Silently Reroute You to a Weaker Model](gotchas/reasoning-extraction-phrasing-can-silently-reroute-model.md): A "show your reasoning" line (especially in a system prompt) can trip a safety check that silently hands the request to a weaker fallback model, invisible in the app.
- ["Upstream idle timeout exceeded" on free OpenRouter models: tell Claude to work in smaller chunks](gotchas/upstream-idle-timeout-on-free-models-work-in-smaller-chunks.md): The error reads as fatal but is one oversized step against a congested free endpoint.
- [A Memory Add-On Keeps Its Own Copy, So Revoking Source Access Later Removes Nothing](gotchas/shared-memory-retains-content-after-source-access-revoked.md): Once memory ingests a permissioned doc it is a second un-revocable copy, and the workspace admin can typically read every user's personal memories too.
- [A Newly Added MCP Connector Only Reaches Conversations Started After It, Re-Paste Your Prompt Into a Fresh One](gotchas/new-mcp-connector-only-applies-to-conversations-started-after-it.md): Adding an MCP connector while a conversation is already open does nothing for that conversation.
- [A Peer Session Can Be Invisible for Two Boring Reasons: Remote Control Off and Version Skew](gotchas/peer-session-invisible-check-remote-control-and-version-skew.md): Both failures are silent, and the version-skew one selects against exactly the long-running sessions you most want to reach.
- [A Skill Can Call a Subagent, But a Subagent Rarely Calls a Skill Back](gotchas/skills-call-subagents-easily-the-reverse-rarely-works.md): In practice a skill can chain into a subagent (e.g., a release-notes skill calling a release-notes-generator subagent), but the reverse direction is unreliable because the subagent's own system prompt doesn't mention that skills exist.
- [After a Long Autonomous Run, Check /usage's Per-Model Breakdown, the Orchestrator May Not Be the Model You Started On](gotchas/check-the-per-model-usage-breakdown-after-a-long-autonomous-run.md): A multi-hour /goal run started on Fable 5 came back showing Opus 4.8 as the main orchestrator spawning Fable 5 subagents, so read the per-model split in /usage rather than assuming the session stayed on your chosen model.
- [Agent-Team Split-Pane View Reliably Works Only in tmux, Not iTerm2](gotchas/agent-team-split-pane-reliably-works-only-in-tmux.md): Despite iTerm2 being documented as a supported terminal for the agent-team split-pane display mode, only tmux was actually confirmed working.
- [Ask Claude to Pull Stock Images From a Royalty-Free Library Like Pixabay](gotchas/source-royalty-free-images-via-pixabay-not-arbitrary-web.md): When Claude fills placeholder images on a generated site, direct it to pull from a named royalty-free source such as Pixabay rather than arbitrary web images, to avoid copyright cease-and-desist risk.
- [Ask the Orchestrator to Remove an Unneeded Agent-Team Teammate, Don't Tear It Down Yourself](gotchas/remove-agent-team-teammate-via-orchestrator-not-directly.md): If a teammate turns out unnecessary, tell the lead/orchestrator to clean it up rather than killing it directly, since the docs warn manual teardown outside the orchestrator's control can leave the team in a weird state.
- [Auto Mode Tells Claude to Edit With sed and Heredocs Instead of Edit/Write: Counter It in Settings](gotchas/auto-mode-tells-claude-to-edit-with-sed-and-heredocs-instead.md): Auto mode injects a harness instruction that makes Claude read with cat/sed and edit with sed/heredocs instead of Read/Edit/Write, which busts Read-tool caching and has caused real data loss.
- [Beat /goal's ~4,000-Char Limit by Putting the Full Instruction Set in a File](gotchas/goal-prompt-4k-char-limit-put-instructions-in-a-file.md): /goal silently caps around 4,000 characters, so for a long autonomous mission put the whole spec (mission, guardrails, phase arc, deliverables, definition-of-done, never-ask rule) in a file and make the /goal prompt just 'read this file and execute everything below the divider line as your goal.'.
- [Blank Thinking Summaries? Check ~/.claude.json for a Silent Server-Side Experiment Assignment](gotchas/blank-thinking-summaries-check-claude-json-for-a-silent-server.md): Server-side experiment assignments cached in ~/.claude.json (clientDataCacheSlots / experimentKey) can silently blank thinking summaries.
- [Changing a Teammate's Model Mid-Run Restarts the Entire Agent Team](gotchas/teammate-model-change-restarts-whole-agent-team.md): Asking to change which model a specific teammate runs on doesn't hot-swap just that teammate; it typically shuts down all current teammates and recreates the team.
- [Check the Record Feature Captured Your Voice, Not Just the Screen](gotchas/check-whether-the-record-feature-captured-your-voice-not-just-the-screen.md): Before relying on a screen-record-to-skill run, confirm what it actually captured: some recorders take the screen without your audio, which loses the narration carrying all of your reasoning.
- [Check What the Agent Can Actually Edit Before Promising a Fix, Or the Deliverable Is Only a Report](gotchas/check-the-write-path-before-promising-an-agent-fix.md): Claude can only fix what it has a write path to.
- [CLAUDE.md edits don't apply mid-session: restart, /clear, or compact to load them](gotchas/claude-md-edits-are-invisible-until-restart-clear-or-compact.md): The file is read once at session start, so a mid-session rule edit is silently ignored however you rephrase it.
- [Code Work can't see .claude/skills: re-add skills via Customize and restart the session](gotchas/code-work-needs-its-own-copy-of-your-terminal-skills.md): Terminal-built skills are invisible there, and a skill added mid-session doesn't load until restart.
- [Compare a Redesign/Polish Skill's Output Against the Original Before Accepting It](gotchas/compare-redesign-skill-output-before-trusting-it.md): Running a 'redesign' or 'de-AI-slop' skill against a Claude Design export is not guaranteed to be an upgrade, in one head-to-head the original Claude Design output was preferred over the redesign skill's version (which swapped in stock images and changed the logo), while a different sub-skill (brutalist) won.
- [Denying a Subagent's Permission Request No Longer Stops It, It Tries a Different Approach](gotchas/denying-a-subagent-permission-no-longer-kills-it.md): When a subagent asks for a tool permission and you press no, it used to stop completely; now it continues working and tries an alternative approach instead of dead-ending.
- [Disable the 'Workflow keyword trigger' So the Word 'workflow' Stops Launching Agents](gotchas/disable-the-workflow-keyword-trigger.md): Saying 'workflow' in a prompt was spuriously launching the dynamic Workflow tool and burning quota; a /config 'Workflow keyword trigger' setting disables just the keyword while keeping the feature.
- [Do Not Quote a Cost Figure the Agent States in Prose: Read It Off /usage](gotchas/cross-check-a-cost-figure-the-agent-states-against-usage.md): In one multi-hour run the agent's own summary put the spend near 447 dollars while the session's /usage stats across the session and its subagents came to closer to 800.
- [Don't Render Multiple Agent-Driven Videos at Once on the Same Machine](gotchas/avoid-concurrent-video-renders-on-one-machine.md): Rendering several HyperFrames/video-editing sessions in parallel saturates CPU and RAM, which can visibly degrade anything else running at the same time, including a live screen recording.
- [Don't Route Judgment Calls Through `/advisor` Mid-Session: Any Prior Tool Call Breaks It on Fable 5](gotchas/advisor-fails-after-any-prior-tool-use-not-above-a-token-count.md): The trigger is any prior `tool_use` block, not a token threshold, so the advisor works as a session's first tool call and never again, use an `Agent(model="fable")` subagent instead.
- [Escape With Queued Prompts Now Only Drops the Last Queued Message, It No Longer Cancels the Running Task](gotchas/escape-no-longer-cancels-a-running-task-when-unqueuing.md): Pressing Escape while you have queued messages used to both remove the last queued message and cancel whatever task was currently running; now it only removes the queued message and leaves the running task alone (unless that queued prompt had already entered the task).
- [Expect Skill Activation to Max Out Around 84% Even With a Well-Tuned Description](gotchas/skill-activation-rate-20-to-84-percent.md): An informal test found skills with a poor description activate as low as 20% of the time they should, and even with an optimized description plus supporting hooks, the measured ceiling was around 84%, not 100%.
- [Fast mode bills API credits, sticks across sessions, and re-pays your whole context uncached](gotchas/fast-mode-bills-api-credits-and-reprices-your-context-uncached.md): One mid-conversation toggle reprices the entire accumulated cache at the uncached fast-mode rate, and it stays on until you type "fast off".
- [If /dream Doesn't Trigger Memory Consolidation, Just Ask in Plain English](gotchas/dream-command-not-working-workaround.md): The dedicated /dream (or similar) command for manually triggering auto-dream's memory consolidation is reported as unreliable; the workaround is to tell Claude directly, 'consolidate my memory files,' which runs the same cleanup process.
- [If an Ultracode Run Looks Like Plain Extra Effort, Check That Dynamic Workflows Are Actually Available](gotchas/check-ultracode-is-actually-in-effect-before-trusting-an-ultracode-run.md): Ultracode only does anything when dynamic workflows are enabled and the model supports it. A run that never spawns a workflow may simply not have had ultracode in effect.
- [If Every Sandboxed Git Command Fails, Check extensions.worktreeConfig](gotchas/if-every-sandboxed-git-command-fails-check-extensions.md): On Linux, a repo with `extensions.worktreeConfig` set had every sandboxed git command fail before 2.1.239, because the sandbox made the not-yet-created `.git/config.worktree` unreadable.
- [If HyperFrames' Local Preview Shows '0 of 0 Seconds', Skip It and Ask for a Full Render Instead](gotchas/hyperframes-broken-preview-skip-to-full-render.md): HyperFrames' HTML-based localhost preview sometimes fails to load a composition's duration and just shows 0 seconds of 0 seconds; rather than debugging the preview, tell Claude Code to render the full video and review that instead.
- [In Auto Mode, /rewind Silently Leaves Bash-Written Edits on Disk](gotchas/rewind-leaves-bash-written-edits-on-disk-in-auto-mode.md): sed/heredoc/python edits never reach the checkpoint system, so /rewind reports success while the files keep every change; trust git diff, not the rewind confirmation.
- [IS_DEMO=1 Hides Your Email in the UI, Not in the System Prompt](gotchas/is-demo-masks-the-email-on-screen-not-in-the-system-prompt.md): IS_DEMO=1 masks your account email and org in the header and in outputs, but it does not remove the `# userEmail` block from the system prompt, so a session recorded with it still leaks the address to anyone who gets the model to repeat its system prompt.
- [Know That Sharing a Feedback-Survey Transcript Now Uploads Your CLAUDE.md Too](gotchas/feedback-survey-share-now-uploads-your-claude-md.md): The share now carries the system prompt, tool definitions, and model parameters; secrets are redacted but proprietary process written into CLAUDE.md is not.
- [Know the Official Ralph/Loop Plugin Doesn't Reset Context Each Iteration](gotchas/official-ralph-plugin-does-not-reset-context.md): It isn't a true Ralph loop without a fresh context window per pass; use a bash script or ralph-tui so context doesn't accumulate across iterations.
- [Never Let the Agent Run Its Own Script From Inside an Unpacked Untrusted Archive](gotchas/never-run-agent-written-scripts-from-inside-an-untrusted-archive.md): The working Opus 5 exploit was Claude's own decoder importing a struct.py the archive supplied; run agent-written scripts from a clean cwd and invoke Python with -I.
- [Never Wire Your Claude Max Account Into Third-Party Agent Runners](gotchas/dont-wire-max-account-into-third-party-agent-runners.md): Routing a Max subscription into external always-on agent tools (e.g. OpenClaw) can get the account banned; use a separately-issued API key instead.
- [Prefer a Hook-Based Memory Auto-Inject Over an MCP Memory Tool the Model Must Choose to Call](gotchas/hook-based-memory-injection-beats-mcp-tool-call-for-reliability.md): mem-search auto-injects your top semantic memory matches into every prompt via a hook, while MCP-based memory tools like claude-mem require the model to actively decide to call a search tool, so a memory that matters can simply not get retrieved.
- [Reserve --dangerously-skip-permissions for Disposable Environments](gotchas/dangerously-skip-permissions-can-damage-your-os.md): Running Claude Code with `--dangerously-skip-permissions` removes the accept-prompts safety net entirely; used aggressively it can damage your actual machine.
- [Restate Command Output in Your Reply, Because the Bash Tool Block Is Not Reliably Shown](gotchas/restate-bash-output-in-text-it-is-not-reliably-shown.md): Anthropic now tells the model outright that Bash output reaches it but not dependably the user, so an agent that runs the tests and stops has reported nothing.
- [Set an explicit timeout on background shell commands in unattended sessions, they stop after 30 minutes by default](gotchas/background-shell-commands-now-stop-after-30-minutes-set-a-timeout.md): In `-p`, Agent SDK, CI and cloud sessions, a long build, server or watch started with `run_in_background` is killed at 30 minutes unless the call sets a longer `timeout`, capped at 2 hours. Interactive sessions have no limit since 2.1.288.
- [Set Up Env Vars and MCP Servers From the Terminal, Not the Desktop App](gotchas/desktop-app-env-and-mcp-setup-is-harder-than-terminal-files.md): Configuring environment variables and MCP servers in the Claude Code desktop app is more cumbersome than editing the plain .env and settings.json files you'd use in the terminal.
- [Shift+Enter for a New Line Now Works in Ghostty and iTerm Without Extra Config](gotchas/shift-enter-newline-works-in-ghostty-and-iterm-with-no-config.md): Terminals like Ghostty and iTerm now support Shift+Enter to insert a new line in the prompt box out of the box, where before you had to change terminal configuration to make that keybinding pass through.
- [Spot a Spawned Agent-Team Teammate by Its Colored Terminal Text](gotchas/agent-team-teammate-spawn-shown-by-colored-text.md): When Claude Code spawns a sub-agent or agent-team teammate, its output renders in a distinct color in the terminal, which is the quickest way to tell a delegation actually happened.
- [Stop Restarting Claude Code After Editing a Skill: Mid-Session Changes Now Reach the Slash Menu](gotchas/skills-edited-mid-session-now-appear-without-restart.md): Fixed in 2.1.216; the restart-after-install ritual cost a full context reset on the most common skill-authoring loop, and the habit outlives the bug.
- [Strip the UTF-8 BOM From Agent, Skill, and Command Files: a BOM Silently Disables Them](gotchas/strip-the-utf-8-bom-from-agent-skill-and-command-files-a-bom.md): Until 2.1.239, an agent/skill/command .md file starting with a UTF-8 BOM was ignored with no error at all.
